**KDD-080 (2026-07-28). ★ W2.6 DISBAND — RETIREMENT SUFFICES. Decided Option C (don't build) on a STRUCTURAL argument, not a schedule: under fail-safe, failure is only ever visible as ABSENCE, and absence is what W2.4 retirement already handles. Fork-B PERMANENTLY deferred against KDD-077 §3. + ★ THE §3(b) PRECONDITION — a threat-model sharpening.**

### §1 — The question, and the frame

W2.6's front question was never "how to build disband" but **"what is disband allowed to act on, given the protocol cannot adjudicate intent"** (KDD-077 §3, promoted to load-bearing partly for this case). §3's three permissible responses are the frame: **(a)** act on a chain-evident observation, **(b)** apply a symmetric penalty, **(c)** don't act. The recon evaluated disband against all three, from source, at HEAD `92064d3`.

### §2 — ★ THE STRUCTURAL SQUEEZE: the observable failure space is {absence, unprovable} — there is no third region for disband to live in

**KDD-077 §4's fail-safe posture plus consensus validation empty the space a chain-evident disband would need:** a broken quorum **cannot emit wrong output** (sub-threshold close aborts, divergent premit aborts, the coordinator can at most fail its own roll — §4's enumeration); a **provably-invalid artifact never persists** (the W4-b attribution verify and the V-sequence reject pre-connect), so on chain *"produced garbage" is indistinguishable from "produced nothing"* — both are absence; and a **valid-but-biased** output (≥t compromised) is unprovable by construction — §3(c) territory, accepted at §9.1, bounded by rotation cadence (KDD-045, enforced fairly by KDD-079 Guard 2). ★ **Every failure is therefore either caught as absence or unprovable — no failure is present-but-misbehaving AND chain-evident AND uncaught.**

### §3 — The trigger decomposition: KDD-047 §7.3's "< t available" lands on shipped machinery

"Available" is an off-chain fact; the trigger is observable only through its consequences, and both routes shipped: **registry-evident loss** → **KDD-076 forced reform** (fires at ONE unresolvable member — strictly earlier than inquorate's six); **registered-but-dead** → sub-threshold cannot threshold-sign → **silence** → **KDD-074 idle retirement** (rotation-as-heartbeat, as the KDD-071 placeholder anticipated). Both dissolve to the pool with a fresh random re-draw — §7.3's disposition, under different producers. The ~30-block exit latency §7.3 wanted is a **tuning question on `nRetireWindow`** (see the §7.3 amendment; §7.3's own cost-asymmetry argues against urgency, and KDD-053 re-route + ODC-047's K-floor bound the wait's cost).

### §4 — ★★ THE §3(b) PRECONDITION (the new finding — a general threat-model refinement, not a disband detail)

**§3(b)'s symmetric penalty has an implicit precondition: a PROVABLE RESOLUTION STEP to symmetrize against.** The false-accuser rule works in-ceremony because the accused *can cryptographically justify* — the reveal resolves the complaint provably, so penalizing whichever side the resolution goes against never adjudicates intent. A steady-state failure report ("quorum X failed my roll") has **no justification counterpart** — *"no proof of a non-signature"* cuts **both ways**: the quorum's failure cannot be proven, and neither can the reporter's lie, so the symmetric penalty cannot be grounded on either side. ★ **Without a provable resolution, §3(b) collapses to §3(c).** This sharpens KDD-077 §3: **the three permissible responses are not always three** — (b) is conditional on a provable resolution existing, and any future mechanism reaching for it must first exhibit one.

### §5 — Why A and B fail, and C follows

**Option A (chain-evident disband):** the residue A would trigger on is **empty** (§2/§3) — A collapses into *already built*, leaving only the latency tuning. **Option B (symmetric-penalty disband):** fails §4's precondition — it is fork-B under another name, and fork-B's deferral is *principled* (§3), not scheduled. **Option C follows:** no separate disband mechanism is built; **retirement IS the involuntary-exit path**. ★ The deferral will not expire with time: it lifts only if the §3 limit itself is addressed, or a future consensus artifact makes some present-but-misbehaving failure *provable* (which would re-open A with a real trigger — the recorded re-open condition).

### §6 — The swept loose ends (closed by this decision)

- **(i) `DISBANDED = 3` stays reserved-unused PERMANENTLY** (persisted-bytes stability — never renumber). Its envisioned blame-carrying semantics (the "failed" terminal, distinct member consequences from blameless REFORMED) have **no §3-valid trigger at quorum level**; member-level blame is **PoSe §8's** — whose 15-of-60 signal is chain-evident *per-member* absence, the §3(a) pattern at the right granularity.
- **(ii) Top-up is SUBSUMED**: adding a member to a live DKG quorum requires re-keying regardless (the share arithmetic admits no incremental join), and re-keying with a changed set IS a fresh formation — the shipped fresh-draw reform is the answer. The roadmap's "disband / top-up" line resolves to reform on both halves.
- **(iii) The KDD-071 placeholder RESOLVES**: rotation-as-heartbeat + idle retirement are the share-liveness signal; no separate mechanism is owed (the roadmap's "may never be needed separately" confirmed).
- **(iv) ODC-034 CLOSED** (see its amendment): shipped reform is the pool-release path it demanded — owed since the W2.4 landing, swept here.

**Cross-ref:** KDD-077 §3/§4 (the frame and the squeeze; §3 sharpened by §4 above), KDD-047 §7.3 (+ its decomposition amendment, same date), KDD-074/075/076 (the machinery that subsumes the trigger), ODC-036 (the threshold that makes sub-threshold silent), KDD-053 + ODC-047 (why the latency is cheap), KDD-045/079 (the rotation bound on the unprovable region), ODC-034 (+ closure amendment), PoSe §8 / KDD-046 (where member-level blame lives), the W2.4 arc + `fd9a041` (the shipped producers).

**KDD:** KDD-080

**Next-free: KDD-081 / ODC-055.**

**ODC-055 (2026-07-28). ★ THE DKG ANNOUNCE-TIMING RACE — born-restricted ONE-SHOT relay × GMAUTH verify-latency × mesh topology: at realistic mesh density every ceremony starves (qual=0). DEFECT-WITH-FIX (catch-up relay on verify, `195edc4`). The EIGHTH compositional-seam finding — and the FIRST caught live-on-fleet, not by source-reading.**

**The defect.** The ceremony transport's relayHook is **born-restricted** (inv pushed only to GMAUTH-verified peers who are members of the resolved session — correct, anti-spam) and **one-shot** (a single call at store time — correct alone). But a ceremony's member-connections **open at ceremony start and GMAUTH-verify seconds later** — so the relay instant always precedes verification on fresh connections, the announce reaches zero verified member peers, no inv ever hits the wire, no getdata follows, and the ceremony aborts at the first window end with qual=0. Deterministic at every boundary. Proven on the N98 fleet with net logging: zero `got inv: ptxq*` lines fleet-wide while each member's store+relay leg demonstrably ran; the verified member-connection's conntime equals the ceremony start to the second.

**★ MASKED at N22, BIT at N98 — and the retroactive explanation.** On the 22-GM fleet every member was every other member's **long-lived, already-verified mesh peer**, so the store-time relay always found verified targets: ceremonies completed **by topology accident**. At N98 (8–19 mesh peers per node) the accident vanishes. ★ **This retroactively explains the N22 record**: both historical quorums formed **sub-11** (9 and 6 in_qual) — consistent with this race nibbling at whichever members' verification happened to lag — and SG-2b's 11/11 convergence was **never confirmed** (the roadmap caveat; the differential test rejected at IMP-D5). ★ **The program-level finding, recorded honestly: real-relay DKG delivery was never proven anywhere in the project** — unit ceremonies are in-process; N22 rode the mesh accident. The W2.5b fleet run is the first true exercise of the delivery layer, and it caught this within its first three boundaries — the fleet doing exactly what it exists to do.

**The fix (`195edc4`, additive-only).** `CatchupInvsForMember` (transport core: live-session membership → every stored inv across **all five phase stores** — family-wide by construction) + `PTX_Ceremony_CatchupRelayOnVerify` hooked at the GMAUTH verify point, immediately after the QSENDRECSIGS push — the codebase's existing on-verify-push shape. The store-time relay stays (correct for already-verified peers); the catch-up closes the late-verification case. No P2P protocol change, no consensus surface; dormant on non-DKG networks. Pinned by the late-verify unit row (INV-A: core neutered → fails alone with the qual=0 signature) and a **line-anchored** structural pin on the call site (INV-B: call commented → pin fails alone, behavioral green — an unwired fix is visible). ★ Disclosed: the pin's first form was bare-substring and **passed its own inversion** (a comment is not a caller — the W4d lesson firing on its own new pin); strengthened before landing.

**★ Observability (the lesson this cost).** The diagnostics that would have named this in minutes were `BCLog::NET`/`NET_GM`-gated — dark at the fleet's default level. Landed with the fix, **unconditional by posture**: the relayHook logs when an announce reaches 0 verified member peers (the silent-death condition), and the catch-up logs when it fires (the recovery observable). Rule worth keeping: **a delivery path dying silently must log at default level** — fail-safe aborts are only diagnosable if the leg that starved them says so.

**★ The owed test — registered against its real blocker, not faked.** The minimal standalone wire test for this class is NOT two nodes: **n=11 is hardcoded** (V5; the KDD-048/persist-t pre-testnet blocker), so the smallest true real-relay ceremony test is an 11-member fleet with members freshly connected at ceremony start. **Owed, BLOCKED-ON-PERSIST-T** — when KDD-048's parameterization lands, the small-N wire test lands with it. Until then the regression pin is the unit row and the wire proof is the fleet (the live RED: post-deploy boundary → relay/catch-up lines → `got inv: ptxq*` → getdata → qual; **clean 11/11 confirms the diagnosis and closes SG-2b's owed convergence record**; still-sub-11 falsifies and re-opens).

**The seam lineage (eighth):** ODC-042 as-of, ODC-045 can-never-rotate, the age anchor, the lineage clock, ODC-052 routing×reform, ODC-054 Guard-2×gate, the run_bootstrap arming gap (operational, unregistered), now **relay-restriction × verify-latency**. Every prior seam was found by source-reading; this one **required the fleet** — the two discovery modes are complementary, and the fleet's first catch validates the W2.5b investment.

**Cross-ref:** KDD-065 (the member-connections this races against), SG-0/GMAUTH (the verification whose latency is the race's other half), SG-2b (the owed convergence record this closes or re-opens), BUG-021 (the getdata leg, fixed earlier — this was the announce leg), KDD-077 §4 (fail-safe behaving correctly throughout — no wrong output, clean aborts), KDD-048/persist-t (the blocker the owed small-N test is registered against), `195edc4` (fix + pins), the N98 stand-up arc (registration `db52acf`/`0b1a8a6`).

**BUG-023 / BUG-024 / BUG-025 (2026-07-29→30). ★ THE h720 WEDGE + h480 REPLAY-FORK INCIDENT — one incident, THREE distinct defects, none of them the suspected one: consensus-affecting PTX state that is not a pure function of the chain. `225d127` EXONERATED of retro-rejection by a 2×2 counterfactual; the "accumulator desync" root-caused to a startup clobber + a fJustCheck ordering asymmetry. Chain declared disposable — findings registered from live evidence, fixes land in code, fleet rebuilds from block 0.**

**The incident.** N98 fleet, chain healthy to h719 (04:05Z 2026-07-29): the accumulator rolled h712 `09deb5…:0` (14.00) → h717 coalesce `0187ccfc…:0` (15.00), the 15.00 UTXO live at tip — **on-chain the accumulator tracking was PERFECT throughout**. The chain then froze at h719 (the h720 settlement boundary) for ~18 hours across two images, and a `-reindex` of gm01's datadir forked at 479, rejecting committed block 480 (`ptxpayout-wrong-recipient`). Initial readings — "225d127's tightened rule retro-rejects committed history" and "LotteryState/UTXO desync = state-tracking race under demand" — were both WRONG in mechanism; the three real defects follow.

**BUG-023 — the VerifyDB startup clobber (the "desync"'s actual origin).** `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB` at `nCheckLevel>=3` (`validation.cpp:3782-3796`) runs the REAL `DisconnectBlock` over the last `-checkblocks` (default 6) blocks. Its coins effects go to a throwaway `CCoinsViewCache` and its evoDb writes are sandboxed by the rollback transaction at `:3738` — but `UndoSpecialTxsInBlock` (`specialtx_validation.cpp:1495`) assigns `GetLotteryState() = prevState`, a bare in-memory global OUTSIDE both sandboxes. Level 3 never reconnects (the reconnect loop at `:3804` is level>=4 only), `LoadLotteryStateFromDB` runs BEFORE the verify step, and nothing reloads after. Net: **any restart with a PTXCOALESCE inside the checkblocks window silently regresses that node's live lottery state to the pre-coalesce snapshot** — deterministic, fleet-wide when the fleet restarts together, image-independent. PROOF (2026-07-29): every node's boot log shows the CORRECT load (`accumulator 0187ccfc… value=1500000000` at tip 719) immediately followed by `Verifying last 6 blocks at level 3` (window 714-719 ∋ 717), and every node's `ptx_lottery_status` then reports pool 14.00 — the persisted-vs-live split that cracked the case. ★ Under regressed VALIDATOR state the boundary is TRIPLE-SEALED — payout spending the regressed outpoint = `bad-txns-inputs-missingorspent`; no payout = `ptxpayout-missing-at-boundary` (P11 fires on the regressed state's live-looking accumulator); the CORRECT payout = `ptxpayout-wrong-input` (P2 compares against the regressed global) — and restart can never help, because restart is the trigger. Repair demonstrated restartlessly (atomic `invalidateblock`+`reconsiderblock` h718 restored the intact h717 snapshot into memory fleet-wide, pool 15.00 verified) before the chain was declared disposable. Note `verifychain` (RPC) reaches the same code at runtime. FIX SHAPE: VerifyDB must leave in-memory special-tx state as it found it (save/restore around the walk, or reload-from-tip-snapshot on exit — covers init and RPC callers alike).

**BUG-024 — the fJustCheck coalesce→payout ordering asymmetry (the wedge's actual demand coupling, and the probable 04:05 onset).** `ProcessSpecialTxsInBlock` §3.5 orders coalesce-apply before the payout checks *"so that LotteryState.accumulator_outpoint reflects the post-coalesce value when PTXPAYOUT P2 runs"* — but `CheckAndApplyPTXCoalesce` honors `fJustCheck` (checks, does NOT advance state) while P2/P5 read the global regardless. So a block containing BOTH a coalesce and a payout — exactly what `BlockAssembler::CreateNewBlock` builds at a settlement boundary with pending PTXSESS rolls, the payout deliberately spending the fresh coalesce's output — **fails its own `TestBlockValidity` (`fJustCheck=true`) with `ptxpayout-wrong-input`, though it would CONNECT fine (`fJustCheck=false` applies the coalesce first)**. Valid-on-connect, unbuildable-by-producer. Consequences at a boundary with pending rolls: the producer can neither include the payout (P2 self-reject) nor omit it (P11) nor exclude the rolls (the assembler doesn't know to) — a STRUCTURAL LIVENESS WEDGE that fires the first time settlement and roll demand coincide. PROOF: (a) post-state-repair reproduction 22:25Z — kernel found, candidate self-rejected `ptxpayout-wrong-input`, with a single leftover PTXSESS (`a5fd8cc3…`, mempool.dat-persisted) present in EVERY node's mempool; (b) all four historically successful settlements (480/540/600/660) are PAYOUT-ONLY blocks — no boundary ever carried a coalesce; h720 was the first boundary×demand coincidence. The 04:05 onset error text was never captured (pre-`812c606` staker deaths log a bare `ThreadStakeMinter() error` — the observability half of why `812c606` mattered), so the onset attribution is inference from the reproduction + the fleet-wide poison tx; recorded at that confidence, not higher. `225d127`'s containment was built on the misdiagnosis of this as a roll-tracking race; its skip branch composed with P11 into the missing-at-boundary loop (14 hours of skips) — the revert (`3b5d27b`) stands, and the revert ALONE does not unwedge (clobber + asymmetry both bite regardless of image). FIX SHAPE: compute the effective post-coalesce accumulator during validation and let P2/P5/P10-P11 read IT under both fJustCheck values — restoring the §3.5 intent without mutating under fJustCheck.

**BUG-025 — `ptx_pose.dat`: consensus-affecting state that survives `-reindex` (the h480 replay-fork; the "retro-rejection" REFRAMED).** gm01's `-reindex` (image `225d127`) rejected committed h480 at P10 (`ptxpayout-wrong-recipient`) and forked at 479. ★ THE 2×2 THAT SETTLED IT: clean-datadir reindexes of the SAME blk files replay the ENTIRE chain 0→719 green under BOTH `225d127` AND `3b5d27b` (`w2-fleet/reindex-check-{3b5d27b,225d127}`, preserved) — and both BUG-022-labelled commits are PRODUCER-only (`git show --stat`: `blockassembler.cpp` / `miner.cpp`; no validator diff exists). **The chain is replay-valid; no rule ever tightened; the image axis lost.** The datadir axis won: the PoSe tracker persists per-node (`ptx_pose.dat`, wall-clock-accumulated JSON), loads unconditionally at `init.cpp:1448`, and is NOT wiped by `-reindex` (`tiertwo/init.cpp:75` wipes evoDb only) — gm01's replay validated h480's winner selection against 88 FUTURE pose records; the clean runs logged `no state file found, starting fresh` and matched history. ★ THE CLASS, stated once for all three bugs: **P10/P11 winner selection and the P2 accumulator check consult state that is not derived purely from the chain being validated** — an in-memory global a startup walk can clobber (023), a check-time view an apply-order can starve (024), a node-local file a replay inherits from the future (025). The durable lesson survives the exoneration in corrected form: live-vs-replay divergence of consensus inputs IS the consensus-split shape — it just needs no rule change to arise. FIX SHAPE (owed, not landed with 023/024): wipe `ptx_pose.dat` on `-reindex` at minimum; the full question — pose as a pure chain function (the P11 comment's own standard, `specialtx_validation.cpp:1349-1366`) — is design-sized.

**Disposition.** Chain h0-719 DISPOSABLE by decision (contains nothing worth carrying; the poison PTXSESS and the 18h wedge die with it). Evidence preserved until this entry landed: `gm01`/`gm01-reindex` (datadir + both logs), the two `reindex-check-*` datadirs, `caller1-4` logs. Fleet deletes and rebuilds from block 0 on fixed HEAD carrying: the ODC-055 delivery fix (`195edc4`), the `812c606` staker-liveness half (RETAINED — it is what made every failure above a log line instead of a dead thread), the `225d127` revert, and the BUG-023/024 fixes. ★ NAME COLLISION recorded: `812c606`/`225d127`/`3b5d27b` and the surviving `miner.cpp` comment say "BUG-022", which this register had already assigned to the rpcevo lock-order inversion (`dfc00dd`) — in-source relabel to BUG-024 owed at next touch.

**Method note.** What discriminated was not a redeploy: the commit-scope check (`git show --stat` — producer-only) killed the rule-change theory in one command; the 2×2 replay matrix (datadir × image, four cheap containers) isolated the contamination axis; the load-line-vs-`ptx_lottery_status` split (persisted correct, live wrong) exposed the clobber; and the post-repair `wrong-input` reproduction unmasked the ordering asymmetry hiding UNDER the clobber. Four mechanisms were stacked at one height — each fix-attempt layer (`812c606`, `225d127`, the revert, the state repair) peeled one back and surfaced the next. A wedge with layered causes reads as ONE bug from the outside; only serial peel-and-reproduce separates them.

**Cross-ref:** ODC-050 (fail-safe→fail-dead — realized twice here: staker death pre-`812c606`, skip-loop after), KDD-077 §5 (liveness bounds), BUG-020 (view-dependent divergence lineage), BUG-022 (register row = rpcevo lockorder; collision noted above), `812c606` (retained liveness half), `225d127`+`3b5d27b` (containment + revert), ODC-055/`195edc4` (rides the fresh fleet), the P11 pose-derivation comment (the standard BUG-025 is held to), W2.5b fleet arc (`db52acf`/`0b1a8a6` — the fleet catching its second and third live finds).

**BUG:** BUG-023, BUG-024, BUG-025

> **AMENDMENT 2026-07-30 — ALL THREE FIXES LANDED, each RED-proven separately.** Order followed the operational bite, not the numbering: BUG-024 first (`ff1db80`), then BUG-025 (`ca4267b`), then BUG-023 (`a6ee1ab`). Unit suite 406 → 415, full run green.
>
> **BUG-024 (`ff1db80`).** `CheckAndApplyPTXCoalesce` now exports the EFFECTIVE post-block accumulator through out-params filled under BOTH `fJustCheck` values; P2/P5/P11 read that, never the raw global. §3.5's intent is restored without mutating state under `fJustCheck`, so `TestBlockValidity` and connect agree by construction. The apply path was audited for residual raw-global reads — it has none. RED: inverting to the raw-global read fails `Bug024_CoalescePlusPayout_AcceptsUnderJustCheck` with exactly `ptxpayout-wrong-input` (the h720 symptom); `Bug024_StalePayoutStillRejects` fails in the OPPOSITE direction, i.e. **the pre-fix code accepts the stale payout and rejects the correct one**; and `Bug024_CoalescePlusPayout_ConnectParity` still PASSES under the inversion — the asymmetry itself, reproduced.
>
> **BUG-023 (`a6ee1ab`).** A scoped sentry saves/restores the LotteryState global around `CVerifyDB`'s walk (after the evoDb rollback tx, destroyed before `cs_main` releases, so the lock requirement holds in the destructor). Covers init and the `verifychain` RPC. ★ SEVERITY SHARPENED: `-checklevel` is **rejected outright** at `init.cpp:1058` for ANY value including 4, so VerifyDB always runs at `DEFAULT_CHECKLEVEL = 3` — the clobbering path is not an unusual configuration, it is the only reachable one. RED/GREEN proven LIVE on the chain that produced the bug: fleet node `gm31`'s datadir, stopped clean at tip 719 with the h717 coalesce inside the default 6-block window and state at the correct 15.00, copied byte-identically twice; both runs logged `Verifying last 6 blocks at level 3` (the walk demonstrably ran) and both logged the same correct load line `value=1500000000` — unfixed `3b5d27b` → `pool_balance_sat 1400000000`, fixed → `1500000000`. The sentry is the only variable. NOT unit-tested, deliberately: the natural fixture (`TestChain100Setup`) belongs to the uncompiled legacy `test_Hemis` binary, hangs in its 100-block PoW ctor under `test_ptx`, and leaks `ECC_Start` state (it broke 24 downstream cases when tried) — a VerifyDB unit venue is owed **behind** that fixture debt, not ahead of it.
>
> **BUG-025 (`ca4267b`).** `PTXPoSeTracker::ResetForReindex()` clears in-memory records AND removes `ptx_pose.dat`/`.tmp` (never throws, per `Load()`'s contract), called immediately after `LoadBlockIndex` — the earliest point where `fReindex` reflects the on-disk flag as well as the command line, so an **interrupted-and-resumed** reindex (which carries no `-reindex` argument) is covered by the same call, still ahead of any `ThreadImport` replay. It clears memory too because the startup `Load()` runs before the flags are known. RED: a no-op body fails all four assertions of `Bug025_ResetForReindexClearsMemoryAndFile` while the `Bug025_StalePoseFileIsInherited` mechanism pin still passes.
>
> **COMPOSITION, pinned (`Bug023x024_ClobberedGlobalRejectsAtCoalesceNotPayout`).** With the global clobbered to the pre-coalesce accumulator, a valid coalesce+payout block is rejected at the **coalesce input check** — upstream of everything BUG-024 repairs. The two defects sit at different layers, both fixes are required, and this is independently why the `3b5d27b` revert alone did not unwedge the fleet. Full boundary × demand × restart composition on a live multi-node chain is owed at the fresh-fleet stand-up.
>
> **`225d127` DECIDED ON ITS MERITS — do NOT reinstate**, and on a harder ground than the exoneration. Its guard tests `HaveCoin(currentAccumOutpoint)` against `pcoinsTip`, but at a boundary with rolls the assembler has already advanced that outpoint to **the coalesce's own output, created in the same block** — which can never be in the UTXO set. So the check false-positives on *every legitimate coalesce+payout boundary* and, via P11, converts one unbuildable block into another — permanently, even with BUG-024 fixed. Its detection value (a specific log line instead of a bare `missingorspent`) is real; the skip response is not. `3b5d27b` stands.
>
> **In-source relabel discharged**: `miner.cpp`'s two incident comments BUG-022 → BUG-024, plus `gen_fleet.py`'s topology note. `rpcevo.cpp:807` is the genuine BUG-022 (lock-order) and was left alone.

**BUG-025's carried design question, registered as ODC-056 (2026-07-30).** ★ **PoSe IS NOT A PURE FUNCTION OF THE CHAIN — three legs, only one of which `ca4267b` closes.** The standard is the codebase's own: the comment at `specialtx_validation.cpp:1356-1366` records that `RecordHonestParticipation` was moved to all validators at block connect precisely to make "the pose tracker consensus-derived from the chain", after caller-vs-validator divergence had split consensus at every settlement boundary. Measured against that standard today: **(1) Persistence + unconditional load** — closed by `ca4267b` for the replay case (wipe on reindex), but the file remains a node-local cache of consensus-affecting state. **(2) No undo on disconnect** — `UndoSpecialTxsInBlock` contains NO pose handling (verified by inspection of the whole function), so pose is monotonic across reorgs: a node that experienced a reorg permanently over-counts honest participation relative to one that did not, and the two disagree on P10/P11 forever. This leg is entirely open and is NOT a reindex problem. **(3) The penalty paths are declared but never invoked** — `RecordWithhold` (+5), `RecordAbstain` (+2) and `RecordInvalidCommit` (+10) have no production call sites at all (only the deferral note at `rpc/ptx.cpp:366`), so `quorum_eligible` is currently always true for known nodes and the only live scoring is the honest-participation decay. Wiring them later re-opens the split unless done chain-derived. **Options:** (a) drop the file and recompute pose by replay; (b) keep it as a cache and wipe whenever the chain is replayed — what `ca4267b` does, leg (2) still open; (c) commit pose to evoDb with a per-block snapshot and undo, exactly as `LotteryState` already is, which is the only option that closes leg (2) as well. **Recommendation (c)**; decision owed before mainnet, and owed BEFORE any work that wires the penalty recorders. Not a defect row — a design question, deliberately not buried under the wipe.

**BUG-026 (2026-07-30). ★ THE h420 WEDGE — the POSE-AXIS `fJustCheck` ASYMMETRY, sibling of BUG-024, plus a REJECTED-BLOCK STATE LEAK. Found by the fresh-fleet composition proof, on FIXED code (`870acc7`), by the forced boundary × demand trigger the unit composition stood in for.** The N98 fleet's sole producer built settlement-boundary block h420 and **rejected its own block**: `CheckAndApplyPTXPayout: PTXPAYOUT output script does not match expected winner` → `ptxpayout-wrong-recipient` → `InvalidChainFound: invalid block=ae0c4a2c… height=420`, best stays h419. All 98 GMs wedged. The block carried 2 NORMAL + **6 PTXSESS + 1 PTXCOALESCE + 1 PTXPAYOUT** — the boundary × demand coincidence, deliberately forced.

**FINDING A — the asymmetry (the wedge).** `specialtx_validation.cpp`: `CheckAndApplyPTXCoalesce` (1336) → `if (!fJustCheck) { … RecordHonestParticipation(nid) … }` (1365, mutating pose with **this block's own** PTXSESS quorum_members) → `CheckAndApplyPTXPayout(…, g_ptx_pose_tracker, …)` (1381, reading the mutated tracker). Under `fJustCheck=true` the assembler skips 1365 and selects on **pre-block** pose; at connect 1365 runs and the validator selects on **post-block** pose. Entropy is identical on both paths (`f(420, hash(h419))`, per `ptx_payout.h:31`'s own contract) — **the only divergent input is the `poseTracker` argument**. ★ **DETERMINISTIC, not probabilistic:** `PTX_SelectWinner` (`ptx/ptx_winner_selection.cpp`) uses `total_tickets` as the modulus (`winning_ticket = entropy % total_tickets + 1`), measured **330 → 396** (+66 = 6 PTXSESS × 11 members), which re-maps the entropy across a different cumulative-ticket layout; the winner survives only by ~1-in-N coincidence. **TWO independent divergence channels:** the modulus shift, and `if (rec.lottery_tickets <= 0) return;` — a 0-ticket GM **enters the candidate set** on first credit, shifting every cumulative boundary as well. Measured divergence: W1 `yC4Sy2GroV4KCr4dvKEV636dCz6o9UHe3y` (assembler) vs W2 `yLHn3U2wF28KYAv288u8iFu8GZx7mPpMkE` (validator). **Exactly BUG-024's shape on a different axis** — `ff1db80` threaded the effective post-coalesce accumulator out under both `fJustCheck` values; **nothing equivalent was ever done for pose.**

**FINDING B — a failed `ConnectBlock` leaks its mutations into live state (distinct, arguably as severe).** Proven numerically at h419: the h418 PTXCOALESCE output — the true on-chain accumulator — is **16.00**, and the persisted snapshot reloaded at restart logged `value=1600000000` ✔; but the **live in-memory `pool_balance_sat` read 22.00** ✘, a **+6.00 gap exactly equal to the 6 rolls of the REJECTED block**. The rejected block's coalesce was applied to the live global and never rolled back. Consequences observed: the contaminated accumulator made the assembler reference an outpoint absent from the UTXO set, producing **48 consecutive `bad-txns-inputs-missingorspent` template failures, one per block, 15:07→15:54**, ending only at restart — i.e. **the wedge is self-perpetuating, each retry re-leaking**. (BUG-022's staker-liveness fix held throughout: `block-template construction FAILED … producer stays alive` — the only reason this was recoverable.) **Same family as BUG-023** ("VerifyDB must leave in-memory PTX state as it found it"); `a6ee1ab` closed the VerifyDB path, **the failed-ConnectBlock path is open**. This is a NEW leg of ODC-056 — not leg (2)'s reorg/disconnect case, but **no-undo-on-failed-connect**, which needs no reorg at all.

**RECOVERY (observed, not engineered) — and the falsification.** gm01 was stopped to snapshot its datadir and restarted; it reloaded clean state (16.00 / 330 tickets), rebuilt h420 and **connected**. ★ The recovery was **NOT** the mempool clearing — the connected block `21a70239…` carries the **same 6 PTXSESS + coalesce + payout and the same 21.99900000 amount**, differing only in recipient (W2). The single variable was clean pre-block state, which is the same root cause as Finding A. Post-recovery arithmetic exact: 16.00 + 6.00 = 22.00, payout 22.00 − 0.0001 = **21.99900000**, pool → 0, `last_settle` h420.

**NOT IMPLICATED.** **BUG-024 HELD** — the coalesce+payout block built with **zero** `ptxpayout-wrong-input` and **zero** `ptxpayout-missing-at-boundary`; the accumulator axis is repaired and this is a different seam. **BUG-025 remains correct** — this is a **second, non-reindex path** to `ptxpayout-wrong-recipient`, and does not weaken the stale-pose-on-reindex fix.

**SEVERITY: CRITICAL / liveness.** Permanent while demand continues; any settlement boundary carrying ≥1 roll wedges, and on a live network with continuous demand that is the normal case, not an exotic coincidence. Demand-dependence confirmed by contrast: h360 (payout-only, no rolls) connected clean.

**FIXTURE:** `w2-fleet/bug026-fixture/` (23M, md5-manifested) — the rejected block raw+decoded, W1, pre-block pose (44 cand / 330 tickets), entropy inputs, the 6 mempool PTXSESS, chain state at the wedge, gm01's 22M datadir **stopped cleanly AT the wedge**, and logs through the recovery.

**FIX SHAPE (two parts; B is the deeper one and A alone leaves B's leak intact).** **(A)** snapshot pose **before** the same-block `RecordHonestParticipation` and pass the snapshot to `CheckAndApplyPTXPayout`, so both `fJustCheck` paths read identical pre-block pose — mirroring `ff1db80`'s proven `effAccum` shape. Preferred over moving the update after 1381, which changes operation ordering and needs the 1365→1381 span audited for post-update dependencies. **(B)** a failed `ConnectBlock` must roll back its coalesce (and pose) mutations to live in-memory state — the transactional property `LotteryState` already has on disk via `WriteLotteryStateSnapshotForBlock` but not in memory on the failure path. ODC-056's recommendation (c) (pose into evoDb with per-block snapshot + undo) would close A's persistence leg and B's pose half together.

**Cross-ref:** BUG-024/`ff1db80` (same `fJustCheck` shape, accumulator axis — the direct sibling), BUG-023/`a6ee1ab` (same in-memory-state-not-transactional family, VerifyDB path), BUG-025/`ca4267b` (the other route to `wrong-recipient`), ODC-056 (pose not chain-derived — this adds the failed-connect leg and gives it a concrete liveness consequence), BUG-022 (staker liveness — held, and made recovery possible), ODC-050 (fail-safe → fail-dead), the W2.5b fresh-fleet composition proof (this find IS the proof earning its cost — the unit composition `Bug023x024_…` could not surface it).

**BUG:** BUG-026

> **AMENDMENT 2026-07-30 (i) — FIX B WAS INCOMPLETE: the rollback must reach the FILE.** Caught in review before deployment, and the h480 partition is the proof it mattered. `RecordHonestParticipation()` calls `Save()` on EVERY credit, so a block that is later rejected has **already written its credits to `ptx_pose.dat`**. The first cut of the sentry restored only the in-memory map, so a restart reloaded the contamination and the leak returned — which is exactly the asymmetry the partition exhibited: gm01's restart reloaded a **clean accumulator** (evoDb, per-block snapshot, rolled back by the surrounding transaction) but a **contaminated pose** (flat file, written eagerly, never rolled back). `PTXPoSeTracker::RestoreRecords()` now calls `Save()`. RED proven by inversion: commenting out that single `Save()` fails `Bug026_RestoreRecords_RoundTrips` — and ONLY that case — via a fresh `PTXPoSeTracker::Load()` that re-reads the file. Suite 415 → 419. ★ **SCOPE, stated so it is not over-read:** this makes the rollback **DURABLE, not TRANSACTIONAL**. A crash between the contaminating `Save()` and the restoring one still leaves the file dirty, and pose still has no per-block snapshot and no disconnect undo. Full transactionality = pose into evoDb with snapshot+undo exactly as `LotteryState` has (ODC-056 option (c)) — **owed, NOT closed here**. ★ **AUDIT RESULT that refutes the tempting unification:** adopting LotteryState's evoDb pattern for pose would NOT have fixed A or B. LotteryState already HAS that complete pattern and still leaked at h420 — the evoDb transaction correctly rolled back the *persisted* write (the h419 snapshot stayed at the true 16.00) while the *in-memory mirror* kept the rejected block's mutation (22.00). The family therefore splits into **three independent axes**: read-ordering (A), in-memory rollback (B), durability/undo (ODC-056). One more in-memory PTX state remains **uncovered**: `PTX_DKG_Commitments_EraseMined` mutates the relay store at rel-line 76 of `ProcessSpecialTxsInBlock` with **four** downstream failure points after it, and `E-5`'s "disconnect does NOT re-pend" justifies a *disconnect*, not a *failed connect* — `PTX_DKG_Commitments_ForceAdd` already exists to restore it, so extending the sentry is ~15 lines plus a `GetAll` accessor. `CPTXQuorumStore::recordCache` is uncovered but benign **by position only** (last mutation before commit) — fragile, since inserting any check after it silently opens a leak.
>
> **AMENDMENT 2026-07-30 (ii) — OPERATIONAL: under state contamination, in-place single-node repair EJECTS the repaired node.** Recorded because the intuitive remedy is actively harmful. At h480 the fleet split **1 vs 101**, and the 101 were **perfectly unanimous** (one h480 hash, one state tuple `pool 0 / tickets 341`) — no fragmentation. But it held for the wrong reason: all 98 had been contaminated **identically** by the same rejected block, so they stayed mutually consistent. **Consistency is not correctness.** The node carrying the CORRECT state — gm01, cleaned by the restart I performed to snapshot its datadir — is the one consensus expelled. ★ Therefore: **restarting a stuck node forks it off**; recovery must be simultaneous across all nodes, or a rebuild from clean. Deploying the fix fleet-wide did NOT heal the existing split either (pose contamination is persisted in `ptx_pose.dat`; `BLOCK_FAILED_VALID` is persisted in the block index) — **the fix is preventive, not curative**. ★ Second-order finding from the same event: **the single-producer premise is self-defeating under demand.** The majority's h480 was staked by the h420 payout winner — winning 21.99990000 HMS gave that GM spendable coins and made it a staker. The lottery de-concentrates block production by construction, so "restarting the producer halts the chain" stops being true at the first settlement. Any future drill that assumes a controlled single producer must either run before the first settlement or accept de-concentration. ★ Third: every fleet-wide measurement must be **fleet-wide**. The h480 partition stayed invisible for ~40 minutes because the checkpoint probe polled `gm01` alone, and `gm01` was the fork. The dashboard now reports the **majority** height with forks called out in the headline and sources all chain data from a majority-chain node.

**★ ptx-bea PoC DECOMMISSIONED — the isolation guardrail is RETIRED, deliberately (2026-07-30).** The W2.0a isolation spine carried a standing operational guardrail — *"zero lifecycle commands against `ptx-bea-*`/ptxtestnet"* — which every W2 session honoured and reported ("GUARDRAILS HELD"). It existed because the ptx-bea PoC was **publicly running** on 172.30.0.0/24 while W2 built alongside it on 172.31.0.0/24. **That premise is gone:** the PoC runs the retired trusted dealer on a different branch and images (already recorded at KDD-069), it is superseded by the ptx-w2 fleet in every capability the program still needs, and it had degraded to a crash-loop — **9 of its 12 containers at ~33,959 restarts each**, a continuous spawn/IO drain on the fleet host that was invisible to `docker stats` (0.09% steady CPU) and was competing with the W2.5b bootstrap for the same array. **Decommissioned by explicit decision, not silent bypass:** `docker compose -p docker-bea down` (12 containers), plus the dead ptxtestnet demo fleet nodes in project `docker` (`ptx-caller` + `ptx-gm01..gm11`, several already exited/restarting). **RETAINED from project `docker`:** `ptx-cloudflared` (upstream `cloudflare/cloudflared:latest`, token-based tunnel — ingress is managed remotely in the Cloudflare dashboard, so the `ptx-explorer.lnky.uk` hostname survives independently of anything on disk) and `ptx-explorer` (the ~580-line Flask origin, `explorer/proxy.py`, serving `/static` + a `/ptx-api` proxy leg) — kept deliberately as the **serving shell** for the W2.5b fleet dashboard, which needs only a file drop into `explorer/static/` plus one `send_from_directory` route. `ptx-grafana`/`ptx-prometheus` deliberately NOT swept (unnamed in the decision; repointable at ptx-w2). ★ **The guardrail is retired ONLY for ptx-bea/ptxtestnet.** The isolation discipline it encoded — explicit `-p <project>` on every lifecycle command, named services rather than blanket sweeps, never acting on an ambiguous target — **stands and is reaffirmed**: it is what surfaced that "the PoC" was ambiguous between projects `docker` and `docker-bea` before anything was destroyed. Cross-ref KDD-069 (PoC on a different branch/images — the supersession), W2.0a §2 (the isolation spine that created the guardrail), BUG-026 (the W2.5b arc this frees resources for).

**★ PHASE 2 TOPOLOGY — WALLET-LESS GAMEMASTERS (decided 2026-08-01, source-verified before adoption).** GMs move to `-disablewallet=1 -gamemaster=1 -gmoperatorprivatekey=<bls>`; collateral, payouts and staking all move caller-side. **This is a STRUCTURAL close of the BUG-019 class, not a mitigation:** `ThreadStakeMinter` is created inside `#ifdef ENABLE_WALLET` **and** gated on `!vpwallets.empty()` (`init.cpp:1889-1891`), so with no wallet the staker thread is **never created** — a GM cannot consume its own collateral because it has neither coins nor a staker. **Feasibility was verified against source, not assumed:** the whole GM-side PTX surface is wallet-free — of 30 `src/ptx/*.cpp` only `ptx_wallet.*` (a read-only `CKeyStore` view helper, KDD-035) and `ptx_mempool.cpp::PTX_AutoCommit` (**caller-side** PTXSESS funding) touch a wallet; the DKG ceremony runs entirely on `operator_sk` (`CBLSSecretKey`); and `BuildPTXDKGTx` emits a tx with **no vin and no vout** which per KDD-058 is block-injected and never relayed or mempooled, so there is nothing to fund. Wallet-SAFE and confirmed usable against a wallet-less GM: `protx_list` (tolerates `pwallet==nullptr`; only `wallet_only=true` throws), `generateblskeypair`, and all `ptx_lottery_status`/`ptx_pose_status`/`ptx_quorum_*` status RPCs — so `fleet_watch`/`quorum_roster`/the dashboard keep polling GMs unchanged. ★ **Trap recorded:** `generatetoaddress` is **NOT** wallet-safe despite taking an explicit address (`EnsureWalletIsAvailable`, `mining.cpp:155`), and under `-disablewallet` wallet RPCs are **unregistered outright** (`RegisterWalletRPCCommands` early-return), so stray GM-side calls fail `RPC_METHOD_NOT_FOUND "Method not found (disabled)"` rather than returning empty. ★ **BUG-019 MOVES, it does not vanish** — collateral now lives in the caller wallet and the caller is the staker, which is precisely the holds-collateral-AND-stakes condition. It is covered automatically because the daemon protection is **wallet-scoped, not role-scoped**: `init.cpp:1886` fires `LockGamemasterCollaterals()` on `!vpwallets.empty()` (no `-gamemaster` required), it locks any `IsMine` DGM collateral across every wallet in `vpwallets`, and at `870acc7` it precedes the staker start. The harness guards are therefore **RETARGETED to the caller and KEPT, never retired** (`relock_collaterals`, `validate_fleet.lock_gate`). Harness changes: a single `cluster.treasury` (the primary caller) now owns every wallet RPC that `gm01` used to; `gen_fleet.py --gm-wallet-less` (default 1) emits the topology and **refuses** `--gm-wallet-less 1` with `--caller-staking 0`, which would emit a fleet with **no block producer at all** (wallet-less GMs have no staker, staking-off callers have none either — the chain would halt at PoS activation).

> **KDD-035 AMENDMENT (2026-08-01) — forced by the topology, recorded because the INTENT changes.** KDD-035 put each GM's `scriptPTXPayment` on the **GM's own wallet** so that "the winning GM's wallet reflects the win" — attribution by wallet ownership. A wallet-less GM cannot hold a payment address, so that intent is **not implementable** under Phase 2 and is amended rather than quietly dropped. **AMENDED:** payment addresses are minted **caller-side but PER-GM and DISTINCT** — one fresh treasury address per GM. **What changes:** where the win is reflected (a caller-side per-GM address, not the GM's wallet). **What is preserved:** the property that actually mattered — per-GM attribution, resolved by looking the address up in the registration map, persisted as `ptx_payment_addr` per label in `registration-N*.json`. ★ A single shared caller address would collapse per-GM attribution and is **explicitly rejected**. The KDD-035 home docs (`ptxbea-api-reference.md`, `ptxbea-operator-guide.md`) carry the primary registration; this is the DKG-side pointer amendment.

> **TRANSPORT: NOT a new design — KDD-058-A already covers it (verified 2026-08-01).** The wallet-less topology raised "how does the nType=11 body reach a caller-producer?" as an apparent open question. It is **already built, wired and proven**, and its stated rationale is exactly this problem: *"locked collateral cannot stake … near-zero GM stakeable balance is the DESIGNED steady state — a finished nType=11 result held only by quorum members lands ~never at scale."* Chain: `PTX_DKG_ClosePhase5` → `PTX_DKG_Commitments_AddAndRelay` (`ptx_dkg.cpp:1585`, **network-wide RelayInv, deliberately not the member mesh**) → `PTXDKGCOMMIT` + `MSG_PTX_DKG_COMMITMENT` inv/getdata → replicated store on every node → **any staker's assembler** (`blockassembler.cpp:284`) → `EraseMined` at connect. ★ **Live proof on the N98 fleet:** 7 of 8 ACTIVE quorums were mined by `gm01` while `gm01` was **not a member** (mined h2247–h2457) — the payload demonstrably crosses non-member → third-party producer → block. The only Phase-2 variable is that the producer becomes a **caller** rather than a GM: identical code path, different wallet-holder. **Decision: Option A — verify in the rebuild** with one added gate (a quorum forms **and** is mined in a caller-produced block); no code change, and the rebuild proves formation anyway.

> **ODC-057 (opened 2026-08-01) — should un-mined DKG commitments be PERSISTED?** The KDD-058-A store is **replication-only by deliberate design**, Dash-faithful: *"minableCommitments is memory+replication in the lineage; only MINED commitments hit disk."* Consequence: a **whole-network simultaneous restart loses un-mined entries** (single-node restarts re-learn from peers via inv; a fresh boundary re-forms). This is a **documented accepted trade, not a defect** — registered here so it is decided on its own merits rather than folded into the Phase-2 rebuild, where it would be a second moving variable against the like-for-like economics baseline. **Options:** (a) leave as-is, matching the lineage; (b) persist un-mined commitments with startup reconciliation; (c) shorten exposure by reducing FINALIZE→mined latency instead. ★ **Distinct from — and must not be conflated with —** the uncovered `PTX_DKG_Commitments_EraseMined` failed-connect leak noted in BUG-026 AMENDMENT (i): that is an in-memory rollback gap on the failure path (sentry extension, ~15 lines + a `GetAll` accessor); this is a durability question about the un-mined set across a total restart. Both touch the same store; neither closes the other. **Owed before mainnet; no decision taken here.**

**★ BUG-027 — POSE DIVERGENCE ACROSS REORGS HALTS A WALLET-LESS FLEET (opened 2026-08-01, measured on the Phase-2 rebuild).** Root cause is **ODC-056 leg (2)**, already registered and still open: `UndoSpecialTxsInBlock` (`specialtx_validation.cpp:1556`) restores `LotteryState` from its pprev evoDb snapshot, then undoes the quorum store, the DGM manager and LLMQ — and **touches pose nowhere** (verified: zero pose references in the whole function). `RecordHonestParticipation` credits at connect are therefore **never removed on disconnect**, so pose is monotonic across reorgs while the accumulator is not.

**MEASURED EVIDENCE** (artifact: `w2-fleet/bug027-capture/bug027-evidence.json` + `growth.jsonl`; fleet held HALTED as a preserved exhibit). The invariant `pose_ticket_sum == 11 × total_rolls` holds on a clean node. On the rebuilt N98/8-caller fleet at `total_rolls = 2` (expected 22):

| tickets | nodes | reading |
|---|---|---|
| 22 | 95 | clean — no chain switches |
| 33 | caller4, caller6 | credited 1.5× |
| 44 | caller5, caller7 | credited 2× |
| 88 | gm01, gm02, gm24, gm49, gm50, gm73, gm98 | credited **4×** |

Ticket count scales with chain-switch count, confirmed directly: **gm01 = 48 tip-height regressions / 88 tickets; gm03 = 14 regressions / 22 tickets.** ★ **The accumulator was CORRECT on all 106 nodes** (`rolls=2`, `pool=1.0`) — because `LotteryState` rides evoDb snapshot+undo and pose rides a flat file with neither. That asymmetry is the diagnosis, and it is why this is **NOT a BUG-026 regression**: BUG-026 [A] fixed within-node `fJustCheck` read-ordering and [B] fixed within-node failed-connect rollback; both demonstrably hold here. This is the **third axis** the BUG-026 commit explicitly carved out as not closed.

**★ THE ESCALATION — a consensus defect becomes a LIVENESS failure.** Divergent pose selects a different settlement winner, so the h300 boundary block was rejected (`ptxpayout-wrong-recipient`, 11 nodes), stranding callers on **7 separate tips**. Under the wallet-less topology **the callers ARE the entire producer set** — the 98 GMs run `-disablewallet=1` and have no staker thread — so stranding the producers **fragmented the stake**:

```
MAJORITY tip (99 nodes, h319) — last block 20:57, static 48 min on a 60s chain
TOTAL stakeable 122,357 HMS | ON THE MAJORITY TIP 505 HMS = 0.4%
caller1 (treasury, 104,837 = 86% of stake) stranded off-tip at h313
only caller3 (0.4%) producing on the chain 99 nodes agree on
```

The 99 nodes holding the majority chain **cannot extend it**, because 98 of them are structurally unable to stake. **CHAIN HALT.** This was impossible on the pre-Phase-2 topology, where gm01 held the stake *and* mined and so could never be stranded away from its own chain. Making GMs wallet-less is correct and stays — but it **couples the divergence-prone population to the producing population**, and that coupling converts a consensus-hygiene defect into a halt.

**FIX = ODC-056 option (c)**, no longer "owed before mainnet" but **BLOCKING for any multi-producer (i.e. shipping) topology**: pose into evoDb with per-block snapshot + undo, mirroring `LotteryState`'s proven template (`ps_S`/`ps_H` keys; write at the same `!fJustCheck` points that already write the LotteryState snapshot; restore from the pprev snapshot as the FIRST act of `UndoSpecialTxsInBlock`; refuse-the-disconnect on a missing snapshot rather than restoring a default; `PurgeStaleSnapshots` analog). The flat file degrades to a cache, which also closes **ODC-056 leg (1)**. RED is measured, not reconstructed: the halted fleet itself is the failing case.

> **★ ODC-058 (opened 2026-08-01) — LIVENESS COUPLING IS A PROPERTY OF THE WALLET-LESS TOPOLOGY, not of BUG-027.** Registered separately because it outlives its first instance. When GMs cannot stake, the producer set and the divergence-prone set are **the same population**, so *any* mechanism that strands producers on different chains — pose today; network partitions, latency asymmetry or ban-driven isolation in Phase 4 — fragments stake and can halt the chain, even when every individual node is behaving correctly. The old single-producer topology was immune by accident, not by design: one node held both the stake and the mining role. ★ **Consequence for Phase 4 (partition testing): a partition on this topology is not "diverge now, reconcile later" — it is a candidate HALT**, and the recovery question ("which chain accumulates enough stake to win?") has no good answer when stake is concentrated in a stranded node. Phase 4 planning must treat producer-stranding as a first-class failure mode and decide the intended mitigation (stake distribution policy? producer-side chain-preference rules? a liveness floor?) BEFORE running partitions. Not a defect row — a structural property of the chosen topology, recorded so it is designed for rather than rediscovered.

> **LESSON (recorded because it cost ~48 minutes of misreading): a static readout on a chain that should be advancing is a HALT, not steady state.** The BUG-027 capture logged `h=319 … clean=95 contaminated=11 markers=68` unchanged for 48 minutes and it was reported as stable — every *derived* field was indeed stable, which is exactly what made it read as healthy. Height is the liveness signal and it must be judged against **elapsed wall-clock and the target block time**, never against its own previous sample. Monitors that surface derived state must surface *blocks-since-last-advance* alongside it, or a halted chain looks identical to a quiet one.

> **★ BUG-027 CLOSED 2026-08-02 — ODC-056 option (c) LIVE-VALIDATED; ODC-056 leg (2) CLOSED with it.** Fix `98c747e` (pose snapshot `ps_S`/`ps_H` written before `ptxStateSentry.commit()`; restore from the pprev snapshot as the first act of `UndoSpecialTxsInBlock`; refuse-the-disconnect on a missing snapshot). Proven on the fleet under the same realism that exposed the bug — 98 wallet-less GM / 8 staking callers, 60s blocks — for a **6-hour soak, h≈347 → h773 (426 blocks)**. ★ **NON-VACUITY FIRST, because a reorg-undo fix proven without reorgs proves nothing:** ~**160 chain switches per producer** (gm01 160, caller3 161, gm03 106, caller1 63) — the disconnect path was exercised ~160×. Against that: pose **106/106 CONSENSUS-IDENTICAL at every sample** (`rolls`/`tickets`/`records` a single signature fleet-wide), **zero wedge markers on all 106 debug.logs** (all five patterns), **7 settlement boundaries passed** (h=360/420/480/540/600/660/720), chain **never stalled >30s** against a 360s alert floor, tips transient-1-to-9 and always reconverging to 1. Compare the pre-fix measurement in the entry above: per-node divergence scaling with each node's own reorg count (gm01 48 regressions → 88 tickets), 7 tips, stake fragmented to 0.4%, **48-minute halt**. ★ **What the closure actually rests on:** the failure was *compound* — divergence → wrong-recipient rejection → producer stranding → stake fragmentation → halt — and the fix breaks it at **link 1** (pose is now transactional across disconnect), so links 2–5 cannot fire. ODC-058's coupling is untouched and remains open by design: it is a property of the topology, not of this defect. The unit case `Bug027_PoseReversedOnDisconnect_NotMonotonicAcrossReorgs` proves the mechanism reverses; only the live soak proves the compound outcome, and only the multi-producer topology generates the reorgs that make either meaningful.

> **★ ODC-059 (opened 2026-08-02) — FORM-WITHOUT-COMPLETE: a quorum can run DEGRADED indefinitely, and nothing says so.** ★ **The instance, measured:** during the BUG-027 soak, quorum `d7d212e5` (formation h=390, mined h=417) shows `formed_size: 11` / `completed_size: 10` — member `gm98:78e76cc9` carries `in_qual: false`, has **no PoSe record at all**, and consequently the quorum awards **10 tickets per roll, not 11**. It ran that way for **~380 blocks** (h=390 → h=773) while every other quorum in the window was 11/11. ★ **Not a defect:** 10 ≥ t=6, the quorum formed, completed, mined, went active and has been rolling correctly; all 106 nodes agree exactly (`records = Σ completed_size = 87`); zero consensus impact. Registered because the **observability gap is real and outlives this instance**: the *only* thing that surfaced a degraded quorum was ticket arithmetic in an external soak harness — no log line, no RPC alarm, no gate. A member fell out of a live quorum and the system's own instrumentation was silent. ★ **Open questions owed (the reason this is a register entry and not a note):** **(1) Detection** — should a `completed_size < formed_size` quorum raise a first-class signal (log at formation, a field the fleet gates already read, an RPC alarm), rather than being inferable only by arithmetic? **(2) Retry vs limp** — is form-without-complete meant to be tolerated silently for the quorum's whole life, or should a member that forms and fails to complete be retried, replaced, or the ceremony re-run? Current behaviour is limp-forever, by omission rather than by decision. **(3) Below-threshold behaviour** — the case that makes this worth registering: **what happens if enough members fail to complete to drop the quorum under t?** Does it refuse to go active, go active and fail at first use, or go active and wedge? That path is unexercised and unspecified; one member out of eleven happened to be harmless, and nothing in the design says the next one will be. ★ Cross-ref: KDD-045 (static membership + periodic same-set re-DKG — a member that never completes is a re-DKG input, not just a one-off), ODC-025 (rotation cadence), ODC-058 (degraded-producer populations are a liveness question on this topology). **Harmless this time; a design consideration owed before mainnet.**

> **★ ODC-059 AMENDMENT (i) — 2026-08-02: MECHANISM MEASURED, and one claim in the entry above is WRONG.** Root-caused from the fleet after the entry was written, prompted by a single `1 ceremony ABORT(s)` on the dashboard. ★ **MECHANISM — a propagation-timing split at the ceremony-trigger anchor, NOT a divergence.** The quorum hash *is* the h390 block hash: the ceremony triggers on connecting that anchor. That block propagated to the fleet with a **+61s one-block split — 15 nodes at 01:41:00Z, 91 nodes at 01:42:01Z** (compare a healthy anchor, h450: 105 of 106 within the same second). **gm98 was alone-early; all ten of its co-members were in the late 91.** So gm98 opened its session at 01:41:00 and broadcast HASH_COMMIT **into a void — no other member had a session yet** — and was therefore never in the other ten's qualified set. Because **absent ≠ faulty**, `bad=0` on every node and **no complaint ever fired**; gm98 then ran all four phases believing `qual=11` while the other ten held `qual=10` (verified: 10 nodes report `qual=10`, gm98 alone reports `qual=11`, at both h406 and h410), and aborted at PREMIT close with `(sub-threshold close)`. ★ **NO reorg was involved** — every node walked the identical chain 388→389→390→391, same hashes; this is not BUG-027's family and not a consensus divergence. ★ **CORRECTION to the entry above:** it states the degradation surfaced only via ticket arithmetic — *"no log line, no RPC alarm, no gate."* **The "no log line" claim is false.** There is a precise one: `PTX formation: ceremony ABORTED quorum_hash=d7d212e5… (sub-threshold close)`, emitted by gm98 at 02:01:02Z. The real gap is **narrower and more actionable**: the abort is logged **only on the node that loses**, one line among 106 debug logs, and **nothing aggregates or surfaces it** — the fleet dashboard's `ABORT` counter is in fact the one instrument that did its job, and it is what prompted this root-cause. **Therefore question (1) Detection resolves toward PROPAGATING AN EXISTING SIGNAL (aggregate the losing node's abort; reconcile `completed_size < formed_size` against it), not inventing a new one.** ★ **QUESTION (3) UPGRADED — theoretical → DEMONSTRATED.** The instance was benign only because the split fell **1-early / 10-late** and 10 ≥ t=6. The *same* 15/91 propagation split straddling a quorum differently — e.g. **5-early / 6-late** — is a **live, measured mechanism for driving a quorum below t**. Below-threshold is no longer a hypothetical branch to specify; there is a demonstrated path to it. ★ **RECORDED AS UNPROVEN, deliberately not asserted:** it is tempting to conclude that ODC-055's catch-up relay rescues *late* members while nothing protects an *early* one — which would neatly explain why the early member is the victim, and the relay is certainly live (915 catch-up events fleet-wide). **But this dataset contains no late-member case**: the only late node at the h450 anchor was `caller6`, a non-member. A hypothesis to TEST, not a finding. ★ **CROSS-REF — LATENCY-ADJACENT, forward to Phase 4.** This is propagation-timing-induced, so it belongs to the latency class rather than the reorg class: **a naturally-occurring +61s anchor split is a mild preview of what netem will induce deliberately in Phase 4**, and question (3)'s below-threshold path is a Phase-4 concern. Phase 4 should treat "ceremony straddles a propagation split" as a named case alongside ODC-058's producer-stranding.

> **★ ODC-059 AMENDMENT (ii) — 2026-08-02: THE DISPOSITION QUESTION (4), ASKED AND ANSWERED IN ONE PASS. What happens to an aborted member's ELIGIBILITY? → SCENARIO A, BY EXPLICIT DESIGN: it is sidelined until its quorum dissolves.** ★ **SOURCE (the eligibility gate's membership predicate).** `PTX_Formation_BuildPool` (`ptx/ptx_formation.cpp:34`) excludes on the **FORMED** set, and says so deliberately: *"D-SG1a-1: the exclusion set is the FULL formed-11 of every ACTIVE record **(QUAL or not)**, keyed by proTxHash"* — it iterates `rec.members`, never the qualified subset. This is a **conscious decision, not an oversight**; the surrounding KDD-060 comment explains the shape (filter-then-score, exclusion applied *before* scoring so a post-score drop cannot change selection and split the chain). ★ **MEASUREMENT (`ptx_debug_selectquorum` @ h810, run on four nodes incl. gm98 itself): `eligible=98  active_excluded=88  pool=10  formed=False`.** 88 = Σformed over the 8 active quorums, **gm98 included despite `in_qual:false` and no PoSe record**. Under a completed-based predicate the pool would be **98 − 87 = 11 → a 9th quorum would form**. Observable consequence: **17 consecutive `pool below threshold — deterministic skip` events, every formation boundary from h540 to h810**, i.e. **gm98 alone is the single GM standing between this fleet and a 9th quorum.** ★ **Scenario B REFUTED** (gm98 was not returned to the pool; it has appeared in no formation since h390). ★ **Scenario C REFUTED — no split-brain:** gm01, gm28, caller1 **and gm98 itself** all compute the identical `pool=10`, so gm98 agrees it is excluded. The `qual=11` vs `qual=10` disagreement was confined to the live ceremony and did **not** survive into chain-derived eligibility, which is uniform. ★ **MAGNITUDE, stated honestly so the leak is neither dismissed nor inflated:** the pool ceiling binds *anyway* on this fleet — 98 GMs at 11/quorum with no dissolution in-window = 8 quorums + 10 spare — so exhaustion is not itself the finding. The finding is that **each form-without-complete permanently costs exactly one GM of headroom for its quorum's entire lifetime, and these ACCUMULATE across events**. One costs a 9th quorum; under repeated propagation splits (amendment (i)) the drain is cumulative and monotonic until dissolution. **A slow leak, confirmed — Scenario A as feared, not the self-healing Scenario B.** ★ **RESIDUAL DECISION OWED (the genuinely open part):** should the predicate stay **formed**-based (conservative — a formed member might hold usable key material, and the exclusion is applied pre-scoring for determinism) or become **completed/qual**-based (self-healing — returns the sidelined GM at the next boundary)? ★ Feasibility note that makes the second option real rather than theoretical: **`in_qual` is carried in the persisted quorum record** (it is what `ptx_quorum_info` reports and what every node agreed on above), so a completed-based predicate is **computable deterministically from chain state** and would not reintroduce a selection split. Weigh against KDD-045 (static membership + periodic same-set re-DKG) and the rotation/dissolution cadence (ODC-025), which together set how long "until its quorum dissolves" actually is.

> **★ ODC-059 AMENDMENT (iii) — 2026-08-02: THE PREDICATE DECISION, REGISTERED AS IN-PROGRESS. Gate (1) ANSWERED — the aborted member IS crypto-clean; (2) and (3) OWED. ★ DO NOT CHANGE THE PREDICATE UNTIL (2) IS RESOLVED.** Amendment (ii) established that a completed-based predicate is *feasible* (in_qual is chain-persisted, so no selection-split risk). Feasible is not sufficient; three trades gate it, and only the first is now closed. ★ **(1) KEY-MATERIAL SAFETY — ANSWERED: RELEASING AN ABORTED MEMBER IS SAFE. Four independent confirmations.** **(a) Phase gate, applied twice:** `PTX_DKG_StoreSkShare` (`ptx/ptx_dkg.cpp:1429`) and its sole caller (`:1558`) each `return false` unless `session.phase == PTXDKGPhase::FINALIZE`. gm98 aborted at **PREMIT — two phases short** — so the store is refused at the first line of both. **(b) Single writer:** `StoreSkShare` has exactly one call site, inside the FINALIZE path; the only other `PTX_BLS_PersistShare` callers (`ptx_bls.cpp:195/197/308`) are rotation promote/supersede paths operating on shares already held, unreachable from an aborted ceremony. **(c) Measured on the node itself:** gm98's debug.log contains **zero** occurrences of `StoreSkShare` / `sk_share written` / `sk_share stored` — it persisted nothing, in memory or on disk. **(d) Per-ceremony freshness:** every ceremony generates a fresh secret polynomial (`GetStrongRandBytes(ikm,32)` per coefficient, `t` coefficients, `ptx_dkg.cpp:377-385`), so a future formation starts from new randomness with no cross-ceremony reuse. ★ **The one residue, and why it is inert:** gm98 *did* accumulate `session.sk_share_i` in memory (`:1214`) before aborting, but it was never persisted, the session object is destroyed in the teardown epilogue that every exit funnels through (`SetActiveSession(nullptr)` → `ClearForming` → `ClearLive`, "the session dies when the last in-flight dispatch completes"), and it is cryptographically meaningless regardless: the group key was computed from the **10 qualified contributions excluding gm98's**, so its accumulator is not a share of the live group key. **Conclusion: the safety objection to a completed-based predicate is DISCHARGED.** ★ **(2) RELEASE vs REPAIR — OWED, and it is the live question now.** Releasing the member (completed-based predicate) is not obviously the right fix: **KDD-045 treats a never-completing member as a re-DKG input**, which points at *repairing the quorum* (re-run the ceremony to complete `d7d212e5` at 11) rather than *abandoning the member to the pool*. These are different fixes with different costs — release is a one-line predicate change that leaves a permanently-degraded 10/11 quorum in service; repair restores full strength but needs a re-DKG trigger, and re-DKG on a *degraded but functioning* quorum is itself an availability event. **Not decided here.** ★ **(3) DISSOLUTION CADENCE — OWED; the measured input, which sizes URGENCY, is now on record and it is not reassuring.** Over the whole soak: **ZERO dissolutions, supersessions or rotations — all 8 quorums `superseded_height=-1 disbanded_height=-1 last_rotation_height=-1`, the oldest ACTIVE for 746 blocks (formed h300, tip h1046)**. So "until its quorum dissolves" is, on this fleet today, **unbounded** — nothing retires a quorum, and the sidelined GM is sidelined indefinitely. Under ODC-025's proposed N=1440 the window would be ~24h per event; with rotation not yet running it is ∞. **This moves the leak from "short-lived and self-limiting" toward "long-binding", i.e. worth fixing — but the fix is (2)'s open choice, not automatically the predicate change.** ★ **SEQUENCING + PHASE-4 COUPLING:** resolve **(2)** next, then let **Phase 4 size the event RATE** — this is a latency-adjacent defect (amendment (i)), the trigger is a propagation split, and netem will amplify exactly that. **The predicate decision is therefore best MADE WITH the Phase-4 latency picture in hand**, since latency drives how often GMs are sidelined and thus whether a 1-GM-per-event leak is negligible or compounding. Until then the predicate stays **formed**-based, unchanged.

> **★ ODC-059 AMENDMENT (iv) — 2026-08-02: PERIODIC RE-KEY EXISTS AND IS WIRED. (3) CORRECTED — the leak is BOUNDED, not unbounded; (2) REFRAMED — the repair path is ALREADY BUILT.** ★ **CORRECTION to amendment (iii), and it is my error, stated plainly.** (iii) recorded "until its quorum dissolves" as **UNBOUNDED** on the evidence that all 8 quorums carry `last_rotation_height=-1`. That inference was **wrong**: it read a sentinel as terminal without checking the interval against the ages. `nRotationInterval` is a live chainparam — `consensus.ptxFormation = {"ptxbea", 30, 1440, 80, 8, 200, 1, 40}` (`chainparams.cpp:986`; fields B / **R** / budget / L / retire / grace / rate) — so **R = 1440**, and the oldest quorum was **802 blocks old against a 1440 interval: NOT YET DUE, not never.** ★ **The corrected figure: the sideline window is BOUNDED BY R = 1440 blocks (~24h at 60s), not unbounded.** ODC-025's N=1440 is therefore **not merely proposed — it is configured and running.** ★ **ERROR CLASS, recorded because it is the STANDING DIAGNOSTIC's sibling:** *a sentinel field (`-1` / empty / unchanged) read as TERMINAL ("never rotates") when it means PROVISIONAL ("hasn't rotated yet")*. The diagnostic's rule was "a static readout is not steady state"; this is the same mistake on a different axis — **before concluding "never", check the schedule that would make it "not yet"**. ★ **(2) RELEASE vs REPAIR — REFRAMED: the repair path already exists, so the leak is SELF-LIMITING.** Rotation is **same-set over the FORMED set**: `PTX_Formation_SelectRotationMembers` (`ptx_formation.cpp:314`) resolves through the shared resolver and asserts `membersOut.size() == predecessor.members.size()` — **11, not the qualified 10** — so **gm98 is included in its own quorum's rotation**. At **h1830** (`d7d212e5` formed h390 + 1440) that quorum re-runs its ceremony **with gm98 offered a fresh completion chance**. **The built-in age-rotation IS the KDD-045 repair path**, already implemented. **(2) therefore shifts from "build active repair" to "CONFIRM rotation repairs it"** — a live, demonstrable question, not a design one. A completed-based predicate (release) and propagation-split prevention become **optional optimisations, not required fixes**. ★ **(1) KEY HYGIENE — SOUND, and the answer to "can a busy quorum serve forever on one key?" is NO.** The due-test is **purely age-based, use-independent**: `const int age = anchor->nHeight - rec.formation_height; if (age < params.nRotationInterval) continue;` — a busy quorum ages exactly as an idle one, and the code names this as what bounds KDD-045's key-compromise window. A **fairness floor** at `nRotationInterval + 2*nBoundaryInterval` (=1500) stops a persistently high-hash quorum starving past the interval via tie-break losses. `nRetireWindow=200` is a **separate, complementary** arm for *idle* quorums; age-rotation covers the busy ones. **This is proactive defensive re-key, not idle-only cleanup.** ★ **STALE COMMENT FLAGGED AND FIXED (own commit):** `ptx_formation.cpp:589` read *"the stub returns due=false today, so the else-arm below is the only reachable path"* — a **P-b6a-era note left behind after P-b6b landed the real policy**, four lines above the call to the fully-implemented `PTX_Formation_RotationDueAt`. It is a **future-wrong-analysis generator**: a reader trusting it concludes rotation is dead, which is exactly the wrong conclusion nearly drawn here. ★ **UNPROVEN, and the reason the fleet stays up:** all of the above is **code-and-config verified; rotation has NEVER FIRED on this fleet**, and it touches precisely the machinery W2.3 records as owed (ROTATING→SUPERSEDED repurpose, superseded-at-connect transition + undo, the rotation-validation arm). **h1740 is the FIRST live rotation** — a membership change transacted at a block with connect *and* undo paths, i.e. **exactly the transactional-state shape the BUG-023/024/025/026/027 family just closed**, and therefore a credible place for a NEW member of that family. **h1830 is gm98's self-heal test.** Both are being captured fleet-wide (agreement, wedge markers, partition), same discipline as the settlement boundaries.

> **★ STANDING DIAGNOSTIC (2026-08-02, third instance of the class — promoted from lesson to rule): a "violation" that is IDENTICAL on every node is a MEASUREMENT bug, not a consensus bug.** Real pose/consensus divergence is **per-node** and scales with that node's own history (BUG-027's signature: gm01's 48 reorgs → 88 tickets, while nodes with fewer reorgs held different values). So when a fleet-wide check fires with all N nodes reporting *the same numbers*, the fleet is agreeing with itself and the probe is what disagrees — check the probe first. ★ **The instance that earned the promotion:** the soak harness asserted `tickets == 11 * rolls`, which silently assumed every quorum completes 11/11. Against the 10/11 quorum of ODC-059 it screamed `POSE VIOLATION` on 106/106 nodes for four hours while the chain was perfectly correct — the "deficit" grew 1-per-roll and equalled that quorum's roll count exactly (17). ★ **Prior instances of the same class:** the `IS DETERMINISTIC GAMEMASTER` grep that could never match (the daemon emits two spaces), and the wait-loop that exited on *empty* output rather than on its sentinel. All three were probes indicting a healthy fleet. ★ **The structural fix, not just the patch:** invariants must be **roster-grounded** — derived from the system's own declared state (`records == Σ completed_size`) rather than from a hardcoded constant that encodes an assumption nobody stated — and every fleet-wide check should carry a **cross-node signature comparison** so agreement-vs-disagreement is visible before any threshold is applied. Both are now in `w2-fleet/bug027-proof/proof_bug027.py` with the rationale in its docstring.

> **★ ODC-060 (opened 2026-08-02) — THE LAN FORK BASELINE: races are SLOT COLLISIONS shadowed by first-seen-wins relay, NOT slow propagation. The Phase-4 reference numbers, measured before any latency is injected.** Measured from the full chain history of the 98-GM/8-caller fleet (h0–h1161, 106 nodes, 60s blocks, single host). ★ **THE SAFETY PICTURE — HEALTHY, nothing to fix:** fork rate **0.475/block** (536 raced heights; a race every ~2.1 blocks) but **100% depth-1** — 25,479 per-node reorg events, every single one depth 1, max 1; median reconvergence **one block (61s)**; **ZERO permanent forks** — at settle depth (tip−100) all 106 nodes agree on the canonical hash; orphans evenly distributed across producers (caller2–8: 183–202 each; no disadvantaged node). ★ **THE MECHANISM, established in three steps and one refutation.** **(1) Propagation is NOT slow — it is LOSSY-under-race.** The parent-aware measurement (catchup/reorg connects excluded) is **bimodal and quantized**: 61.6% of node-block deliveries arrive **<1s**; 25.3% arrive at almost exactly **+60s/+120s** — nodes that miss a relay wave get the block only when the NEXT block forces catch-up. The naive spread figure ("41s median") was a **diagnosis artifact** of averaging those two populations. **(2) The misses are RACE-SHADOWING:** 98.7% of lossy heights are raced vs a 48.8% base rate (clean heights: 5.7%) — first-seen-wins relay means a node that accepted the losing block never announces the competing winner, so the mesh behind it misses the wave. **(3) The races are 75% SLOT COLLISIONS, 25% STALE MINTS** (by block-timestamp comparison of loser vs winner, 40 raced heights sampled, 0 unretrievable): three competitors per raced height on average carry the *identical* timestamp — several stakers eligible in the same slot all mint — while the 25% stale-mint tail is the shadowing FEEDBACK (a node one block behind stakes on the stale tip), which sustains **RACE TRAINS**: 95 contiguous runs, mostly length 1–2, tails at 31–40, longest **86 consecutive raced heights**. **(Refuted: CPU contention — 32 cores, load 1.6, every container <1%.)** ★ **ODC-059 REFRAME, quantified:** the +61s anchor split that sidelined gm98 is the **ordinary interior of a race train**, not an outlier — the *condition* (a minority-vs-majority wave at a formation anchor) is common; only the full trigger (ceremony straddles it AND the early cohort is the 1-of-11) is rare. ODC-059's event-rate estimate rises accordingly. ★ **PHASE-4 GUIDANCE — what to hold constant and what to expect:** the reference numbers are **race rate 0.475/blk, shadow-delivery share 25.3%, train-length histogram (longest 86), relay-wave floor <1s** — netem does not primarily slow the wave, it **widens the race window and lengthens the trains** (the cascade is the amplifier), so measure Phase 4 against the train distribution, not the naive spread. A pre-latency LAN already spends a quarter of its deliveries one block late; that is the floor Phase 4 builds on. ★ **OPEN CONSIDERATION (the reason this is an ODC, not only a baseline):** should same-height competitors be announced (relay competing headers) to break the shadow, or is first-seen-wins kept lineage-faithful and the shadow accepted as the cost of slot-quantized PoS? Interacts with ODC-058 (stake concentration decides race winners — caller1 at 72% of canonical production) and with the ODC-059 event rate. No decision here. ★ **METHODOLOGY SELF-CATCHES, recorded because each briefly produced an alarming wrong number:** (a) "depth-86 forks" = contiguous raced runs merged into one event (true depth: 1; the runs are TRAINS); (b) "1 permanent fork" / "97 nodes off-canonical" = testing at the live tip instead of settle depth (measures the in-flight race); (c) "gm01 produced 885 blocks" = 1s log resolution + stable sort resolving ties alphabetically (GMs are wallet-less; true producer attribution needs the coinstake, which shows caller1 at 72.3%). All three caught by cross-check before any conclusion rested on them. Tooling: `w2-fleet/rotation-capture/fork_characterize.py` + `spread_clean.json`.

> **★ ODC-060 AMENDMENT (i) — 2026-08-02: THE MESH EXPERIMENT. ANSWER: (b) — the shadowing rate was ELEVATED BY THE SPARSE PRODUCER MESH, and is largely a config fix; the entry's "75% inherent collisions" was MIS-CLASSIFIED and is corrected here.** The re-open was correct: "lineage-faithful mechanism" did not establish the *rate* was healthy, and it wasn't. ★ **DESIGN.** Runtime-only (`addnode`, zero restarts — the rotation exhibit untouched): at h1220 caller degree was raised **7 → ~47** (full producer-producer mesh: 28/28 caller-caller edges, producer hop distance **1**, verified from `getpeerinfo`, not assumed) and GMs 34.5 → 42.7; post-window h1232–h1412 (181 blocks) against the sparse steady-state pre-window h1030–h1209 (180 blocks); mesh **restored to sparse at h1462** (addnode-remove + manual-flag disconnects only), preserving natural conditions for the h1740 rotation. The exposed asymmetry that shaped the design: **the producers were the sparsest nodes in the fleet** (callers 7 peers vs GMs 35–46) — wave-one of any caller's block reached 7 nodes. ★ **RESULT — every mesh-sensitive metric collapsed; the control held:** shadow share **34.4% → 5.1%** (÷7); race rate **0.739 → 0.166/blk** (÷4.5); stale-mint share **22% → 0%**; longest race train **40 → 2**. ★ **THE CAUSAL MECHANISM, measured directly, not inferred from the symptom** (read-only 3s telemetry, `w2-fleet/densify-telemetry/`): per-producer mint timing shows **zero blind mints in the dense phase** — 121 attributed canonical mints, every parent-arrival lag positive (min +0.7s) — and every residual loser is a **true same-slot collision** (winner_age negative: the competitor did not yet exist anywhere at mint). The feedback loop that builds trains — a shadowed producer minting against a block it has not seen — **did not fire once** under a dense producer mesh. ★ **CORRECTION to the parent entry's 75/25 split, now measured rather than argued:** the timestamp classifier conflated two populations, because **a shadowed producer hashes the same slot boundaries as everyone else** — its stale mint carries the *same timestamp* as the winner and is indistinguishable from an independent collision by block time alone.
