The dense mesh removed the stale-tip state and the rate fell to **~0.11–0.17/blk = the TRUE inherent slot-collision floor**; therefore **~78% of the sparse-mesh race rate was mesh-induced**, not 25%. ★ **CONSEQUENCES:** (1) the gm98/ODC-059 event-rate is **mesh-tunable** — a dense producer mesh shrinks both the anchor-split probability and the shadow width that strands a lone-early member; (2) **production topology guidance owed**: block producers must be densely inter-peered (full producer-producer mesh at minimum) — on this evidence that single config choice removes ~85% of shadowed deliveries and ~78% of races; (3) **Phase 4 should run netem against BOTH meshes** — sparse-producer (the pathological floor) and dense-producer (the recommended one) — since the trains netem amplifies are mesh-dependent; (4) the residual dense-mesh floor (~5% shadow, ~0.15 races/blk, trains ≤2) is the inherent cost of 60s-slot PoS + first-seen-wins and is the number PTX shadow-tolerance should be sized against. ★ **INSTRUMENT SELF-CATCH (fourth of the class, recorded):** the live sampler briefly manufactured a "+59s stale-mint cluster" — its `getblockcount`/`getbestblockhash` pair is not atomic, so a tip advance between the calls recorded losers one height low and compared them against the *previous* height's winner, fabricating exactly +60s. Caught by log reconciliation (the "h1389 losers" are h1390 same-slot competitors, block times identical); after correction the live and log instruments agree exactly. Same lesson as ever: an alarming number from one instrument earns a second instrument before it earns a conclusion. Tooling: `rotation-capture/shadow_window.py`, `densify-telemetry/telemetry.py`, `mesh_experiment.json`.

> **★ FIRST ROTATION TRAIN — 2026-08-03: THE W2.3-OWED ROTATION MACHINERY LIVE-VALIDATED ×8, THE IDLE-REFORM ARM FIRED FOR THE FIRST TIME, AND ODC-059 QUESTION (2) IS ANSWERED EMPIRICALLY: ROTATION HEALS.** Captured fleet-wide (pre-armed harness + 3s telemetry; exhibits in `w2-fleet/rotation-capture/`). ★ **THE TRAIN.** All 8 rotations fired exactly on the age schedule, staggered one per 30-block boundary, most-due-first: h1740 (eaa7b74a) → h1770 → h1800 → **h1830 (d7d212e5)** → h1860 → h1890 → h1920 (c142bf8f) → h1950 (7a75c9a1). Each: same-set ceremony over the FORMED 11 (`— 11 same-set members` on all 106 nodes), ~27 blocks to mine, successor connected ⇒ **predecessor `superseded` at exactly the successor's mined height** (1767/1797/1827/1857/1887/1917/1947/1977) — **KDD-063 handover-at-accept working as specified** — and **every successor carries a NEW group_pk** (diffed against the pre-state snapshot): the defensive re-key is real. 7 of 8 successors completed **11/11**. Throughout: chain live, zero wedge markers, tips reconverging, transient quorum-state disagreement only at set-change moments (≤1 min, propagation lag — e.g. 104-vs-2, then agreement). ★ **SCOPE, stated so it is not over-read: this validates the CONNECT arm** (rotation-validation V12 accepted 8 successors; superseded-at-connect transitioned 8 predecessors). **The UNDO arm — disconnect of a block carrying a supersession — is NOT specifically verified**: race-train reorgs during the window may have crossed supersession blocks, but no targeted check was run; that leg of W2.3 stays owed. ★ **ODC-059 (2) — ANSWERED LIVE: the leak SELF-HEALS at rotation, exactly as amendment (iv) predicted.** gm98 was re-offered in d7d212e5's same-set rotation (successor `81f7ec57`, form@1830), **completed it — 11/11, `in_qual: true` — and now holds a PoSe record (fleet pose records 87 → 88, `gm98_has_pose` on 106/106 nodes)**. The sideline window measured end-to-end: h390 abort → h1857 heal, ≈ 1467 blocks ≈ R+overhead. **Release-vs-repair is therefore settled by observation: the built-in rotation IS the repair; no predicate change needed.** The completed-based predicate remains an *optional* optimisation for shortening the window, decided with Phase 4's event-rate data. ★ **NEW — THE IDLE-REFORM ARM (nRetireWindow=200, W4-f) FIRED LIVE FOR THE FIRST TIME, unassisted.** c142bf8f's successor `06c56aab` (form@1920, **11/11**, connected 1947) accumulated **zero attributed rolls** for the retire window and was **`MarkReformed: quorum REFORMED at height 2130`**; the freed slots triggered a **replacement formation `389e32a` at h2130**. Retire → reform → replace ran end-to-end with no intervention. **Watch-item, not concluded:** why one healthy 11/11 quorum drew zero rolls in ~200 blocks (chance ≈ e^-5 at observed roll rates, or a selection bias) — unexplained, flagged for the Phase-3 load run where roll volume makes the distribution measurable. ★ **ODC-059 EVENT RATE — THREE MORE INSTANCES IN ONE EVENT, mechanism identical, and the victims are interesting:** `389e32a` completed **8/11**. The three non-qual members — **gm02, gm49, gm73 — are all caller-hub GMs** ({1,2,24,49,50,73,98}), and the h2130 anchor histogram shows exactly **96+7 nodes on time and 3 nodes 1–2 slots late**; each of the three logged its own `ceremony ABORTED (sub-threshold close)`. Anchor-split → alone-late → absent≠faulty → sub-threshold abort, verbatim gm98's mechanism, now ×3 — consistent with ODC-060's finding that anchor splits are the ordinary interior of race trains (the reform transition itself was racing). Sidelined count now **4 GMs across 2 events**; all heal at their quorums' rotations (`389e32a` due h3570). ★ **FIFTH INSTRUMENT SELF-CATCH (the fleet-identical tell, again):** the capture harness alarmed `ROSTER SHORTFALL on 106/106 nodes` — its invariant `pose_records == Σ completed_size(listed quorums)` silently assumed no supersessions and no heals; records persist for superseded-quorum members and for healed gm98, so the first rotation broke it by construction. Identical-on-all-nodes was, as codified, the probe indicting itself. Harness patched: the alarm is now **cross-node-disagreement-only**.

> **★ FIRST-ROTATION ADDENDUM (2026-08-03) — THE UNDO ARM WAS EXERCISED AFTER ALL, and the register's "owed" understated what the logs already proved.** The scope caveat in the entry above ("the UNDO arm is NOT specifically verified") was written before a log census; the census finds the arm has been firing throughout, on natural reorg churn: ★ **(1) PTXDKG-record undo — hundreds of firings, 106/106 nodes.** `UndoBlock: erased PTXDKG quorum record on disconnect` fired en masse at h536/h537 (~105 nodes each, the original 7th/8th formations) and h2157 (112 firings — the reform-replacement's block raced twice), plus scattered singles: depth-1 races replaced PTXDKG-carrying blocks, every node erased the record on disconnect and re-persisted the **identical commitment tx from the replacement block** (`ec3b9411…` re-included — KDD-058-A's replicated minable store is precisely why the loser's payload survives the loser). ★ **(2) Supersession-crossing undo — ~7 natural single-node events**, disconnects at h1767/1857/1887/1947/1977 — supersession-connect heights, where the disconnect must erase the successor AND unwind the predecessor's SUPERSEDED transition. All reconverged: current fleet state has all 8 supersessions in place and 106/106 agreement. ★ **CALIBRATION, stated so it is not over-read: SUBSTANTIALLY DISCHARGED, on natural occurrences — not force-tested.** No adversarial depth, no crafted supersession-straddling reorg, no crash-during-undo. The residual owed item narrows from "undo arm unverified" to "undo arm unstressed". ★ Also resolved in the same pass: the reform-replacement `389e32a` received its **first roll at ~h2194** (pose records 88→92, its four spare members' first records) — so its zero-roll start was slowness, not exclusion, and **`06c56aab`'s 200-block drought stands as the singleton** for the Phase-3 routing-distribution measurement.

> **★ INSTRUMENT-CLASS RECURRENCE (2026-08-03) — THE POOL-FORECAST PHANTOM, SECOND OCCURRENCE, ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED READ-AUTHORITATIVE.** The fleet dashboard again showed `pool -1 / in-flight 1` against a chain-truth pool of 10 (verified two ways: `ptx_debug_selectquorum` → `eligible=98 active_excluded=88 pool=10`, and Σformed=88 over the 8 listed quorums). ★ **The class, now demonstrated twice:** deriving pool occupancy from ceremony-event arithmetic silently assumes a lifecycle invariant that the next lifecycle feature breaks. First occurrence: `started−active` assumed ceremonies are monotonic — broken by the W4-f idle-reform returning 11 GMs to the pool. The repaired form `started−done` assumed **every ceremony emits 11 DONE lines** — broken by ODC-059 itself: degraded completions emit fewer (`d7d212e5` 10, `389e32a` 8), so `done//11` undercounts and the phantom returned. A third lifecycle variant would break a third derivation. ★ **FIX (deployed, verified rendering `pool 10 / in-flight 0` against live chain):** the pool is no longer derived — it is **READ from the authoritative computation the daemon itself forms with** (`ptx_debug_selectquorum` at the cycle anchor), rendering `?` on RPC failure rather than ever guessing; in-flight now uses `STARTED − EXITED`, where `ceremony session EXITED` is emitted by the **single teardown epilogue every exit path funnels through** (DONE, ABORT, interrupt alike), so it is invariant to aborts and degraded completions **by construction, not by assumption**. Rationale in the dashboard source. The general rule joins the instrument lessons: **display state that the chain computes must be read from the chain's computation, never re-derived from event arithmetic.**

> **★ ODC-061 (opened 2026-08-03) — ROLL ANCHORING UNDER REORGS: the #1 feature's correctness surface, audited source + measured on 537 live rolls. VERDICT: results are reorg-immune by construction and no roll has ever been lost; the TWO open gaps are FINALITY COMMUNICATION and the DEEP-FORK UNRECOGNIZED-QUORUM case.** ★ **Q1 — THE SEED IS SELF-CONTAINED; a reorg can never change a roll's RESULT.** `round_seed = SHA256(game_id || block_height_LE4 || caller_pubkey || nonce || params_hash)` (KDD-002, `ptx_seed.h:17`) — the anchoring block's **height** is an input at creation; its **hash never enters**. The seed and results ride in the payload (seed also in the OP_RETURN vout), and consensus (`specialtx_validation.cpp:940-996`) verifies the BLS threshold signature **over the carried seed** against the quorum's group_pk — nothing is recomputed from chain context. A reorg can therefore relocate a PTXSESS or (deep case, below) orphan it; it cannot alter its outcome. Depth-1 races at the same height leave even the height input identical. ★ **Q2 — RE-ANCHORING WORKS, proven 537/537:** every `ok` receipt in the demand driver's log has its txid **on the final chain — 0 lost, 0 stuck** — across a history of ~1,000 raced heights and mass block replacements (h536/537/2157). 534/537 anchored next block; the 3 outliers (seq 15: +22 blocks through the h387 churn; seq 90: +12; seq 507: +16) are **observed resurrect-and-re-anchor survivors**, valid at their late heights because ★ **validation imposes NO freshness bound: `nSeedHeight` is checked only ≠0, and `nExpiryHeight` is set by the RPC but consumed by NOTHING — a dead field** (flag: either wire an expiry or delete it; an unbounded re-anchor window is currently load-bearing for liveness and should be a decision, not an accident). ★ **Q3 — THE DANGEROUS CASE IS REAL IN SOURCE, UNREACHABLE IN THIS REGIME, AND NAMED FOR PHASE 4.** PTXSESS validation resolves the signer via `GetQuorumRecord(payload.quorum_hash)` → **`ptx-unknown-quorum` reject** if the validating chain lacks the record. `quorum_hash` IS the formation-anchor block hash, so a fork deep enough to orphan a serving quorum's **anchor** (> mined−anchor ≈ 27+ blocks, or any partition that forms a quorum on the minority side) produces rolls that are **permanently invalid on the winning fork — the roll is LOST while the caller holds a receipt**. Unreachable here: measured max reorg depth is 1 and anchors are ≥27 blocks settled before serving; the shallow case is additionally defended by KDD-058-A replication (h2157 measured: record erased on disconnect, re-persisted from the replacement block's identical commitment tx). **Phase 4 must carry "partition across a formation boundary → minority-side quorum's rolls die at reconvergence" as a named case** alongside ODC-058/ODC-059's; mitigation options (post-reconvergence re-roll protocol; caller-side receipt validation against finalized anchors) are design work, not bugs. ★ **Q4 — FINALITY IS NOT COMMUNICATED, and the receipt does not mean what a caller would assume.** `ptx_roll` returns synchronously with `tx_id` + `block_height` — but `block_height` is the **SEED input height, not the anchor** (the tx is typically unmined at return, anchoring next block), there is **no confirmations/anchor field, and the API reference contains no finality language at all**. At 0.475 races/block a caller treating the receipt as final is exposed — though only in the narrow sense established above: the **result cannot change and the tx re-anchors**; what is unstable is the caller's belief about *where/when* it anchored, and (deep-fork case only) *whether* it anchored. **Owed: document the finality semantic (provisional-until-N; N chosen against the measured race profile, e.g. settle-depth), add `anchor_height`/`confirmations` to the RPC or a `ptx_roll_status` query, and decide the deep-fork posture — before external callers integrate.** ★ Incidental data: per-quorum roll routing over the receipt set (predecessors 44–67; successors 6–18 in ~250 blocks; `06c56aab` 0-in-200 remains the outlier) — feeds the Phase-3 routing-distribution measurement.

> **★ ODC-062 (opened 2026-08-03) — FORMATION-BOUNDARY / ORPHANED-QUORUM ROLL EXPOSURE: the loss is IRREVERSIBLE, the mitigation is PREVENTION + HONESTY, and the serve-depth gate that would close the non-partition case DOES NOT currently exist.** Sharpens ODC-061's Q3 into a decision item. ★ **ROOT, restated precisely:** the roll *result* is block-independent (safe, ODC-061 Q1) and the signing quorum is confirmed-history-derived (safe in normal operation) — but if a fork orphans a quorum's **formation** (deep reorg) or a **partition forms a whole quorum on the minority side**, the winning fork never contained that quorum: `GetQuorumRecord(quorum_hash)` fails, every roll it signed is `ptx-unknown-quorum` **forever**, and the caller holds an un-anchorable receipt. ★ **NO SELF-CORRECTION EXISTS, and none is possible for the roll itself:** the chain reconverges, but the result was deterministic in an orphaned quorum's ceremony material (its group key exists nowhere on the winning fork); no other quorum can reproduce or re-sign it, so the roll can neither be re-served-identically nor re-anchored. **Irreversible from the caller's side** — which is why the mitigation posture is prevention + honesty, not correction. ★ **(A) SERVE-DEPTH GATE — CHECKED IN SOURCE: it does NOT hold.** `GetActiveQuorumsAtHeight` (`ptx_quorum_store.cpp:354`) admits every ACTIVE record with `minedHeight ≤ nHeight` — **no maturity filter** — and `ptx_roll` selects at the live tip, so **a quorum is servable the instant its PTXDKG record is 1 block deep**. The formation *anchor* is ~27 blocks settled at first-servability (ceremony length ≈ M), which is what makes the anchor-orphan case need a >27 reorg; but the **record's own block at depth 1 is inside ordinary race range** — h2157 measured 112 record-erase undos on exactly such a replacement, and the 537/537 survival owes to KDD-058-A re-mining the identical commitment at the same height, not to any gate. **Adding the gate closes the deep-reorg leg AND the record-churn window.** ★ **IMPLEMENTATION CONSTRAINT (so the fix lands in the right layer):** `GetActiveQuorumsAtHeight` feeds six consumers **including consensus paths** (V5 reconstruction, the connect guard, the formation pool) — a maturity filter there would change consensus. The gate belongs **only in the signing-ctx path** (`PTX_LoadDKGSigningCtx`, `rpc/ptx.cpp:114`, documented "NO CONSENSUS SURFACE"): select from quorums with `minedHeight ≤ tip − K`, coordinator-side, unit-testable, zero consensus delta. K should ride the same N as ODC-063's finality answer. ★ **(B) honesty = ODC-063's finality semantic** (anchor provisional until N), so callers of *young-quorum* rolls know their exposure. ★ **THE PARTITION CASE IS IRREDUCIBLE by depth** — a minority-fork quorum is locally confirmation-deep and serves in good faith; no gate helps. This welds ODC-062 to **ODC-058**: a partition on this topology now has TWO costed failure modes — stake-fragmentation halt AND permanently-lost rolls — and both belong to the **same Phase-4 named case** ("partition across a formation boundary"), whose mitigation must be designed before partitions run. Candidate postures for the partition leg (design work, not chosen here): minority-side serve-freeze on partition detection (ties to ODC-058's liveness floor), post-reconvergence caller notification protocol, or accepting the loss with the receipt-verification RPC returning a definitive "orphaned" status.

> **★ ODC-063 (opened 2026-08-03) — PRODUCT/API: THE FINALITY SEMANTIC — "RESULT FINAL IMMEDIATELY, ANCHOR PROVISIONAL UNTIL N".** The correct contract, derivable from ODC-061's findings, currently communicated NOWHERE. ★ **The split-finality fact:** a roll's **result** is final at the moment `ptx_roll` returns — block-independent seed, threshold-signed, immutable (ODC-061 Q1) — but its **anchor reference** is provisional: the tx is typically unmined at return (`block_height` in the response is the SEED height, not an anchor), anchors next-block 99.4% of the time, re-anchors freely otherwise (no expiry), and in the ODC-062 fork cases can be lost entirely. The API reference contains no finality language; there is no confirmations or anchor field; a caller cannot even ask. ★ **OWED (product/API work, before external callers integrate):** **(1)** `ptx_roll` response gains explicit semantic fields — `result_final: true`, `anchor_status: "pending"`, and documentation of provisional-until-N; **(2)** a settled-anchor query (`ptx_getroll`/`ptx_verifyroll` returning the SETTLED anchor height/txid + `finality: pending|final|orphaned`) so high-stakes callers can wait for finality and low-stakes callers can proceed on the immediately-final result — **reorg-safety chosen per use, by the caller**; the `orphaned` arm is ODC-062's honesty channel. **(3) DESIGN QUESTION — what is N?** Inputs on record: measured LAN race profile (100% depth-1, reconverge ≈1 block → N=6 is generous *here*); the settlement window (60) as the system's own settle-depth precedent; Phase-4 latency results will bound the adversarial-free deep-reorg tail — **choose N after Phase 4, from measured depth distribution under latency, not from lineage convention**; and K (ODC-062's serve-depth gate) should equal N so "quorum old enough to serve" and "anchor old enough to trust" are one number with one justification.

**BUG-028 (2026-08-05). ★ THE DKG SHARE DESTROYED BY DISCONNECT/RECONNECT OF THE SAME BLOCK — a quorum reports `formed=11 completed=11 ACTIVE` while every member holds no key material. Found by soak forensics over a 4-day unattended run; SILENT — the health indicator is actively false about signing capability.** **SYMPTOM.** **62 of 1565** rolls failed (3.96%), every one with `PTX: BLS threshold not met: got N/6` (`rpc/ptx.cpp:317-320`), distribution **`0/6` ×46 (74%), `1/6` ×9, `2/6` ×7** — never above 2, i.e. near-total fan-out failure rather than a margin shortfall. Uniform across all 8 callers (1.5–6.7%), bursty rather than load-correlated. **MECHANISM.** On block DISCONNECT, `ptx_quorum_store.cpp:337` calls `PTX_BLS_UndoPromote`, whose contract (`ptx_bls.h:212-214`) is that the reverted `CURRENT(successor_qh)` is **DISCARDED**. On RECONNECT, `ptx_quorum_store.cpp:283` calls `PTX_BLS_Promote`, whose contract (`ptx_bls.h:184-188`) is `PENDING(successor) → CURRENT` and which is a documented **NO-OP** when no PENDING share exists. Shares are written **only** at ceremony FINALIZE (`ptx_bls.cpp:44-48`: *"the share is written only on local ceremony COMPLETION … a failed/aborted formation leaves the key unset"*), and the ceremony has already EXITED — so nothing repopulates PENDING. ★ **THE UNDO IS ASYMMETRIC: the chain record is reversible (erase ⇄ re-persist), the share destruction is not.** **CONSEQUENCE.** The quorum returns ACTIVE with `formed=11 completed=11` and continues to be routed rolls, while every member permanently holds nothing. **EVIDENCE.** Traced h5044 in `caller7.log:50701-50712` — `signers=11`, all 11 `FanOutSign` calls `HTTP 500`. gm01 had stored the share at 05:37:02 (`StoreSkShare`, `ceremony DONE … completed=11`); block 4947 was then disconnected and re-persisted repeatedly 05:39–05:45. Confirmed by **read-only live probe** of gm01: `gm_bls_sign(seed, 2087aac1…)` → *"this node holds no CURRENT sk_share for quorum 2087aac1…"*. ★ **NOT INFERRED FROM LOG ABSENCE:** `gm_bls_sign`'s error branch has no `LogPrintf` (only the success path logs, `rpc/ptx.cpp:555`), so silence at 07:15 proved nothing — hence the live probe (see ODC-064). **HYPOTHESES FALSIFIED, each by specific evidence:** degraded `completed=6` quorums (the traced failure had `signers=11` and `0/6`, not `5/6`); rotation supersede (no `MarkSuperseded` and no `predecessor=2087aac1` before 07:15); ceremony never completed (`StoreSkShare` + `DONE completed=11` at 05:37); transport/timeout (HTTP 500 is a *served* response, the error is chain-level and uniform). **FIX OWNER: PTX** — must tolerate disconnect-then-immediate-reconnect of the same block; reducing reorg rate lowers exposure but does not remove it. ★ **NO STANDALONE PATCH IS SCOPED YET, deliberately.** `UndoPromote`'s destructiveness is documented as *intended* P4 behaviour, and `ptx_bls.h:212-218` states it is shaped to COMPOSE with a record-side revert that is itself **owed** — *"owed to whichever of KDD-063 / W2.4 T-H lands first"*. The fix therefore likely lands **with** that owed work rather than as an independent change, and **which one it composes with is undetermined**. **DESIGN GATE before any implementation:** read the `ptx_bls.h` rationale first — the discard may exist for a reason a naive retention fix would violate. **EVIDENCE CAPTURED:** `w2-fleet/soak-forensics-20260805/` (410M) — 106 node `debug.log`s, `demand-N98.jsonl` (1565 roll records), `split-history.jsonl`, `fleet_watch.out`, driver logs, `rpc/live-state.json` (tip 5777). **Cross-ref** KDD-081 (the -070 reorg-safety falsification), KDD-082 (the general principle), ODC-064 (why this needed a live probe), ODC-060 (the reorg churn that triggers it), ODC-056/BUG-026 (the in-memory-state family; distinct trigger — failed connect vs successful disconnect). ★ **FIXED + CLOSED 2026-08-05 @ `56c938a`** (append-only amendment; the entry above stands as recorded). ★ **THE DEFECT, RESTATED AS THE ASYMMETRY — a better statement than the original mechanism paragraph:** a promotion has two halves, and only one was reversible. The **predecessor** goes `SUPERSEDED_RETAINED → CURRENT` on undo, **retained 120 blocks on a reorg-DEPTH clock** (`promotion_height`), **restorable**. The **successor** was **ERASED** — **no retention, no clock, NOT restorable**. That asymmetry *is* BUG-028. **THE ERASE WAS NEVER JUSTIFIED BY THE PROMOTION:** the promotion did not *create* the successor's share — it pre-existed as `PENDING`, stored at ceremony FINALIZE (`ptx_dkg.cpp:1436`, `:1566-1567`) — so the erase destroyed material the promotion never brought into being, which is why no redo could reconstruct it. The documented *"is its inverse"* property (`ptx_bls.h:214-215`) was therefore **violated by the erase, not protected by it**: the inverse of `PENDING → CURRENT` is `CURRENT → (retained, re-promotable)`, not deletion. ★ **THE DESIGN GATE'S DECIDING ARGUMENT — the clock, not the value.** The obvious fix (re-base `PTX_PENDING_TTL_BLOCKS`, 8 → 120) is **impossible at any value**: the TTL expires on `tip_height - formation_height` (`ptx_bls.cpp:230`), whereas reorg survival must be measured **from the UNDO**. A share formed h4920 and undone h5100 is already 180 blocks old when protection must begin, so even TTL=120 expires it instantly. A wrong *basis* cannot be corrected by a *number*. The TTL was also answering a different question entirely (FINALIZE→successor-connect latency, `ptx_bls.h:168-174`, explicitly PROVISIONAL with measurement OWED) and re-basing it would have destroyed that hygiene function and orphaned the owed measurement. **REORG-DEPTH TARGET = the CONSENSUS bound, not a topology measurement:** `DEFAULT_MAX_REORG_DEPTH` (100, enforced `validation.cpp:2960` — deeper forks are rejected) + `PTX_SUPERSEDED_REORG_MARGIN` (20) = **120**, the constant the predecessor half *already* used. Sizing instead to ODC-060's measured train lengths (40 pre-mesh, 2 post-mesh) would have baked a production-topology assumption into key retention. **THE FIX:** new role `PTXShareRole::UNDONE_RETAINED` (=3); `UndoPromote` demotes the successor and stamps the **undo height** as its retention clock instead of erasing; `Promote` accepts `PENDING || UNDONE_RETAINED` (the redo path — see **KDD-083**); `PTX_BLS_DiscardUndone` mirrors `DiscardSuperseded` on the same basis and the same constant. Both halves of a promotion now move together — if 20 is ever wrong, it is wrong for both. Flat 41-byte wire form unchanged (the role byte is not range-checked on decode, `ptx_bls.cpp:140`). **KNOWN LIMIT, pinned in test and header rather than left implicit:** `-maxreorg` is a runtime option, so a node started above 120 permits reorgs deeper than either retained role is kept for — the basis is the DEFAULT permitted depth plus margin, not a guarantee against an operator-widened window. ★ **CORROBORATION (independently supports the mechanism):** retirement machinery existed but fired **~200 blocks late** — `MarkReformed` at h5160 against failures from h5044 = **~116 blocks of exposure per death**, which at the observed roll cadence is **≈1 roll in 8**, matching the observed **12–14% failure bursts**. The arithmetic reached the observed burst rate from the mechanism alone, without fitting. **VERIFICATION:** suite **424 cases, 0 skipped, 0 failures**; every claim RED-proven before green — the redo (`Promote` returned `0 != 1`), durability (stubbing the successor-persist reloads a stale `CURRENT`, i.e. **wrongly signable** after restart), and the observability regression (see **ODC-065**). **Cross-ref** KDD-083 (the `Promote` contract amendment), ODC-065 (the two transferable verification lessons this fix exposed).

**KDD-081 (2026-08-05) — AMENDMENT TO KDD-070: SLOT RETENTION IS NOT REORG-SAFE FOR DISCONNECT/RECONNECT. Amends, does not replace; -070 stands as recorded (append-only).** KDD-070 records the slot mechanism as providing **reorg-safe retention**. `PTX_BLS_UndoPromote` is documented (`ptx_bls.h:212-214`) as **intentionally discarding** `CURRENT` on revert. **Both statements are individually correct; in composition they are wrong.** A reorg that disconnects and immediately reconnects the *same* block destroys the share irrecoverably, because the only write path (ceremony FINALIZE, `ptx_bls.cpp:44-48`) has exited and `PTX_BLS_Promote` no-ops without a PENDING share. KDD-070's reorg-safety claim **does not hold for the disconnect/reconnect case**. **FALSIFIED BY LIVE EVIDENCE, not by argument** — BUG-028. **REGISTER NOTE, stated precisely to avoid overclaiming novelty:** **ODC-060 measured the rate and mechanism; the structural reason ties are possible at all was unnamed.** ODC-060 and its amendment (i) already characterise slot collisions, race rate, first-seen-wins shadowing and race trains in depth. What was absent is the structural cause — see the ODC-060 amendment (ii) below. What was genuinely believed-covered-and-was-not is -070's reorg safety.

**KDD-082 (2026-08-05) — IRREVERSIBLE STATE MUST NOT HANG OFF REVERSIBLE SUBSTRATE. The generalisation of BUG-028, given its own row for FINDABILITY: a future author adding block-keyed PTX state has no reason to read KDD-070's amendment history, and this constraint's whole value is that it applies to state which does not exist yet.** Base-chain reorg churn is normal and survivable **for base-chain state**, which is fully reversible by construction. PTX derives cryptographic key material from chain events and holds it in a store with **no reconstruction path**. ★ **CONSTRAINT: any PTX state derived from a chain event must either be RECONSTRUCTIBLE from chain data alone, or be RETAINED across disconnect/reconnect of the originating block.** Neither holds for the DKG sk-share today: it is reconstructible only by re-running a ceremony (which the formation driver will not do for an already-mined quorum), and it is explicitly discarded on undo. Applies beyond this defect — to any future PTX state keyed on block events. **Cross-ref** KDD-081, BUG-028, ODC-056 (the in-memory sibling: state not derived purely from the chain).

**KDD-083 (2026-08-05) — THE PROMOTABLE SET INCLUDES `UNDONE_RETAINED`. Amends the `PTX_BLS_Promote` key-isolation contract (`ptx_bls.h:184-188`); landed with the BUG-028 fix @ `56c938a`.** **CONTEXT.** `Promote`'s stated guarantee was: *"if there is no PENDING share for `successor_qh`, it is a NO-OP (returns 0) — a connect for quorum Y never promotes PENDING(X)."* BUG-028 requires a reorg's **re-connect** to promote a share that is no longer `PENDING`, so the stated set had to widen. **DECISION.** The promotable set becomes **`PENDING` or `UNDONE_RETAINED`**. `PENDING` is the first promotion (the rotation successor from ceremony FINALIZE); `UNDONE_RETAINED` is the **REDO** — a reorg that disconnected a PTXDKG block and then re-applied it. ★ **WHY KEY ISOLATION SURVIVES INTACT — stated in full so no reader has to rederive it:** the guarantee is keyed on **`quorum_hash`, not on role**. The lookup is `find(successor_qh)`, and **only that key is ever mutated**. The amendment widens **which ROLES at that key are promotable**; it does **not** widen **which KEYS are reachable**. A connect for quorum Y still cannot touch quorum X's share — for exactly the same reason it never could, which is the `find`, not the role check. If there is no `PENDING`/`UNDONE_RETAINED` share for `successor_qh`, it remains a clean NO-OP returning 0. **CONSEQUENCES.** (1) `UndoPromote` becomes a **total inverse** rather than a one-way door, which is what its own contract already claimed (`ptx_bls.h:214-215`). (2) ★ **IDEMPOTENCY IS PRESERVED BUT NOW RESTS ON A DIFFERENT FACT — also stated in full:** previously a second `UndoPromote` call was a no-op *because the successor had been erased*; now the successor is present but its role is `UNDONE_RETAINED`, so the **successor-must-be-`CURRENT` guard** rejects the second call. Same outcome, different mechanism — a reader who assumes the old reason will misread the guard. (3) Neither retained role is **signable**: `PTX_BLS_GetCurrentShare` requires `CURRENT`, so retention never becomes a second signing path for a quorum the active chain no longer activates. (4) Retention is bounded by the **same constant** as `SUPERSEDED_RETAINED`, with the `-maxreorg` limit recorded at both the header and the test. **Cross-ref** BUG-028 (the defect this fixes), KDD-081/KDD-082 (why the erase was wrong in principle), ODC-065.

> **★ ODC-060 AMENDMENT (ii) — 2026-08-05: THE STRUCTURAL CAUSE OF EQUAL-WORK TIES, and a SUPERSEDED FINDING recorded because it was nearly acted on.** The parent entry and amendment (i) measured the race rate and its dominant driver without naming **why ties are possible at all**. ★ **TIES ARE GUARANTEED, NOT INCIDENTAL.** `GetBlockProof` (`pow.cpp:141`) derives work **solely from `nBits`**; two blocks at the same height share a difficulty target, so their chainwork is **bit-identical by construction**. `CBlockIndexWorkComparator` (`validation.cpp:126-145`) then falls through to `nSequenceId` — **arrival order, node-local and non-deterministic** — and finally to pointer address. Each equal-work candidate re-enters `setBlockIndexCandidates` and can displace the current tip, producing disconnect→reconnect churn. ★ **NO PTX CODE PARTICIPATES IN TIP SELECTION** — this is stock Bitcoin/PIVX fork-choice machinery; PTX is downstream of it, not a cause. Different fix owner from anything PTX-side. ★ **CONSISTENT WITH AMENDMENT (i):** the dominant term in the observed contested rate is **producer mesh sparsity (~78%)**, measured by raising mesh degree 7 → ~47 (race rate 0.739 → 0.166/blk, shadow share 34.4% → 5.1%, stale mints 22% → 0%). The structural tie mechanism above is **why the residual floor (~0.11–0.17/blk) exists and cannot reach zero**. ★ **SUPERSEDED FINDING, recorded because the wrong conclusion was briefly held and nearly staged as a config change:** a 2026-08-05 pass measured **0s block-timestamp spread across all 1399 contested heights** on gm01 and concluded the collisions were simultaneous and propagation was not implicated. **Amendment (i) refutes that classifier** — *a shadowed producer hashes the same slot boundaries as the winner, so its stale mint carries the identical timestamp and is indistinguishable from an independent collision by block time alone.* The timestamp test **cannot discriminate**; only the mesh experiment could, and it had already been run. Sixth of the instrument-self-catch class, and the first where the refuting evidence was **already in the register** — the lesson is to search the register before re-deriving a measurement.

> **★ ODC-064 (opened 2026-08-05) — OBSERVABILITY GAP: THE FAILURE CAUSE WAS UNRECOVERABLE FROM FOUR DAYS OF FLEET LOGS.** BUG-028's actual cause could not be recovered from any captured log. **Two sites:** (1) `ptx_fanout.cpp:317-319` logs only the HTTP status and **discards `response.body`**, so the server's error text — which names the exact condition — was never recorded anywhere; (2) `gm_bls_sign`'s failure branch has **no `LogPrintf`** (`rpc/ptx.cpp:555` logs the success path only). Diagnosis therefore required a **live read-only probe of a running node**; had the fleet been rebuilt first, the cause would have been **unrecoverable**. ★ Same family as the GMAUTH `BCLog::NET_GM` lesson and the vacuous-check guard: **a branch that produces no output cannot be reasoned about from its silence** — absence of a log line is not evidence a branch did not fire. **OWED:** log `response.body` at the fan-out site; log the failure branch in `gm_bls_sign`. Both are small and neither is consensus-affecting. **Cross-ref** BUG-028 (the diagnosis this nearly blocked), the fleet-identical measurement-bug lesson. ★ **CLOSED 2026-08-06** (append-only; entry above stands as recorded). Both owed sites landed, logging-only, no behaviour change. **(1) FAN-OUT** (`ptx_fanout.cpp`, the `FanOutSign` non-200 arm): now logs `body=` alongside the status, **trimmed to 200 chars** with newlines/tabs flattened to spaces so a broken or hostile peer can neither flood the log nor forge extra log lines; over-length bodies are marked `…[truncated]`. **(2) `gm_bls_sign`** (`rpc/ptx.cpp`): BOTH failure arms — no-CURRENT-share, and BLS-partial-sign-failed — now emit `PTX gm_bls_sign: FAILED node=… quorum=… reason=…` before throwing. The no-share arm is the exact line whose absence forced the live probe during BUG-028's diagnosis. ★ **A THIRD non-200 ARM WAS FOUND AND DELIBERATELY LEFT ALONE:** the generic RPC helper's `if (response.status != 200) return result;` logs nothing, but it assigns `result.body = response.body` on the preceding line, so the body is **PROPAGATED to the caller rather than discarded** — a different situation from the FanOutSign arm, and not a defect. Recorded so it is not "fixed" by a future sweep that pattern-matches on the absence of a log. **VERIFICATION:** suite **428, 0 skipped, 0 failures**; `ODC064_FailureBranchesLogTheirCause` RED-proven (4 failures before the fix). ★ **HONEST LIMIT OF THAT TEST:** it asserts the failure branches CONTAIN the logging with the required fields; it does NOT prove the line fires at runtime, because this codebase has no log-capture harness and building one is a larger change than the logging it would verify. Stated rather than glossed. ★ **AN ANTI-VACUITY CATCH WORTH KEEPING:** the first version of that test bounded its search window loosely and matched the *generic helper's* arm, then passed one sub-check on the SUCCESS path's own `res.read(response.body)` — i.e. it would have gone green without the fix. Caught by reading WHICH sub-checks passed in the RED run, not merely that the test was red. **A red test is not automatically a correct test.** **Cross-ref** ODC-065 (observability changes get verified like behaviour changes — the argument this closure honours), the vacuous-check guard.

> **★ ODC-065 (opened 2026-08-05) — VERIFICATION ARTEFACTS THAT ACTIVELY ASSERT HEALTH WHILE THE SYSTEM IS WRONG. Two instances, both surfaced by the BUG-028 fix, both transferable, neither anticipated in the plan.** **WHY ONE ROW AND NOT TWO:** they are the same failure shape at two points in the loop — an artefact whose *job* is to detect a defect instead **testifies that the defect is correct behaviour** (before the fix) or **falls silent about it** (after the fix). Splitting them buries the shape; the register's own findability principle (see KDD-082's rationale for its standalone row) argues for one entry naming the shape, cross-referenced from the BUG. **INSTANCE 1 — THE DEFECT WAS NOT UNTESTED; IT WAS TESTED AND AFFIRMED.** Three existing cases asserted the erase as *correct*: `P4_UndoRevert_RestoresPred_DiscardsSucc_ClearsHeight` (`BOOST_CHECK(!P3_held_role(succ, rs)); // successor discarded`), `P4_Revert_SurvivesLoadShares` (asserted the successor was gone from **disk**), and `P4_MultiBlockDisconnect_ReversesEachKeyedPromotion` (asserted it twice more). ★ **This is a sharper case than the usual "the unit tests were blind."** A blind spot is an absence; here the suite made a positive, confident, green assertion that the destructive behaviour was intended — and it had done so continuously since P4 landed. **The defect was found by READING THE SOURCE CONTRACT, and the test suite actively argued against the finding.** Green tests are evidence that behaviour matches *what someone wrote down*, never that what they wrote down is right; when a contract and a test agree, they can be wrong **together**, and the agreement is not corroboration because the test was written from the contract. **CONSEQUENCE FOR METHOD:** when a defect is suspected in behaviour that existing tests cover and pass, the tests are **part of the thing under suspicion**, not the arbiter of it — and amending them must be **visible in the diff**, never a silent assertion flip. **INSTANCE 2 — A CORRECT FIX CREATED A NEW BLIND SPOT IN EXISTING OBSERVABILITY.** `PTX_WarnMissingSharesForNode` (`ptx_quorum_store.cpp:883`) tested `PTX_BLS_HeldQuorumHashes()` — **held, ANY role**. Before the fix, a node stranded by a reorg held **nothing**, so the warning fired correctly. **After** the fix it holds an `UNDONE_RETAINED` share, which is **held but unsignable** — so a held-any test would have reported the node **healthy** while its quorum was short a signer, going silent on **precisely the state BUG-028 is about**. ★ **The fix was correct and would have degraded the fleet's ability to observe its own failure mode.** Root confusion: **HELD ≠ SIGNABLE**. The warning's stated meaning is signing capacity (*"the quorum may fall below threshold"*), and signing requires role `CURRENT` (`PTX_BLS_GetCurrentShare`); `PENDING`, `SUPERSEDED_RETAINED` and `UNDONE_RETAINED` are all held and all unsignable. **RESOLVED** by scoping the check to `PTX_BLS_HeldCurrentQuorumHashes()` — one line, RED-proven (held-any returns `0 != 1` on a stranded node). This is why the selection-side safety net was **load-bearing rather than decorative**, though it remains explicitly **not** the fix. **CONSEQUENCE FOR METHOD: when a fix changes the STATES a system can be in, audit every existing health check whose predicate ranges over those states — a new state can satisfy an old predicate for the wrong reason.** **Cross-ref** BUG-028, KDD-083, ODC-064 (the sibling observability gap — there a branch produced no output; here an artefact produced *confidently wrong* output, which is worse because it is trusted), the vacuous-check guard, the fleet-identical measurement-bug lesson.

**BUG-029 (2026-08-06). ★ THE VerifyDB DRY-RUN WALK MUTATES THE SHARE STORE AND THE POSE FILE — BUG-023's mechanism, still live on two globals its fix did not cover. Found by SOURCE READING during a recon that expected to find BUG-023 open; BUG-023 was already fixed, and the recon found its siblings instead.** **WALK TRACE.** `CVerifyDB::VerifyDB` at `nCheckLevel>=3` (`validation.cpp:3775+`) runs the REAL `DisconnectBlock` (`:1346`) over the last `-checkblocks` blocks (default **6**, `validation.h:78`, at default `-checklevel=3`, `:79`) -> `UndoSpecialTxsInBlock` (`specialtx_validation.cpp:1571`) -> `ptxQuorumStore->UndoBlock` (`:1625`) -> `PTX_BLS_UndoPromote` (`ptx_quorum_store.cpp:337`) => **mutates `g_ptx_my_shares`**. **TWO SANDBOXES PROTECT THE WALK AND THE SHARE STORE IS OUTSIDE BOTH:** the throwaway `CCoinsViewCache`, and the evoDb rollback transaction at `validation.cpp:3739` — the latter because `PTX_BLS_PersistShare` (`ptx_bls.cpp:355-361`) writes through `evoDb.GetRawDB().Write(...)`, and `GetRawDB()` (`evo/evodb.h:89`) returns the underlying `CDBWrapper`, **bypassing the `CDBTransaction`** that `BeginTransaction()` (`:49`) installs. Level 3 never reconnects, so nothing promotes the share back. ★ **RECORDS REVERT, SHARES DON'T — the asymmetry is the defect.** Two lines earlier in the same function the record side uses the *transactional* `evoDb.Erase(...)` (`ptx_quorum_store.cpp:322-323`) and IS rolled back. **NET:** a node restarting with a **rotation** PTXDKG block inside the 6-block window has its share left `UNDONE_RETAINED` **in memory AND on disk** — so it cannot sign, restart cannot heal it, and every restart re-runs the walk. ★ **SECOND GLOBAL, SAME WALK — POSE.** `UndoSpecialTxsInBlock` also restores pose (BUG-027's fix, `98c747e`, **2026-08-01**) via `g_ptx_pose_tracker.RestoreRecords(...)`, which **calls `Save()`** (`ptx_pose.cpp:132-141`) and so regresses the flat file `ptx_pose.dat`, not merely memory. BUG-023's sentry was written **2026-07-30** (`a6ee1ab`) for `LotteryState` alone and was **not widened when BUG-027 added pose to the walk two days later**. ★ **A correct fix silently widened a neighbouring guard's blind spot — ODC-065's pattern, third instance.** ★ **56c938a CHANGED THE SEVERITY, NOT THE DEFECT:** before BUG-028 this path called `GetRawDB().Erase(...)` — also raw, also unsandboxed — so a routine startup verify could **irrecoverably destroy** a member's key material. Retention converted that into a recoverable wrong-role. The registered symptom genuinely changed shape between registration and fix. **TRIGGER IS NARROW, EFFECT IS SEVERE:** quorum members only, rotation blocks only, 6-block window only — it cannot fire at scale today because rotations are not running in production, which is precisely why it is fixed before they are. **FIX:** `LotteryStateSentry` generalised to **`PTXStateSentry`** (`validation.cpp`), one restore point covering `LotteryState` + pose records + share store, carrying the invariant as a comment: *every consensus-affecting PTX global that validation can mutate restores here*. New helpers `PTX_BLS_SnapshotShares` / `PTX_BLS_RestoreShares` mirror pose's `GetAllRecords`/`RestoreRecords`; the share restore is **durable, not transactional** — it re-persists every restored share and erases keys the walk added, because the raw writes already reached disk. ★ **ALTERNATIVE CONSIDERED AND REJECTED, recorded so it is not "fixed" later:** routing `PTX_BLS_PersistShare` through the transactional evoDb would repair the disk half at source, but it changes persistence semantics for EVERY caller to fix one walk and **breaks ODC-035's degraded path, which deliberately wants persistence untied from block atomicity**. `GetRawDB()` stays; the walk is fixed at the sentry. **VERIFICATION:** suite **427, 0 skipped, 0 failures**. RED-proven before green — the structural check failed with *"PTXStateSentry not found"*, and stubbing out the re-persist made the disk half reload `UNDONE_RETAINED` and unsignable. The BUG-028 seam is **pinned, not assumed**: the tests assert the mid-walk role is `UNDONE_RETAINED` explicitly. ★ **INVARIANT ENFORCED, NOT MERELY STATED:** `Bug029_VerifyDBSentryCoversEveryMutatedPTXGlobal` (`ptx_payout_gen_tests.cpp`) reads `validation.cpp` and fails if the sentry stops naming any of the three globals — so the next sibling is a review-time catch. **HONEST LIMIT:** it pins the three KNOWN globals against removal/rename/narrowing; it **cannot** detect a fourth nobody has named. Completeness is not cheaply enforceable and is not claimed. **Cross-ref** BUG-023 (the mechanism, and the sentry this generalises), BUG-027 (which widened the walk), BUG-028 (share lifecycle; why the severity changed), ODC-035 (why `GetRawDB()` stays), ODC-065 (correct-fix-creates-blind-spot, of which this is the third instance).

> **★ ODC-066 (opened 2026-08-08) — IPv6 ProTx SUPPORT IS A FORK-BLOCKING MAINNET-MIGRATION PREREQUISITE: 86% OF MAINNET'S GAMEMASTERS CANNOT REGISTER A ProTx UNDER CURRENT CONSENSUS.** **WHY ODC AND NOT KDD:** this registers a DISCOVERED CONSTRAINT that opens design work; the only "decision" it contains — the fork-blocking rank — follows from measurement, not from a choice anyone made. The KDD comes when the v6 support design lands. **MAINNET STATE, measured from the read-only observer at h1,296,313 (2026-08-08):** `protx_list` → *"Evo upgrade is not active yet"* — **Evo/ProTx has never activated on mainnet**; `getgamemastercount` → **392 GMs, all legacy, 392 enabled; ipv4 56 / ipv6 336 (86%)**; all 336 v6 ENABLED and holding **18 of the top-20 payment ranks** — v6 operators are the healthy majority, not an edge case. **THE TWO SYSTEMS, READ FROM SOURCE (not inferred):** LEGACY accepts v6 — enforcement is `CGamemasterBroadcast::Create` → `IsValidNetAddr()` (`gamemaster.cpp:318` → `:193`) = `IsReachable(addr) && addr.IsRoutable()`, **family-blind**, plus `CheckDefaultPort` (`:368`, mainnet 49165). ProTx is **v4-only at THREE sites**: `specialtx_validation.cpp:55` (consensus, `bad-protx-ipaddr`, inherited *"!TODO: add support for IPv6 and Tor"*), `activegamemaster.cpp:237` (active-side `IsValidNetAddr`, its own TODO), `activegamemaster.cpp:43` (local-address discovery iterates **v4 peers only** — a v6-only host cannot even self-discover its address). **CONSEQUENCE.** The PTX stack is DGM-only (selection, quorums, PoSe all read the ProTx list), so the PTX fork FORCES a legacy→ProTx migration for all 392 operators — and 336 cannot complete it under current consensus. **v6 ProTx support must land BEFORE any mainnet ProTx activation height**, or the fork strands 86% of the GM population (or forces v4 acquisition on operators who chose v6). **SCOPE:** the three sites above + a DGM/quorum address-handling audit (`CService` serialization is family-clean; the checks are the blocker). The fleet dual-stack proof (harness sites named in the 2026-08-08 expansion assessment, standup) FOLLOWS the consensus change, not before. ★ **PROVENANCE, recorded because it is the method lesson:** the v4-only finding was first made by source read (2026-08-07) and ranked "public-testnet residue — not dischargeable by fleet work"; operator screenshots of live v6 mainnet GMs apparently FALSIFIED it; observer measurement RESCOPED it — the finding stands, scoped to the ProTx path, which today governs zero mainnet gamemasters **and 100% of them at fork activation**. Instrument-vs-inference again, this time with the human's evidence correcting the source read: the source told the truth about a system mainnet is not running. **Cross-ref** KDD-084 (the migration design this opens), ODC-067 (the probe-artefact class the reconciliation also exercised), specialtx_validation's IPv4 check (the consensus site), the 2026-08-08 standup appends (expansion assessment + reconciliation, untracked).

**KDD-084 (2026-08-08) — THE LEGACY→ProTx MIGRATION DESIGN. ★ REGISTERED OPEN: DECISIONS NOT TAKEN. Recorded now so drift cannot later claim they were.** The PTX fork's dependency on the DGM list makes a mainnet migration of all 392 legacy operators unavoidable (ODC-066); none of its design exists. The open decisions, each with its known constraint: **(1) KEY REGIME** — legacy ECDSA `gamemasterprivkey` → BLS operator key, per GM; every remote server re-provisioned; key generation/handling flow undesigned. **(2) GUI SUCCESSOR** — the legacy *"export to remote server"* wallet flow (observed live in operator use, 2026-08-08) has NO ProTx equivalent; operator-facing wallet/product work implied and unscoped. **(3) COLLATERAL** — no coin movement forced: `ProRegTx` can reference the EXISTING unspent 10k collateral outpoint with an ownership signature; but the wallet must then LOCK it — **the BUG-019 family becomes mainnet-relevant because mainnet wallets stake** (`LockGamemasterCollaterals` covers registered collaterals at init; the migration window before registration completes is the exposure to design against). **(4) PAYMENT CONTINUITY** — legacy queue rank is not carried; a migrated GM re-enters the payment cycle fresh (~392-block cycle ≈ 6.5 h income gap at 60 s blocks); a dual-list transition window (DIP3-shape) is implied; the payment-switch height is undesigned. **(5) ACTIVATION GATING** — partial migration concentrates quorum duty and PoSe exposure on the migrated subset (at activation the PTX-eligible pool is the migrated count, not 392); activation must gate on migrated-count; mechanism undesigned and interacts with spork custody, exchange timing, and seed fixes. ★ **FLEET REHEARSAL QUEUED (not scheduled):** the fleet has only ever rehearsed the POST-migration world — stand up a legacy GM on the fleet, migrate it live to ProTx, measure the gap and the dual-list behaviour. **Cross-ref** ODC-066 (the prerequisite that opens this), BUG-019 (collateral-lock family), KDD-063 (handover-at-accept — the availability posture any transition window must preserve).

> **★ ODC-067 (opened 2026-08-08) — THE PROBE-ARTEFACT CLASS, THIRD INSTANCE THIS WEEK: `listgamemasters`' `addr` FIELD IS THE PAYEE ADDRESS, NOT THE NETWORK ADDRESS.** A bracket-test on `addr` classified all 392 mainnet GMs as v4 while the authoritative `network` field said **336 v6** — the probe contradicted `getgamemastercount` and the probe was wrong, not the chain. **THE CLASS, three instances in one week:** (1) this — a field's MEANING assumed from its name; (2) the transient 7/106 RPC-refusal during the expansion assessment — concurrent pollers colliding with `rpcworkqueue=16`, healthy nodes presenting as failed; (3) `protx_list` called wrong-typed during the reconciliation — a non-JSON error reply (*"Evo upgrade is not active yet"*) that a count-parser would have rendered as a zero, and a zero would have silently "confirmed" the wrong hypothesis. **THE STANDING RULE the class keeps proving:** the chain is authoritative; probes lie; **verify the field's MEANING before trusting its SHAPE** — and when a probe and a chain-side counter disagree, suspect the probe first. Same family as the fleet-identical measurement-bug lesson (all-N-identical numbers = check the probe) and ODC-064 (silence is not evidence); this row exists because the class now has enough instances to name the rule once and cross-reference it instead of re-learning it. **Cross-ref** ODC-066 (the reconciliation that produced instance 1 and 3), ODC-064, the fleet-identical lesson (standup/memory, untracked).

**BUG-030 (2026-08-08). ★ THE VerifyDB WALK LEAVES THE QUORUM-STORE recordCache STALE — THE FOURTH GLOBAL, THE ONE BUG-029's SENTRY COULD NOT NAME, LIVE-CAUGHT BY THE ROTATION GATE'S OWN D-TEST.** **DISCOVERY.** Three live instances on the W2.5b fleet, all on gm05, all within one session: the D-test restarted gm05 3 seconds after supersede@2607; at the next boundary (h2610) gm05 SOLO-attempted `ROTATION of adfacd1f` — a quorum the chain had superseded 3 blocks earlier — phantom ceremony e1099713, ABORT sub-threshold after ~7 min, zero peers. At h2640 it re-attempted, this time COLLIDING with the real d24752ab rotation AT THE SAME ANCHOR (both sessions keyed quorum_hash=9a3224bd; gm05 bound the wrong predecessor). At h2670 a third phantom — installed by the MITIGATION ITSELF (see below). **MECHANISM.** Startup `CVerifyDB` level-3 walks the last 6 blocks; disconnecting a rotation PTXDKG runs `ptxQuorumStore->UndoBlock`, which un-supersedes the predecessor **in the in-memory recordCache**. The outer evoDb `CDBTransaction` rollback (validation.cpp:3739) restores the DISK record — **but not the cache**: the walk's cache mutation survives, so the warm cache holds the predecessor as ACTIVE. ★ **The recordCache "faults in on read, never invalidates" (the W2.5a P3 trap, fourth appearance) — so the stale entry NEVER self-heals**: gm05 re-phantomed at every boundary where the stale quorum read as due, 30 blocks apart, confirmed twice before mitigation. **BUG-029's honest-limit paragraph predicted exactly this**: its structural test pins the three KNOWN globals and "cannot detect a fourth nobody has named." This is the fourth, named. **EVERY SAFETY LAYER HELD — recorded because it is why severity is LOW today:** each phantom died sub-threshold in isolation (the only ABORTs on the fleet, ever); the real rotation 7 completed DONE 11/11, ONE group_pk, superseded exactly +27, DESPITE a colliding divergent session on the same session key; V12d predecessor-uniqueness (pq_p) would have consensus-rejected any phantom PTXDKG regardless. **WHY IT STILL MATTERS:** a node restarted within `-checkblocks`(6) of ANY rotation PTXDKG installs a phantom — and operational restarts near rotations are ROUTINE on a real network; at population scale this is a standing source of wasted ceremony slots, ABORT/ROTATE counter noise, and same-key session collisions whose benignity currently rests on member-set disjointness. ★ **THE MITIGATION HAS A TIMING RULE, LEARNED BY FAILING IT TWICE (recorded honestly):** a restart is only curative when the newest rotation PTXDKG is **>6 blocks deep** — the first mitigation restart (3 blocks after supersede@2667) re-armed the bug with a fresher stale entry, and a second attempt fired at depth 4 through a command that printed its own depth check and restarted anyway. The properly gated third restart (depth >6) cleared it; verification at the next boundary pending at registration time. **FIX SHAPE (design gate, not built):** extend `PTXStateSentry` to the recordCache — given the cache's fault-in-on-read design, a FLUSH/evict at sentry-restore suffices (next read faults in the correct disk state; no snapshot needed), and the fourth global joins the sentry invariant + `Bug029_VerifyDBSentryCoversEveryMutatedPTXGlobal` gains a name. Alternative (heavier): make UndoBlock's cache mutation transactional with the evoDb rollback. The flush is the KDD-079-style minimal option and touches no persistence semantics. **EVIDENCE:** monitor event stream (ROTATE/ABORT count deltas with timestamps); gm05 debug.log (3 phantom ROTATION lines, 2 solo ABORTs); `forensics/rotation-h2220-20260807/supplement-cd-*`; standup APPENDs 2026-08-08. **Cross-ref** BUG-029 (the walk, the sentry, and the honest-limit paragraph this discharges), BUG-028 (the rotation lifecycle that puts PTXDKGs in restart windows), ODC-065 (correct-fix-creates-blind-spot — arguably its fourth instance: the sentry's fix scoped three globals while the walk mutated four), the W2.5a recordCache write-behind trap (standup/memory, untracked).

**BUG-031 (2026-08-08). ★ UndoSpecialTxsInBlock DEMANDS A LotteryState SNAPSHOT FOR EVERY BLOCK, UNGATED — the fork binary REFUSES to start on a real pre-activation mainnet datadir. An activation-gating bug caught by the Phase-1 datadir-compatibility test, on real mainnet chainstate.** **SYMPTOM.** Fork binary (31757dc / C++ 6a3d26f, image ptx-w2:v6-6a3d26f) started offline on a COPY of the mainnet observer's datadir (stock v1.3.1.0-61f5e1d, synced to h1,297,357). It opens every LevelDB (block index, chainstate, evodb, llmq, sporks), reads the block index to the correct tip — then aborts: `: Corrupted block database detected. Do you want to rebuild the block database now?`. The stock binary reads the SAME copy clean to "Done loading". Reproduced on two independent fresh copies (not a copy artefact — the observer was stopped for a consistent snapshot, and stock reads a third copy fine). **TRACE (source path from "all opened" to "Corrupted").** `init.cpp:1631` calls `CVerifyDB().VerifyDB()` at startup (DEFAULT_CHECKLEVEL=3, DEFAULT_CHECKBLOCKS=6, validation.h:78-79). Level 3 DISCONNECTS the last 6 blocks; each disconnect calls `UndoSpecialTxsInBlock` (`specialtx_validation.cpp:1587`), which calls `ReadLotteryStateSnapshotForBlock(pindex->pprev)` and, on false, logs `missing LotteryState snapshot for %s — evodb integrity failure` (`:1589`) and returns false. VerifyDB then reports `*** irrecoverable inconsistency in block data at 1297357` and returns false; `init.cpp:1633` sets `strLoadError = "Corrupted block database detected"`. Stock never wrote PTX LotteryState snapshots (they are a fork addition), so the fork's disconnect cannot undo any stock-built block. **MECHANISM = UNGATED GUARD.** `UndoSpecialTxsInBlock`'s LotteryState arm (`:1580-1594`) requires the snapshot UNCONDITIONALLY — no height gate, no `NetworkUpgradeActive(UPGRADE_PTX)` check, no "did this block carry a PTX special tx" check. Block 1,297,357 is PRE-ACTIVATION (V6/Evo pending @1,507,340; no UPGRADE_PTX slot exists yet), so no snapshot should be expected — the guard fired on a block it should have been INERT for. **CLASSIFICATION — a THIRD category, not the two the datadir test framed.** NOT a DB version gate (VerifyDB runs AFTER the version checks at `init.cpp:1547/1570`, which passed) and NOT an on-disk format/schema divergence in the shared block/chainstate DB (those opened and read fine). It is a **validation-logic / PTX-state-dependency divergence in the disconnect path.** ★ **CONSEQUENCE — because the divergence lives in SEPARATE PTX state, not the shared DB, the downgrade path SURVIVES:** the fork's aborted start added exactly one PTX file (`ptx_pose.dat`, 22 bytes empty); the STOCK binary then starts clean on that fork-touched copy and reaches tip 1,297,357 (preliminary T4 PASS). So the upgrade is **TWO-WAY with reindex downtime, NOT one-way** — T1's failure did not predict a T4 failure, precisely because it is not a shared-format change. (Incidental: the fork bumped CLIENT_VERSION, so stock sees fork-written ancillary files e.g. `fee_estimates.dat` as "up-version" and skips them — non-fatal.) **IMPACT, two-fold.** (1) **Gating bug** — PTX machinery firing on pre-activation history, the exact class the datadir-compat test exists to catch. (2) **Forces a reindex on upgrade** — the fork can rebuild from block files (`-reindex-chainstate` reconnects from genesis cleanly, writing a snapshot per block as it goes), but that is minutes-to-tens-of-minutes downtime per node PLUS ~1.29M pointless LotteryState snapshots written for pre-activation blocks. With the fix the fork could HOT-READ stock's chainstate — an in-place binary swap. **This is the single biggest de-risk to the exchange upgrade path.** **FIX (activation-architecture session, NOT now).** Gate the snapshot requirement in `UndoSpecialTxsInBlock` on PTX activation: a missing snapshot for a pre-activation / no-PTX block is a NO-OP, not an integrity failure (the guard stays correct for post-activation blocks). RED: fork binary aborts "Corrupted block database detected" on a pre-activation datadir copy today; post-fix it hot-reads the same copy to tip, no reindex. ★ **THE FAMILY, AND WHY THIS ARGUES FOR STRUCTURAL ENFORCEMENT.** Fifth instance of "a CORRECT PTX-state hardening whose activation/scope boundary was never drawn": BUG-023 (VerifyDB clobbers LotteryState), BUG-025 (pose survives -reindex), BUG-028 (undo/redo share asymmetry), BUG-029 (VerifyDB mutates share store + pose — the missed globals), and now BUG-031 (the same VerifyDB disconnect path demanding snapshots on pre-activation blocks). The guard is right every time; the SCOPE boundary is missing every time. That recurrence argues the "PTX is inert before activation" invariant needs STRUCTURAL enforcement (a single activation gate the disconnect/undo/snapshot machinery all consult), not another site-by-site fix — the same principle the UPGRADE_PTX validity slot exists to carry. **EVIDENCE:** `mainnet-compat-test/` (copies of the observer datadir; T1 fork abort logs on copy3, stock-clean baseline on copy2, preliminary T4 on copy3); standup APPEND 2026-08-08 (datadir-compat). **Cross-ref** BUG-023/025/028/029 (the family — correct-hardening-ungated), the UPGRADE_PTX validity slot (same "inert before activation" principle; the fix and the slot are one session's work), ODC-065 (correct-fix-creates-blind-spot — adjacent shape). Registered pending fix (activation-architecture session).

**BUG-032 (2026-08-09). ★ FREE-PREVIEW / NO-REROLL BROKEN — `gm_bls_sign` is ungated and `ptx_roll` signs BEFORE it commits, so a caller can learn the result and pay only if they like it.** THE INVARIANT VIOLATED: *the caller must not learn the result until payment is irrevocably committed.* **MECHANISM.** `ptx_roll` orders sign-then-commit: `PTX_FanOutSign` at `rpc/ptx.cpp:292`, results derived `:370`, `PTX_AutoCommit` (build+fund+broadcast the fee-bearing PTXSESS) `~:395`. The signature — which *is* the result (`beacon = SigToBeacon(sig)`, `results = MapBeacon(beacon)`) — exists before any fee is committed. And `gm_bls_sign` (`rpc/ptx.cpp:513-570`) is **completely ungated**: it checks only `PTX_BLS_GetCurrentShare(quorum_hash)` (do I hold a share?) then signs any `round_seed` — **no committed-round requirement, no fee/funding/intent, no requester auth, no rate-limit** (the RPC-table `true` at `:1665` is `okSafeMode`, not auth). **EXPLOIT (no quorum control, no forgery).** A caller/coordinator calls `gm_bls_sign(round_seed, Q_canonical)` on ≥threshold members (free), Lagrange-recovers the sig, computes the result locally, and only broadcasts the fee-bearing PTXSESS **if favorable** — else varies `caller_salt` (free-form, feeds `round_seed` via `PTX_BuildNonce(prev_beacon, caller_salt)`) and re-calls, free, until satisfied. `ptx_roll`'s atomicity protects only an *external* caller trusting an honest coordinator; a malicious coordinator (any node running rolls, which sees the result at `:370` and can decline `AutoCommit` at `:395`) or anyone reaching member RPC bypasses it. **This is the cheapest, most direct break of no-reroll — no forgery, no quorum control, just the free primitive + sign-then-commit ordering.** **FIX (design-approved, Option A / fund-then-sign, same-block, NEW nType — see the design report in the standup 2026-08-09):** a new commitment tx type carrying `round_seed + canonical quorum_hash + params + fee + locked inputs`, mempool-valid without sig; `gm_bls_sign` refuses unless it sees that funded commitment for `round_seed`; quorum signs off the *mempool* commitment; settle rides the SAME block (nExpiryHeight = nSeedHeight, wiring the dormant window fields `transaction.h:514-515`). Mempool-level irrevocability (broadcast+funded+locked) is sufficient — no mined-block wait, meets the 500ms/2000ms budget (~1-2 RTTs, coordinator pushes the commitment directly). Residual: the accepted double-spend-your-own-commitment race, identical to any mempool tx; NO judge, NO slashing — only self-inflicted fee forfeiture (structural, not punitive). **RED (whole cluster):** `gm_bls_sign` returns a valid partial sig for a `round_seed` with no funded commitment — succeeds today, must fail post-fix. Consensus code, co-located with existing PTX rules (see the ★ ACTIVATION-RECONCILIATION CHECKLIST below — this rule OWES a move to UPGRADE_PTX). **Cross-ref** BUG-033 (quorum-shop, closed by the same commitment's quorum binding), BUG-034 (result-forge, closed by settle recompute), the standup 2026-08-09 design report + Option-A feasibility trace.

**BUG-033 (2026-08-09). ★ NO-CHOICE-OF-SIGNER BROKEN — consensus accepts ANY recorded quorum's valid signature for a roll, not the selection-mandated one, and the signer never self-checks canonicity (Q2). SURVIVES re-derivation — the insidious one.** **CONSENSUS GAP.** The PTXSESS case (`specialtx_validation.cpp:946-1001`) looks the quorum up by `GetQuorumRecord(payload.quorum_hash)` (`:986`, returns ANY recorded quorum — active, superseded, retired — no state/height filter) and verifies the sig against its group_pk. It NEVER reconstructs the canonical selection for the anchor height and compares. The selection binding exists only for FORMATION txs (`CheckPTXDKGTx`, `PTX_DKG_SelectQuorumFromList` via `GetActiveQuorumsAtHeight`, `:799-819`) — NOT for rolls. **SIGNER GAP.** `gm_bls_sign` signs on demand (BUG-032), checking only share-possession — no "am I the selected quorum for this height." **So the exploit is LIVE, not defence-in-depth-missing:** a threshold-controlling adversary steers the roll to `Q_other` (different group key → different sig → different beacon → different result = deterministic grinding among `1 + k` choices where `k` = quorums they threshold-control), obtains `Q_other`'s honest sig, and publishes. ★ **CRITICAL — it SURVIVES re-derivation:** `results == MapBeacon(beacon)` is TRUE (honest derivation for `Q_other`), so the natural mapping-verifier passes; only an independent selection-recompute catches it, which neither consensus nor the default verifier does. **COST:** control t=6 members of one active quorum; membership is selection-assigned (`PTX_Formation_SelectAtAnchor`) so the adversary can't pick which quorum — must run enough nodes for ≥6 to land together. Honest path is deterministic (`PTX_LoadDKGSigningCtx` uses the anchor block hash, `rpc/ptx.cpp:114-123`, caller has no input) — no honest-path shopping; the malicious path exists because neither consensus nor the signer enforces canonicity. **FIX:** the BUG-032 commitment binds `quorum_hash` to the canonical selection at `nSeedHeight` (validated at commit); settle must use that quorum → non-canonical rejected. Plus signer-side self-check (refuse to sign for a height it wasn't selected for) as defence-in-depth. **RED:** a non-canonical-quorum settle is accepted today, rejected post-fix. **Cross-ref** BUG-032 (same commitment closes both), BUG-034.

> **★ BUG-034 (2026-08-09) — RESULT NOT BOUND TO BEACON AT CONSENSUS (Q1) — DATA HYGIENE, detectable by re-derivation (registered lighter than 032/033 deliberately).** Consensus (`specialtx_validation.cpp:946-1001`) checks `results.size()==count` (`:954`) and `results[i] ∈ [low,high]` (`:956`) — structural RANGE only — plus the BLS sig over `round_seed` (`:995`). It NEVER recomputes `PTX_MapBeacon(beacon)` and compares to `payload.results` (`PTX_MapBeacon` lives ONLY in `rpc/ptx.cpp:370` caller-derive and `:1156` `ptx_getroundstatus` display — never in a consensus path). So a valid sig with ARBITRARY in-range results passes consensus. ★ **BUT DEFANGED BY DETERMINISM + PUBLIC RE-DERIVATION:** the payload carries `beacon`, `quorum_sig`, and every MapBeacon param on-chain (`transaction.h:527-535`), so ANY observer recomputes `beacon == SigToBeacon(sig)` and `results == MapBeacon(beacon)` and catches a Q1 forgery immediately — it's detectable-inconsistent-anchor, NOT undetectable theft. Reach: any tx-builder (attach wrong results to a legit public sig), no quorum control — but detectable. Severity: HYGIENE (consensus shouldn't anchor detectably-inconsistent data, and a lightweight client trusting `payload.results` without re-deriving is deceived until flagged) — NOT theft-prevention. **FIX:** the BUG-032 settle recomputes `MapBeacon` from the verified beacon and rejects a `results` mismatch (the committed `round_seed` makes this exact). **RED:** a forged-results settle accepted today, rejected post-fix. **Cross-ref** BUG-032/033.

> **★ ACTIVATION-RECONCILIATION CHECKLIST (opened 2026-08-09) — CO-LOCATED PTX CONSENSUS RULES OWED A MOVE TO UPGRADE_PTX.** Standing owed-list for the activation-architecture session. `UPGRADE_PTX` (the PTX-validity height slot; recon 2026-08-06) DOES NOT EXIST in the tree yet — only `UPGRADE_V6_0` (`consensus/params.h:41`). PTX special-tx types are currently EXEMPT from the V6_0 gate (`specialtx_validation.cpp:913-916`) and validated by their own cases whenever present (live on ptxbea, dormant on mainnet). Every PTX consensus rule built co-located under this posture MUST be re-gated onto `UPGRADE_PTX` when that slot lands — the ONE risk of building fixes now (option (a), 2026-08-09) is the reconciliation pass forgetting a co-located rule; this checklist closes that. **ITEMS OWED (append as co-located rules land):** (1) BUG-032/033/034 roll commit→reveal + settle validation (new commitment nType, `gm_bls_sign` commitment gate, settle Q1/Q2 checks) — build now co-located, re-gate on UPGRADE_PTX. (2) the pre-existing PTXSESS/PTXCOALESCE/PTXPAYOUT/PTXDKG validation exemptions themselves (`:913-916`) — decide whether they move under UPGRADE_PTX or stay V6-gated. (3) the v6 ProTx widen (BUG/ODC-066) is V6_0-gated by design and does NOT move (recorded so it isn't swept in error). Owner: activation-architecture session. Do not close until every co-located PTX rule has an explicit gate decision. ★ **PROVEN MANDATORY BY h385 (2026-08-11) — this checklist is no longer theoretical.** The BUG-032 C8 re-keying (`CheckPTXCoalesceBlockRules` re-keyed `ptxSessCount` → `PTX_CollectRollFeeOutputs`, commit 61eee30) shipped **UNGATED** and thereby **retroactively invalidated** an early orphan PTXROLLCOMMIT at h385 (fee source present, no coalesce — valid under the old rule when mined, `ptxcoalesce-missing` under the new). The **live fleet never noticed** (the block was already accepted and connected blocks are not re-validated), but a **fresh node validating from genesis wedged at 384** — the chain was not self-validating under its own current rules. Diagnosed by binary bisect (pre-fix syncs past 385, post-fix rejects it) + a whole-chain band-map (h385 the sole offender) + a reindex-vs-network-IBD split (register: reindex skips C8 on stored blocks, so "reindex clean" is NOT a validity proof). Resolved on the **disposable** fleet by **fresh-genesis** (no principled retrofit height threads between h385 and the fix at h511). ★ **MAINNET COROLLARY (non-optional):** every rule on this checklist MUST take an `UPGRADE_PTX` activation height applied **from a clean fork**, so no pre-activation block is ever retroactively invalidated — h385 is the empirical proof that skipping it strands fresh nodes. Prevention verified for the fix itself: h385-**shape** is UNMINEABLE on the current binary (assembler blockassembler.cpp:244 and validator both call the identical `PTX_CollectRollFeeOutputs` on the identical fully-assembled block; post-fix abandon-gate orphans h564/566/570 all coalesced) — but that guards new blocks, not history, which is what the activation gate is for. ★ **NEW OWED ITEM (4) — PROTECTION:** an invariant test asserting **assembler output always passes C8** (the reconciliation rests entirely on both paths sharing `PTX_CollectRollFeeOutputs`; a future divergent scan re-introduces mineable poison invisibly). ★ **DEPLOY DISCIPLINE (adopt):** every consensus-rule change gets a **fresh-node cold-sync-from-genesis** test before it is called done — that catches ungated retroactive invalidation at deploy, not weeks later. See standup 2026-08-11 + scratchpad GATE0_REPORT.

> **★ ODC — PROPAGATION-GATED SIGNING SCALES WITH MESH SIZE (surfaced 2026-08-12 at 311 GMs). A design characteristic of BUG-032 fund-then-sign at fleet scale, NOT a fault.** **MECHANISM.** `gm_bls_sign` (rpc/ptx.cpp:543) gates on `PTX_SignRoundIfCommitted` — a member refuses (retryable `-32051 not-seen`) until it sees the roll's PTXROLLCOMMIT in its OWN LOCAL MEMPOOL. The roll flow (rpc/ptx.cpp:312 build+broadcast → :320 `PTX_FanOutSign`) relies on **gossip** to carry the commitment to the 11 quorum members; there is NO push of the roll commitment tx (the existing `PTX_FanOutCommit`/`gm_commit` at ptx_fanout.cpp:196 carry the DKG-ceremony commitment HASH, unrelated). ★ **THE SCALING AXIS IS THE MESH, NOT THE FAN-OUT.** Fan-out is intra-quorum (FIXED 11 members/roll), so GM count does NOT scale the fan-out; it scales the **P2P mesh the commitment must gossip across** to reach those 11. At 311 GMs gossip to specific members is slow enough that some rolls TIME OUT before threshold: measured ~43-57% roll fail-rate, latency p50 1.4s / p95 ~11s (was ~1.1s/1.6s), BLS-threshold-not-met got 2/6-4/6, under an aggressive driver. Driver load COMPOUNDS (mempool contention slows propagation) but is NOT the root — easing the driver only stops over-driving past the characteristic; "eased-driver recovered" must NOT round up to "no fix needed" (vacuous-relief, the reindex-is-clean shape). **THE REAL FIX (design lever, recon-confirmed viable, NOT built): DIRECT-ACCEPT-AND-VALIDATE the pushed commitment.** Add a push of the PTXROLLCOMMIT TX to the members; the member runs it through the NORMAL mempool-acceptance path (`AcceptToMemoryPool` → `CheckPTXRollCommitTx` specialtx_validation.cpp:915 + FUNDING validation + conflict checks + resource bounds) — SAME validation as gossip, different trigger. ★ **FUNDING VALIDATION IS LOCAL (UTXO-view lookup), NOT the slow part** — so direct-accept genuinely saves the gossip-propagation wait (the recon question that had to be answered YES). Front-runs gossip, does not replace it: the caller's :312 broadcast still gossips for mining. ★ **MUST be ACCEPT-AND-VALIDATE, never trust-the-push** — a fake-funded push that skipped funding validation would re-open the exact free-preview BUG-032 closed (sign without real payment); the push path also needs its OWN resource bound (gossip's peer-scoring/ban does not cover a direct RPC push → mempool-flood DoS). If it is literally "run the pushed bytes through the normal acceptance path," it is the same logic at a different entry point — no new attack surface; malicious pushes reject identically. **RED (all four legs or unproven):** malformed→reject (CheckPTXRollCommitTx); valid-but-FAKE-FUNDED→reject (funding validation, free-preview stays closed); valid+funded→accept+sign (fast happy path); flood→rate-limited. **MAINNET-RELEVANT** (bigger mesh still). Owner: a fund-then-sign-at-scale session. Registered recon-only 2026-08-12; see standup. ★ **DISCRIMINATOR GATE (2026-08-12) — the ~15s was PARTLY a measurement artifact; the design is right for the REAL part only.** Before building direct-attach, ran the propagation-vs-host-contention discriminators (311 daemons on 32 cores/94GB = the confound). RESULTS: (Test 2, host telemetry) host is IDLE — CPU 90% idle, load 10-16/32, swap 0, containers <1 core aggregate, RPC getblockcount 2ms during a roll → host contention RULED OUT. (Test 1, phase timing) the "15s" SPLITS: a real mesh-propagation piece of ~2s (a roll's fan-out logs 118 not-seen retries exhausting the 1.8s FANOUT_MAX_ATTEMPTS budget → threshold miss) AND a ~15s CALLER-SIDE HANG (ptx_roll blocks BEFORE the fan-out — 0 PTX log lines — under rapid/overlapping same-caller rolls; funding fine at 444 UTXOs, RPC responsive, so NOT funding/lock-global/host). Isolated roll = ~1.2s. ★ **CORRECTION:** direct-attach fixes the REAL ~2s mesh propagation (mesh-scaled, WORSE on mainnet's bigger distributed mesh — build it for THAT), and lets the tight 1.8s budget stop binding. Direct-attach does NOT fix the ~15s caller-side hang — that is the DOMINANT 311-on-one-host symptom and a SEPARATE issue (ptx_roll serialization under overlapping rolls; possibly a single-host usage artifact absent on mainnet). ★ OWED: investigate the ptx_roll-before-fan-out serialization hang separately (not a propagation fix). The gate's lesson: "43-57% fail / 15s latency at 311" did NOT equal "mesh propagation" — most of it was caller-side + budget-edge, only ~2s was the propagation the design targets. ★ **HANG MECHANISM DISCRIMINATED (2026-08-12 follow-up) — the caller-side "15s hang" is NOT wallet serialization and NOT a caller lock; two hypotheses FALSIFIED by direct measurement.** Ran the same discriminator discipline on the hang. **(H-mine: evoDb.cs walk / H-user: wallet-funding serialization) BOTH FALSIFIED:** probing locks DURING reproduced hangs → cs_main=1ms, wallet=3ms, mempool=1ms ALL FREE, no block connecting. The hang is INSIDE the fan-out (network I/O to members), not a caller lock — the earlier "0 PTX log lines = before fan-out" was a log-FLUSH artifact (rolls return BLS-threshold after 5-15s, fan-out lines present). **WALLET-FUNDING CONFLICT also FALSIFIED:** 8 concurrent one-wallet rolls → 9 DISTINCT funded commitments in mempool, 0 input-overlap, 0 funding errors → one provider wallet funds N concurrent players' rolls cleanly. **THE PRIMARY INTEGRATION PATTERN WORKS; no funding-pool fix is warranted.** The hang is REAL but INTERMITTENT+RARE (one 5/10 burst, then ~0 across ~50 rolls in five runs; NOT reliably tied to block-connect). Leading hypothesis: a RARE fleet-periodic event (formation/DKG-ceremony every 30 blocks) makes co-located members transiently busy IN LOCKSTEP → fan-out to all 11 stalls — a SINGLE-HOST SYNCHRONIZATION signature (311 daemons connect each block / run each ceremony together) that mainnet's network-staggered distributed nodes would not exhibit → plausibly a FLEET ARTIFACT. Can't prove the negative on one host → **OWED: characterize on a real-RTT distributed testbed.** ★ **PRIORITY REVERTS to direct-attach** (the real ~2s mesh propagation, mainnet-WORSE); the hang is neutralize-in-harness / characterize-on-testbed, NOT a daemon funding-pool fix (which targets a disproven mechanism). **Lesson (again): the confident reframe (wallet-serialization, day-one mainstream) was FALSIFIED by measurement — discriminate the mechanism, don't build against the story.** ★ **EASED-CONTROL RECONCILIATION (2026-08-12, task byl9r9agd — the confound-free measurement).** 10 rolls at ~18-min spacing (zero driver load, zero overlap) = **3 ok / 7 fail (70%), p50 10s, p95 15s.** This TEMPERS the "rare artifact" lean above. RECONCILIATION with the interactive bursts (which looked fast/rare): my bursts returned ~1.5s but those were `BLS threshold` **fast FAILURES**, not successes — I under-read them. Warm/clustered rolls fail FAST (1.8s budget); cold/spread rolls fail SLOW (10-15s cold-member HTTP); **BOTH FAIL — the failure rate is high either way. The failure is the substance; latency-to-failure was the side-show.** So the 15s is NOT a rare warm-state artifact — the eased (confound-free) control shows ~70% failure at MAXIMAL patience → easing did NOT recover → **propagation is genuinely the root at 311, and the mechanism-falsifications (not-a-lock, not-wallet) stand for WHERE the time goes.** ★ **This STRENGTHENS direct-attach** (delivers the commitment directly → members sign → threshold met → the 70% failure collapses). Caveat UNCHANGED: eased-control rules out driver-load, NOT host-density (still one host) → single-host-vs-mainnet magnitude still owed to the real-RTT testbed; direct-attach helps in both.
