(Sample=10, small, but agrees with interactive high-failure.) ★ **HOST-DENSITY vs PROPAGATION DISCRIMINATED — and a THIRD confound found (2026-08-12).** Ran the member-side discriminator (the load-bearing question: do failed rolls' members RECEIVE-late=propagation or RECEIVE-promptly-RESPOND-slowly=host-density). **HOST-DENSITY RULED OUT:** all 311 co-located GMs answer RPC in **3-7ms** (p50=3, p99=6, max=7, 0 slow, 0 dead) queried in parallel → individual members are NOT resource-starved; the "aggregate-idle-but-member-slow" mechanism is tested and NEGATIVE. So `not-seen` replies are FAST-but-commitment-absent = **PROPAGATION** (mesh-scaled, mainnet-worse via P2P trickle×hops, protocol-not-network). ★ **BUT the fan-out log breakdown surfaced a THIRD confound that inflates the failure rate: STALE FAN-OUT ADDRESS BOOK.** Outcomes (400 contacts): 301 not-seen, **50 no-node-info**, 45 got-partial. The `no node info` members are ALL high-numbered (gm157+) = the 150→311 GROW cohort. `PTX_FanOutSign` resolves member addresses via `PTX_FindNode`→`g_ptx_nodes`, populated by `PTX_LoadNodesFromArgs` from **STATIC `-ptxnode` config at restart** (ptx_quorum.cpp:22), NOT the live DGM list. caller1 logged "150 node(s) loaded" then "240" — its live book is ≤240 while selection draws from **311 eligible** → **~39% of each quorum's members are UNADDRESSABLE**. ★ **THIS CONFOUNDS the eased-control "70% fail = propagation":** much of the failure is members the caller can't even send to, NOT slow propagation. ★ **GATES the direct-attach decision:** must (1) fix the address book (fleet: regen 311-config with matching node_ids + restart callers; PRODUCT/mainnet: resolve member addresses from the LIVE DGM list — static config CANNOT track dynamic membership, a real design gap) and (2) RE-MEASURE the TRUE propagation rate — BEFORE concluding propagation is dominant or building direct-attach. Direct-attach fixes propagation but NOT the address staleness (can't deliver to an unaddressable member). **Third discriminator win: the confident conclusion was confounded by fleet config — measure the mechanism, fix the confound, re-measure.** ★ **FAIL-LOUD/SAFE VERIFIED (2026-08-12) — the unreachable-member failure path degrades SAFELY (the stale book gave a free test of it at scale).** For the failed rolls, checked caller-side + on-chain ground truth. **(1) OBSERVABLE/LOUD:** 14/14 failures returned clear RPC errors (`BLS threshold not met: got N/6` / timeout), **0 false-success** (0 settles, none returned OK). The caller ALWAYS knows. **(2) NO SILENT CHARGE:** fund-then-sign broadcasts the commitment before signing, so a threshold-failure still MINES its commitment (fee/stake realized) — but the orphan is swept by the designed **PTXCOALESCE** path (one visibly aggregating 16 inputs→82.0 pot output), so the forfeit is **RECORDED on-chain**, not silently lost. Window reconciliation: 23 commitments = 8 settles(success) + 15 orphans(fail), orphans coalesced. **(3) PER-FAILED-ROLL COST:** commitment vout = [0.01 caller-change(ismine), **1.0 LOTTERY/forfeit(not-ismine)**, 0.001 change] → the ~1.0 STAKE is forfeited to the pot (recorded), change returns. So: **LOUD + SAFE + RECORDED = GOOD, mainnet-reassuring** — the system degrades safely under member-unreachability (mainnet WILL have it via churn/partitions). ★ **FLAGGED ECONOMIC PROPERTY (permanent, not a bug):** a roll that fails AFTER commitment-broadcast forfeits its stake — the deliberate anti-free-preview (BUG-032 fund-then-sign) tradeoff; refunding would reopen free-preview. Member-unreachability triggers it, so the **stale address book is currently forfeiting stakes on ~70% of rolls (recorded but lost to providers, transferred to the pot)** — sharpens the urgency of the address-book fix. Providers must know: a failed roll costs the stake. No safety bug found; the forfeit is value-conserving (→ pot) and observable.

★ **BUG-034 / E2-REPRO — COMMIT+SETTLE SAME-BLOCK SEPARATION → CHAIN HALT + CALLER-VS-CHAIN DIVERGENCE (2026-08-14, found under concurrency stress).** ★ **CHAIN HALTED ~18min at h5065.** Producer log: `CheckPTXRollCommitSettlePairing: PTXSESS 7faf3b4f... has no coin-chained PTXROLLCOMMIT parent in this block (payment-before-reveal unbound)` → `ConnectBlock: ptxsess-no-commitment-parent` → `block-template construction FAILED ... producer stays alive (BUG-024)`. **MECHANISM:** an UNBOUND PTXSESS (settle whose PTXROLLCOMMIT parent already mined in an EARLIER block, so provably unminable) sat in the mempool; the block-ASSEMBLER kept SELECTING it (it has fees), the VALIDATOR rejects it → every template fails → all producers skip every candidate → halt. **This is the E2 assembler-passes-validation invariant violated (h385 class): the assembler builds a block the validator rejects.** ★ **UNSTALL (applied):** `prioritisetransaction <settle> 0 -1e9` on all 8 producers → assembler skips it → chain resumed h5065→5071, backlog 87→1. Orphan permanently unminable so deprioritise loses nothing; holds across blocks. ★ **UPSTREAM (the bigger finding):** the roll was **SUCCESSFUL** (a settle is only built at threshold → `ptx_roll` returned OK to the client). Commit `07ca41f4` mined @h5065; settle `7faf3b4f` orphaned (never mined). **SAME-BLOCK COIN-CHAIN MANDATE VIOLATED** (the property that gives coin-chain reorg-atomicity). ★ **CALLER-VS-CHAIN DIVERGENCE (by construction):** caller got OK + was CHARGED (commitment mined → fee realized), but the chain has NO settle → no on-chain result/payout record. More concerning than the halt. ★ **ROOT — FUND-THEN-SIGN TIMING RACE:** commitment broadcast IMMEDIATELY (pre-sign, anti-free-preview BUG-032), settle built ~1.4s later (post fan-out); a block mined in that window mines the commitment ALONE → settle orphaned. CONCURRENCY amplifies (more commits in flight near block boundaries) — why the stress test exposed it; **Vileda's pay-before-reveal instinct was RIGHT for THIS (not for the roll failures, which were A/IPv6).** ★ **FIX — TWO LAYERS (prevention-over-mitigation, h385 discipline), OWED (E2-tier, pre-mainnet-mandatory):** (1) **PREVENTION:** `AcceptToMemoryPool` REJECTS a PTXSESS whose commitment parent already mined in an earlier block — provably unminable at acceptance → never enters mempool → no poison, bad state unrepresentable. (2) **BACKSTOP:** assembler EXCLUDES unbound settles (assembler-passes-validation invariant). ★ **DEEPER OWED (neither layer fixes it):** the DIVERGENCE itself — commitment mining WITHOUT its settle available orphans a SUCCESSFUL roll's result; options = commit-settle atomic mining (don't mine commit without settle) OR roll doesn't return OK until settle is minable OR treat orphaned-settle as the forfeiture path (but roll already returned OK). This is the fund-then-sign vs same-block-coin-chain design tension, exposed. Registered as BUG-034 + E2 concrete repro. ★ **BUG-034 SEVERITY ELEVATED + RESOLUTION PATH (2026-08-14 amendment).** ★ **SEVERITY = HIGH, worse than the halt:** a SUCCESSFUL roll SILENTLY loses its on-chain record — caller got OK + was CHARGED, chain has NO PTXSESS / NO result / NO verifiable proof. For a VERIFIABLE RANDOMNESS BEACON this is the CORE DELIVERABLE going missing: an auditor sees a paid commitment and no result, INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM ABANDONMENT. It DEFEATS fail-loud-and-safe (which held for FAILED rolls) — this is the UNTESTED case: a SUCCESSFUL roll failing silently. ★ **ROOT IS STRUCTURAL, NOT A RACE:** fund-then-sign REQUIRES broadcast-before-signing; the settle can only exist AFTER fan-out (~1.4s later). So EVERY roll has a MANDATORY WINDOW where the commitment is mineable and the settle does not yet exist → same-block is UNSATISFIABLE BY CONSTRUCTION for any roll whose commitment mines in its own signing window. Internal contradiction between fund-then-sign and same-block-coin-chain. ★ **EXPOSURE (measured):** 1 LIVE divergence (the orphan, recoverable — still in mempool), 0 historically-lost (last 300 blk: 155 commits = 85 settled + ~70 forfeited, all accounted; 0 dropped settles). Count is low ONLY because same-block made it a LOUD HALT and we caught the first one. ★★ **CRITICAL — DO NOT SHIP TOLERANCE WITHOUT A DETECTOR:** the 2-layer fix (mempool-reject + assembler-exclude) prevents the HALT but makes the orphaned settle disappear CLEANLY → converts LOUD (halt) into SILENT (quietly dropped, nobody notices a roll lost its result). MUST log/alert/COUNT every dropped-unbound-settle as a divergence event, else loud symptom → silent data loss. ★ **RESOLVE-OPTIONS (prefer resolve over manage):** (1) **RELAX same-block → "commitment in THIS block OR ALREADY CONFIRMED" — CHECKED VIABLE:** CheckPTXRollCommitSettlePairing (specialtx_validation.cpp:1282) claims same-block for reorg-atomicity, but that atomicity is provided by the COIN-CHAIN INPUT DEPENDENCY independently (drop the parent anywhere → settle's input vanishes). Same-block is strictly stronger than needed; only the quorum/seed cross-checks read parent from block.vtx (would read from confirmed chain instead). RELAX → orphaned settle MINABLE LATER → **divergence DISSOLVES**, no mitigation needed. Interaction: coalesce/forfeiture must NOT forfeit a commitment whose settle is merely PENDING under the relaxed rule. **This is the cleanest fix — do it FIRST.** (2) **HOLD the commitment UNMINABLE during the signing window** (short timelock / mempool policy until its settle arrives) — preserves fund-then-sign AND makes same-block satisfiable; resolves the contradiction instead of picking a side. (3) **"Treat as forfeiture" is WRONG** — charging for a SUCCESSFUL roll and discarding its result is worse than charging for a failed one. ★ Priority: (1) relax same-block [cleanest, dissolves it] + a DIVERGENCE DETECTOR regardless; the mempool-reject/assembler-exclude become the loud-fail backstop, not the primary fix. E2-tier, pre-mainnet-MANDATORY. ★ **BUG-034 RESOLUTION APPROVED (2026-08-14): NO CONSENSUS UPPER BOUND on `nExpiryHeight` — the prior question ("should a bound exist at all?") answered NO, per-candidate, source-path-checked.** Evidence table: **(1) replay/double-settle — NOTHING:** the settle spends the commit's `vout[1]`; once mined the UTXO is consumed → no second settle at ANY depth (UTXO layer covers it fully). **(2) validation cost — NOTHING:** quorum located by `quorum_hash` from chain state (O(1)); parent cross-check fields reachable via the spent Coin's `nHeight` → one bounded block read, no unbounded scan (verify-in-practice with a real deep parent owed at Phase-2). **(3) payout/settlement — NOTHING, and ★ THE AUTHOR'S OWN PRIOR CONSTRAINT FALSIFIED:** `PTX_BuildPayoutTx` (ptx_payout.cpp:12-51) pays the FEE-ACCUMULATOR (coalesced commit `vout[0]`s) to an ENTROPY-selected winner (`PTX_ComputeSelectionEntropy(blockHeight, prevBlockHash)`) and **never reads a settle** — the "MAX_WINDOW ≤ settlement cadence" precaution in the earlier verdict was WRONG; recorded so it is not re-assumed. **(4) mempool retention — POLICY:** a settle past a horizon isn't relayed/kept by default but stays VALID if it reaches a block; relay policy, not consensus. **(5) semantic staleness — NOTHING:** no consumer needs "definitively over at height X"; the TWO settle-consumers surfaced in the check (the moment the verdict could have flipped, both checked not waved through): `ptx_exclude.cpp` skips unconfirmed txids at build-time (caller policy, not consensus timing); `ptx_formation.cpp` quorum-idle attribution is deterministic at mine-height with `n_retire` dwarfing realistic delays. ★ **THE STRUCTURAL PROPERTY (the result, not just the conclusion):** under no-bound, the ONLY way a valid settle becomes unminable is a reorg removing its commitment — which drops `vout[0]` (the fee) too, so the payment is undone and the roll unwinds consistently. **There is NO state where the caller is charged and the result is permanently unrecordable. "If paid, deliver" holds BY CONSTRUCTION**, with the failure mode being "the payment is undone too" — correct. ★ **Consequences:** Phase-1 detector RESCOPED from permanent-loss counter (empty class → vacuous green) to OPERATIONAL signal (long-pending settles = relay/assembly health + settle-after-commit timing baseline). Phase-3 backstop SHRUNK to the pure anti-halt assembler invariant (never include a settle whose parent isn't on-chain / `vout[1]` spent; one poison tx must never fail every template — E2, h5065 repro); no pending-vs-expired state, just spent-vs-unspent. ★ **LESSON (capture):** the final change is SMALLER than the bug it fixes — dropping an upper bound that was never load-bearing, rather than building a windowed consensus rule with a pending state the coalesce/mempool/assembler all had to agree on. That is the signature of a correct diagnosis. ★ **BUG-034 PHASE 2 LANDED + GREEN (2026-08-14).** **Implementation:** `CheckPTXRollCommitTx` window-0 equality retired (only the structural floor `nExpiryHeight >= nSeedHeight` remains; reject-code `ptxcommit-window-negative`); pairing rule now PURE (block + pre-resolved parents) accepting a same-block sibling OR a confirmed commitment at any depth, quorum/seed anti-shop cross-checks unchanged; new `PTX_CollectConfirmedSettleParents` = the deterministic PRE-SPEND resolver (coin.nHeight → GetAncestor → one block read) captured in BOTH callers (ConnectBlock + ReplayBlock, compiler-forced param) — deliberately NOT GetTransaction/pcoinsTip (connect-vs-replay divergence, BUG-029 class, rejected in recon). **RED→GREEN:** 5 new unit cases (confirmed-parent GREEN; no-parent / quorum-mismatch / seed-mismatch all still REJECT — the anti-vacuity legs proven not inferred; sibling-precedence) + 6 pre-existing 2c cases + FULL SUITE 454/454. **FLEET GREEN:** the real h5065 orphan settle MINED @ h5212 — **parent depth 147 blocks**, accepted by 10/10 sampled validators (each performed the one-block parent read; block propagated normally = the depth-cost check passed in practice at the fleet's max available depth; "thousands deep" untestable on a 5.2k chain — stated honestly). Exposure 1→0: the BUG-034 roll's result is ON-CHAIN. **POPULATION (not one-orphan):** commit-without-settle rate 17.3% → **6.5% = the legitimate-abandon floor** (matches the 91-95% roll OK-rate); detector pending=0 alert=false, delta_p95=147 (the recovered orphan is the outlier that proves the path). **DEPLOY LESSON (banked):** `docker cp` of a 290MB binary ×319 containers = ~290MB COW layer EACH → filled the 94G root to 100% mid-deploy (silent from gm61 — error-swallowing subshells); recovery = image-prune 23.5G + lvextend +15G; the correct deploy = IMAGE REBUILD (`ptx-w2:roll-bug034-3d6fb94e` = new binary + `-rpcbind=[::]` BAKED, discharging the owed durable dual-stack fix) + rolling recreate — one shared layer, reclaimed ~19G net. docker-cp×N is a proven anti-pattern on this box. **MAINNET GATING (owed, checklist):** the relax is a LOOSENING — old validators reject a late-settle block → mainnet requires coordinated activation at UPGRADE_PTX (joins the C8 re-gate on the activation-reconciliation checklist). Fleet = fresh-genesis simultaneous deploy, no retroactive invalidation (pre-relax blocks all window-0, accepted by both rule versions). ★ **BUG-034 PHASE 3 LANDED + INERT (2026-08-14): the anti-halt backstop (E2 assembler-passes-validation invariant, h5065 repro).** **Shape:** the pairing loop body extracted into `PTX_SettleParentVerdict` (OK/BAD_PAYLOAD/NO_PARENT/QUORUM_MISMATCH/SEED_MISMATCH) — the validator's pairing rule AND the assembler's new template filter both call THIS one function, so **filter-fires ⇔ validator-rejects by construction; divergence is unrepresentable** (the h385 one-function contract). `PTX_TemplateUnpairableSettles` (same collector + same verdict over the candidate) feeds the assembler filter (post-addPackageTxs, pre-fee-fold/coalesce): drops flagged settles with a descendant CASCADE (a child of a dropped tx would re-poison via missing inputs) and consistent bookkeeping (fees/sigops/size/parallel arrays, never the coinbase). **LOUD by requirement:** every drop logs `BUG-034 P3: excluding unpairable settle`, and the dropped settle stays in the mempool under the live detector's pending-settle alert — tolerance cannot convert the halt into silence. **THE INERTNESS BAR (the false-positive risk = BUG-034 re-created by its own fix; the critical case = the SAME-TEMPLATE SIBLING, the p50=0 production path):** the filter's sibling map is built from the template's OWN vtx, verdict checks siblings FIRST — pinned by test at verdict level (sibling map, zero confirmed state → OK) AND filter level (healthy pair, view=nullptr → empty; poisoned template → exactly-one NO_PARENT flag). Suite 456/456. **FLEET (zero-bar, all five gate conditions): EXCLUSIONS = 0 across all 8 producers over a 14-roll run; commit/settle pairs mining same-block (3=3, Δp50=0); detector pending=0 alert=false post-deploy; unsettled-mixed 3.9% (below the 6.5% abandon floor — trailing window now majority post-fix rolls); rolls 11/14 OK (known threshold-miss residual, not new).** Deployed via image rebuild `ptx-w2:roll-bug034p3-524b04bf` + rolling recreate (the proven path; 319/319 uniform). **OWED:** adversarial-injection test (a mempool-acceptable, pairing-rejectable settle → filter drops it + cascade composition verified live) — joins the quorum-shop-bound adversarial family. ★ BUG-034 ARC COMPLETE: detector (P1) + relax-no-bound (P2, green @ h5212 depth-147) + anti-halt backstop (P3, inert-verified). Remaining on the arc: mainnet UPGRADE_PTX activation (checklist) + the adversarial-injection test. ★ **ROLL-TYPE BASELINE (2026-08-14, pre-soak) — full parameterization matrix + exclusions.** The product verbs (/roll /draw /pick /teams /shuffle /flip /deal /seed) are NOT separate RPCs — the daemon surface is `ptx_roll(count,low,high,unique,exclude,game_id,salt)`; each verb = a params shape with its own MapBeacon path + settle-payload shape. **ALL 8 SHAPES WORK** (6 rolls each): d100 4/6, others 5/6 (every miss = the known 0/6 threshold residual, type-INDEPENDENT), p50 1.4-4.1s, **shape assertions clean across the board** (count/range/uniqueness/shuffle-is-a-permutation). 44 settles decoded on-chain: results match RPC responses; **params_hash recompute matched 44/44**. ★ **TAMPER-EVIDENCE PROVEN ON-CHAIN:** the full params INCLUDING both exclusion lists are serialized in the settle payload; recomputed PTX_HashParams == on-chain ptx_params_hash, and ALTERED exclusions → hash DIFFERS. The binding holds functionally, not just by design. ★ **INT-EXCLUSIONS CLEAN** (7/7 rolls, 0 violations incl. the forced-value case). ★ **BUG-035 (NEW, pre-existing — NOT arc-related): TXID-EXCLUSION INTERMITTENTLY NON-APPLIED, SILENTLY.** Excluding a confirmed settle's txid leaked its result back into new rolls **2 of 6 rolls, split across callers with identical chain state** (caller3 excluded correctly; caller4 leaked, same txid, deep-confirmed, output unspent). NO skip log on any caller — the resolver's documented not-confirmed skip did NOT fire; the only SILENT drop path in `PTX_ResolveExcludeSet` is a `GetTxPayload` failure (no else-log). Consequence: the on-chain artifact is INCONSISTENT with its own params under re-derivation (payload carries exc_txids, an auditor's MapBeacon would exclude the value the roll included) — and with 2c-iii absent nothing catches it. **TXID-exclusions EXCLUDED from the soak; owed a dedicated fix session (add the missing log + make resolution deterministic — note the resolver's GetTransaction/pcoinsTip dependency is the same node-state-dependent shape rejected for the pairing rule, BUG-029 class).** ★ **2c-iii CONFIRMED UNIMPLEMENTED** (results==MapBeacon not consensus-validated; auditability on-chain, enforcement absent) — pre-existing, now explicit on the owed list. ★ Dashboard latency-proxy regex fixed (the attempt-count follows ', ' not '(' — the old anchor never matched; latency column now populates via the passes proxy for non-driver rolls). ★ **THRESHOLD-MISS RESIDUAL DECOMPOSED (2026-08-14) — "known residual" opened and closed; it was TWO mechanisms, neither what the label implied.** Fan-out log decomposition (~2000 records, 8 callers): **100% of failure reasons = `not-seen`** (zero no-share, zero mid-rotation, zero transport — mechanisms 2 and 3 RULED OUT by measurement, member/quorum concentration uniform). Structure BIMODAL: (a) **near-miss tail** (1-2 members still not-seen at give-up, 19 rolls) and (b) **TOTAL BLACKOUT** (10-11 of 11 not-seen, 16 rolls — the whole quorum never saw the commitment). ★ **Budget experiment** (FANOUT_MAX_ATTEMPTS 12→60, ~1.8s→9s): cold run confounded by caller restart (recorded, not rounded); warm rerun 32/40 OK with the **near-miss tail GONE** (11 successes landed in the 2-9s conversion band; p50 1.6s) — population (a) was TUNING, fixed by the raised ceiling (costless on the happy path: stop-at-threshold still returns at the 6th partial; the old ceiling turned recoverable waits into forfeited stakes). ★ **Population (b) was 100% CALLER1** (all five warm blackout misses on rolls i≡0 mod 8): caller1 had **ZERO connections — self-isolated by its own stale `banlist.dat` (59 bans)** accumulated during its divergent-fork era and SURVIVING the resync (the wipe removed chain data but not banlist.dat). Its broadcasts reached nobody → every caller1 roll blacked out → the "type-independent ~1-per-6 miss" in the baseline = caller1's rotation share hiding inside an aggregate. FIX: clearbanned + reconnect; **RECIPE: banlist.dat goes in the resync wipe.** ★ **ATTRIBUTION (Part 2, structural — no bisect needed):** the not-seen CLASS exists only since fund-then-sign's signing gate (PTX_SignRoundIfCommitted) created the propagation precondition — **the residual is the measured cost of the anti-free-preview property**, trade now explicit: the gate + a finite budget races the source INV trickle and mesh propagation; the raised budget prices the race correctly; direct-attach (C) remains the structural elimination (commitment rides the request). ★ Prior baseline/OK-rate numbers (91-95%, 4-5/6 per type) are SUPERSEDED — contaminated by the caller1 banlist bug + the tight budget; clean full re-test is the standing baseline. ★★ **BUG-036 — A LOST evoDb WRITE BECAME A CONSENSUS PARTITION SIX BOUNDARIES LATER (2026-08-14; the most serious finding of the session).** **THE EVENT:** the fleet PARTITIONED at h5487 — all 311 GMs (+ caller1's IBD-rebuilt store) on one branch; ALL 7 incumbent producer-callers together on the other. GM branch nearly halted (zero producers once caller1 was quarantined — production sits entirely with the callers); caller branch advanced but roll-dead (GM members reject its commitments). **THE PIN (log-ledger + source):** reform stamps phase-shifted between populations — gm01: e2dbf10a REFORMED@5460 (cadence 5340/5400/5460/5520); callers: REFORMED@5490 (5310/5370/5430/5490). Lockstep through 5280 (same second, 15:41:32Z); the callers-only budget-binary docker restart (~16:00Z, SIGKILL-after-10s) LOST the 5280 reform stamp (evoDb write-behind; head markers read consistent → NO replay re-fire — while gm01's own earlier restart DID replay-refire 5220, the 14:27/14:30 double); callers' `last_reform_height` stale by one boundary → MaybeReformAtBoundary (chain-derived pacing over the STORE's max stamp, ptx_quorum_store.cpp) fired at 5310 → whole schedule phase-shifted +30 forever → e2dbf10a stamped 5490-vs-5460 → BUG-033 gate `PTX_QuorumRecordActiveAt(rec, nSeedHeight=5475)` = `reformed_height > 5475` → callers: canonical, GMs: non-canonical → caller6 staked the commit, GMs rejected, callers accepted each other's blocks → partition + ban war. **HYPOTHESIS CORRECTION (recorded per discipline): height-keying was WRONG** — both roll-side (rpc/ptx.cpp:114→ptx_quorum_store.cpp:393) and gate-side (specialtx_validation.cpp:966-971) already share ONE predicate; the divergence was CONTAINS at the STAMP level — **durability, not logic**. ★ **REGISTER 1 — reindex≠network-IBD, RE-REGISTERED AT CONSENSUS-SPLITTING SEVERITY** (linked BUG-033): with the INVERSION — the REBUILT store was RIGHT and the incumbents were WRONG; caller1 (banned, healed 3×, quarantined as a partition source) was correct the entire time; two independent computations (311 incumbents' GM stores + one from-genesis rebuild) agreeing is the strongest verdict available; the 7 callers shared ONE correlated error. ★ **REGISTER 2 — PATH-DEPENDENT STATE, corrected + strengthened to DERIVE-DON'T-STORE:** anything derivable from chain data should be DERIVED (or recompute-verified), because stored copies DRIFT — a lost write in stored-and-trusted state is a latent partition source. STANDING AUDIT QUESTION for all future state: "what else is stored-and-trusted rather than derived?" ★ **REGISTER 3 — FORENSIC COMPLETENESS:** ptx_quorum_info must EXPOSE reformed_height (its absence forced a source-read where a store query should have sufficed); all state transitions need queryable height stamps. ★ **REGISTER 4 — PRODUCTION CONCENTRATION + THE MAINNET FRAMING:** this fleet's producer/validator split made the partition LEGIBLE (stalled branch + roll-dead branch = obviously broken); on mainnet staking and validating are the SAME nodes → a stamp divergence yields TWO SELF-CONSISTENT ADVANCING CHAINS with nothing announcing the disagreement until reconciliation. Severity of the class raised accordingly. ★ **INTERIM OPERATIONAL CONSTRAINT (until the durability fix lands): no daemon restarts near %30 boundaries; docker stops must allow graceful shutdown (raise the stop timeout).** ★ **FIX PLAN: LAYER 2 IS PRIMARY** — recompute `last_reform_height` from chain-scannable events (the store becomes a cache of the derivable, lost writes self-heal at the next boundary, divergent state unrepresentable); RED = a deliberately-corrupted stamp self-heals instead of phase-shifting. **LAYER 1 = the audit question:** why did this write escape the WriteBestBlock atomic pair (BUG-029/ReplayBlocks apparatus)? gm01's replay caught its 5220 gap; the callers' didn't catch 5280 — that asymmetry is the diagnostic. OMISSION (PTX writes outside the pair — contained) vs GAP (the pair itself leaks — everything evoDb protects is exposed). Scoping answer REQUIRED before the layer-1 fix design. ★ RECOVERY: Option A (GM branch), callers 2-8 by RESYNC (IBD = provably-correct stores, also heals the lost-5280 residue that invalidateblock would leave), completion condition = ALL 8 CALLERS PRODUCING (not merely chain-advancing). ★ **A — DGM-DERIVED FAN-OUT ADDRESS RESOLUTION (built 2026-08-12, non-consensus transport fix).** Root: `PTX_FanOutSign` resolved member host:port via `PTX_FindNode`→static `g_ptx_nodes` (`-ptxnode` config, loaded once at startup) → stale on growth (~39% of a grown quorum unaddressable). FIX (5 edits, ptx_fanout.{h,cpp} + ptx_quorum_store.{h,cpp} + rpc/ptx.cpp): carry `map<node_id,proTxHash>` into `PTXDKGSigningCtx` (both paired in `PTXQuorumMemberRecord`); new `PTX_ResolveMemberAddr` prefers the LIVE DGM list (`GetListAtChainTip().GetValidGM(proTxHash)->pdmnState->addr`) and falls back to static `g_ptx_nodes`. ★ **TWO HALVES, DO NOT BLUR (the band-aid-vs-fix shape, corrected):** (1) **DGM-HOST resolution = the REAL fix, PERMANENT** — addresses read from current on-chain ground truth every roll, tracks permissionless churn, no restart-moment needed. (2) **RPC PORT = fleet EXPEDIENT** — the PTX-RPC endpoint is NOT on-chain (DGM advertises only P2P port), so we use DGM-host + chain-default RPC port (`-ptxfanoutport` override). This embeds "GMs expose PTX-RPC at DGM-IP:standard-port", which permissionless operators VIOLATE (non-standard/firewalled/unexposed) → re-introduces the address-assumption class on mainnet. ★ **KDD-085 (OWED, SAME TIER — NOT "hardening"): SIGN-OVER-P2P** = deliver the sign request to the member's ON-CHAIN-advertised address via the P2P protocol it definitely speaks (no port assumption). This is the MAINNET-CORRECT delivery and the OTHER HALF of A. **A's host-resolution is done; A's mainnet delivery (P2P) is still owed — do NOT read "A done" as mainnet-complete.** The port-convention's ONLY value over the rejected restart-band-aid: it unblocks the clean re-measure (B). Direct-attach (KDD parked) is BELOW E1/E2 and gated on B.

**Next-free: KDD-085 / ODC-068 / BUG-035.**

> **★ ODC-071 (opened 2026-08-16) — THE RE-SHARE GAP: A MEMBER THAT LOSES ITS sk_share IS DEAD TO ITS QUORUM UNTIL W2.4 ROTATION REPLACES THE QUORUM ENTIRELY.** Registered out of BUG-039 (the boot-ordering wipe) but INDEPENDENT of it: however a share is lost — disk fault, datadir restore from backup, host migration, operator error — there is NO recovery path. The share is ceremony secret material, not derivable from chain, not recomputable, not fetchable from peers; the member stays in_qual on chain (selection still counts it) while contributing zero signing capacity, silently eroding the quorum's threshold margin (ODC-070's detection surface reports it; nothing repairs it). Acceptable while loss is rare on a tended fleet; NOT acceptable on a permissionless network where operators restart, reindex, migrate and err. **PRECEDENT:** Dash's LLMQ layer leans on QDATA P2P quorum-data recovery for exactly this (the DB_VVEC / DB_SKCONTRIB / DB_QUORUM_SK_SHARE sweep, 2026-08-15 standup) — a member can re-derive its share from re-served ceremony data. **MINIMUM VIABLE FOR TESTNET:** a documented operator recovery procedure (what is and is not recoverable, how long until affected quorums rotate out, what the operator should do — doc/ptx/ptxbea-known-limitations.md carries it as of this entry). **PROPER ANSWER FOR MAINNET (the design work this ODC opens):** a proactive re-share protocol or QDATA-shaped P2P share recovery. **Cross-ref** BUG-039 (the loss class that surfaced it), ODC-070 (margin-erosion detection — the observability half of this gap), ODC-035 (memory-only degraded mode), KDD-063 (handover-at-accept — rotation is the only current replacement path), W2.4 (rotation arm).

> **★ ODC-072 (opened 2026-08-17) — THE COMPOSITION-SEAM BUG CLASS: TWO INDIVIDUALLY-CORRECT FEATURES INTERACTING AT A SEAM NOBODY TESTS.** Registered out of BUG-040 because it is the THIRD instance of the same shape, and three is a design concern, not a run of bad luck: **BUG-037** (pose tracker × replay — each correct alone, the restore had no height anchor), **BUG-039** (ODC-070's share file × BUG-029's sentry — each correct alone, init ordering composed them into a wipe-every-boot), **BUG-040** (BUG-034's settle-parent resolver × TestBlockValidity's stack dummy index — each correct alone, the resolver was the first consensus-path caller to `GetAncestor` deep off the pskip-less dummy). **THE GENERAL FORM:** when a feature adds a NEW CALLER into an EXISTING code path, the path carries unstated assumptions about its inputs that the new caller can violate — `GetAncestor` assumed a fully-built index; the resolver didn't know to check. The next instance will have the same shape. **THE STANDING QUESTION this ODC arms (design-review discipline, every feature):** *what assumptions does the path I am newly calling make about its inputs, and does my call site satisfy them?* — asked explicitly at review time for each new edge into shared machinery (validation, index walks, persistence, sentries, caches). **CORROBORATION FROM THE SAME TREE:** three sites (validation.cpp bounded-ancestry, ptx_quorum_store.cpp anchor walk, ptx_formation.cpp canonicity heal) had each independently hand-armored against the unguarded-pskip hazard with deliberate pprev-walks and warning comments — the knowledge existed as COMMENT DISCIPLINE, not as an ENCODED guard, so the fourth caller (the resolver) didn't inherit it. Lesson: when a hazard is worth a warning comment at three call sites, it is worth a guard at the ONE callee (which is what BUG-040's PR #5927 port finally did). **Cross-ref** BUG-037, BUG-039, BUG-040, ODC-054 (the interaction-seam review that caught the Guard-2×gate-off coupling — the same discipline applied prospectively), BUG-029 (whose sentry was one half of BUG-039's seam).

> **[Fourth instance landed 2026-08-18 — BUG-041]:** the BUG-034 P3 template filter (new reader) × CreateNewBlock's inherited one-sentinel reservation (existing path, PoS pushes two txs): the arrays' unstated `size()==vtx.size()` assumption held for every prior consumer because none indexed by vtx position. Same shape, fourth time; the standing question stands. The fix again encodes the invariant at the source (align at creation) rather than armoring the caller alone.

> **★ KDD-087 (decided 2026-08-18) — A TIME BOUND AND A COUNT BOUND OVER THE SAME TICK ARE ONE BOUND; DERIVE THE COUNT SO THE DOCUMENTED CEILING IS THE REAL ONE.** `ptx_fanout.cpp` enforced both `FANOUT_WALL_MS = 30000` and `FANOUT_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 60` from the same 150 ms tick. Ticks are the only clock either check sees, so the two were the SAME bound in different units and the smaller always won: the wall needed tick ~200 and could never fire, while the budget cut every failure at 9.09 s. §5 had justified 30 s as ">2x the worst legitimate ladder observation (14.33 s) — it rejects nothing real"; the code rejected exactly that observation, so the shipped safety margin was dead code and the documentation was false. **DECIDED: `FANOUT_MAX_ATTEMPTS = FANOUT_WALL_MS / FANOUT_RETRY_MS`** (200), making the wall the single authority and preventing the two from drifting apart again; the declaration order was reordered so the derived value follows its operands. ★ **ADOPTED ON MECHANISM, NOT ON THE PASS-RATE DELTA — read this before citing Gen D.** The §9.5 experiment (§10) moved thin d100+d200 from 45/48 to 47/48; that is a SINGLE EVENT and is NOT statistically significant. It is not the reason. The reason is that a documented ceiling which cannot fire is a defect independent of any measurement, and a doomed roll should fail on a real timeout rather than on an arbitrary constant that merely comes first. Supporting evidence that the change is SAFE (not that it is beneficial): the happy path is unmoved — p50 4.72→4.73 and 7.55→7.56, successes closing in 29-51 attempts, never near either ceiling, because stop-at-threshold still returns at the 6th partial. Only a doomed roll pays, ~30 s instead of ~9 s. ★ **THE WALL HAS NOW FIRED** — `wall-clock ceiling 30000ms hit after 199 tick(s) (5 collected, 3 pending, 3 inflight)`, one occurrence fleet-wide, zero "budget exhausted" lines: the ceiling is reachable and authoritative for the first time. ★ **CONSEQUENCE FOR DIRECT-ATTACH (do not over-read):** removing the confound split the failure class — the delay-shaped near-miss tail WAS our own cutoff (both d200 failures gone, the worse RTT rung clean at 24/24), while a residual class survives 30 s with **6 of 11** members never delivering across 199 re-dial opportunities (5 collected, 3 pending + 3 inflight; corrected 2026-08-19 from an initial "3 of 11" that mis-read `pending` as the whole outstanding set), at d100 rather than d200 (**not RTT-shaped**). Direct-attach's target population is isolated for the first time but its §9.6 close condition is NOT met and n=1 is not a build trigger. Full read-out, including what is and is not established, in `doc/ptx/FANOUT_BUDGET_ANALYSIS.md` §10. **Generalisation for the standing audit: two ceilings evaluated from one clock are one ceiling — derive the dependent one or delete it.**

> **★ KDD-088 (decided 2026-08-19) — DIRECT-ATTACH IS A COURIER SWAP, NOT A SECURITY CHANGE: THE ANTI-FREE-PREVIEW PREDICATE WAS ALWAYS *LOCAL MEMPOOL PRESENCE*, NEVER "WAS THIS BROADCAST".** Direct-attach (the commitment rides the sign request) was parked four times, latterly on one question: does it break BUG-032's anti-free-preview property? **It does not, and the reason is stronger than "we found a way to preserve it".** The gate is `PTX_SignRoundIfCommitted` (`ptx/ptx_mempool.cpp:274`) → `PTX_RollCommitmentPresent` (`:252`), which scans **the member's OWN mempool** for a PTXROLLCOMMIT matching (round_seed, quorum_hash). It has never tested that the commitment was broadcast to the network; gossip was only ever the courier that put the bytes where the predicate could pass — `rpc/ptx.cpp:292` states this in its own comment ("gates on seeing this commitment **in their mempool**"). **Attach swaps the courier and leaves the predicate byte-for-byte identical:** today gossip→ATMP→mempool→gate; attached hex→ATMP→mempool→gate. ★ **THE MANDATORY CONDITION: accept-into-mempool, NOT verify-only.** A member that merely parsed and signature-checked the attached bytes would open the exact hole: no UTXO check (inputs could be spent), no relay (the caller really could withhold), no fee policy. `AcceptToMemoryPool` is what makes the fee real, so "well-formed" is insufficient — the attack requires *mempool-valid*, and mempool-valid means paid. ★ **No propagation wait is reintroduced:** ATMP is local and synchronous, needing the commitment's INPUTS in the member's UTXO view, and the builder guarantees those are confirmed coins ("Sign funding inputs (all confirmed → pcoinsTip)") — chain sync, not gossip. ★ **Threat answers:** (a) "collect six partials from a never-broadcast commitment" fails twice over — handing it to eleven members IS publication (each relays on acceptance), and ATMP enforces funded-ness; (b) double-spend-after-partials is UNCHANGED from today and arguably improved (eleven holders relay immediately instead of after multi-hop gossip); the binding remains the coin-chain (the settle spends the commitment's `vout[1]`, BUG-034's no-upper-bound result). ★ **NEW EXPOSURE, designed in not bolted on:** members now run ATMP on **caller-supplied bytes** — a bounded but real DoS surface (size bound + per-peer rate limit + reject-before-ATMP on malformation), in the test matrix from the start. ★★ **TWO PREMISES CORRECTED BY CHECKING RATHER THAN ASSERTING (both had been assumed the other way):** (1) **NOT consensus-adjacent** — the commitment's format, validation, and commit/settle pairing are untouched, and nothing a validator checks depends on how a member *learned* of a transaction, so there is **no `UPGRADE_*` gate, no activation height, no flag day**; it ships as a rolling deploy. (2) **Compatibility is FREE** — `gm_bls_sign` guards with `request.params.size() < 2`, **not** `!= 2` (`rpc/ptx.cpp:546`), so an old member handed a third parameter **ignores it and behaves exactly as today**; with the caller keeping its own broadcast, a mixed fleet needs no negotiation, no feature flag, no fallback. Together these removed the most expensive parts of the work. ★ **Every observed failure class is eliminated by construction, verified against the code:** `PTX_SignRoundIfCommitted` has exactly three failure branches (missing commitment/retryable, no CURRENT `sk_share`, BLS signing failure) and only the first is propagation-related — near-miss, partial non-delivery and total blackout (FANOUT §12.3) are all instances of that one branch. **Justified on LATENCY ALONE** (FANOUT §11: propagation is 79-91% of roll latency; predicted 1.10→~0.10s / 4.71→~0.45s / 8.01→~1.68s, stated to be falsified against a build) so the blackout root-cause cannot unmake the case. Full recon: `doc/ptx/DIRECT_ATTACH_RECON.md`. **Build direct-attach ALONE but structure the call site so KDD-085 (sign-over-P2P) does not require unpicking it.**

> **★ KDD-089 (adopted 2026-08-19) — TWO-LOCATION CONFIG: THE VISIBLE COPY IS NOT NECESSARILY THE AUTHORITATIVE ONE, AND CLEARING THE WRONG ONE LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE PROGRESS.** Removing a node from the PVE cluster requires clearing corosync state that exists in **two** places: `/etc/corosync/` (the obvious one, named for the service) and `/etc/pve/corosync.conf` (the one `pmxcfs` actually binds on). `rm -rf /etc/corosync/*` completed successfully and changed **nothing observable** — `/etc/pve` stayed read-only, `pvecm` still believed it was clustered — because membership is read from the copy inside `/etc/pve`. Removing **either alone leaves the node equally stuck**, and the one that *sounds* authoritative is the one that is not. ★ **THE FAILURE MODE IS DIAGNOSTIC, NOT MECHANICAL:** the dangerous property is not that two copies exist, it is that clearing the non-authoritative copy **returns success**, so the operator books progress and looks elsewhere for the fault. A no-op that exits 0 is worse than an error. ★ **RULE:** before clearing state to change a daemon's behaviour, identify which path the daemon *reads*, not which path is *named after it* — and confirm the change took by observing the behaviour (here: `/etc/pve` becoming writable), never by the exit status of the removal. ★ **SAME SHAPE, ALREADY IN THE RECORD, WHICH IS WHY IT IS REGISTERED:** the pre-guard rows in `mq_attach_evidence.jsonl` (a *derived* file outliving the guard that would have excluded them, read back as valid), `mce_clean()` (a live-window gate read as a health statement), and the §11 stale baselines. Each is one artefact standing in for another that is the real authority. **Two locations, one authority — derive which, or delete one.**

> **★ KDD-090 (adopted 2026-08-19) — A DESTRUCTIVE COMMAND CARRIES ITS OWN TARGET ASSERTION, EVALUATED WHERE IT EXECUTES.** The px1 separation ran `rm -rf`, service stops and a process kill against a host reached over SSH. The hazard was **never SSH targeting** — it was the same command running on **node1**, whose destruction takes the 4-node cluster and the 161-container fleet with it. ★ **Guarding the *destination* does not catch a command executed locally by mistake, pasted into the wrong terminal, or resumed from history on the wrong box.** Every step was therefore prefixed `[ "$(hostname)" = px1 ] || abort`, which is evaluated **on the machine that runs it** and so is correct wherever it runs. ★ **COROLLARY 1 — GATE THE DESTRUCTIVE STEP ON ITS PRECONDITION, NOT ON THE PRIOR STEP'S EXIT CODE.** `pmxcfs -l` can return 0 without `/etc/pve` becoming writable; the `rm` was therefore gated on an **actual write probe** (`touch`/`rm`), exiting 8 if it failed. Same family as KDD-089: verify by behaviour, not by status. ★ **COROLLARY 2 — KILL BY PID.** `pgrep`-then-`kill $PID`, never `killall`/`pkill -f`; `pkill -f` matched and killed the operator's own shell four separate times before `safe_kill.sh` existed. ★ **COROLLARY 3 — AN AMBIGUOUS IMPERATIVE IS RESOLVED BY LOOKING, NOT BY PICKING THE LIKELIER READING.** "`rm -rf /etc/corosync/*` run on px1" parses as both instruction and report. Inspecting the directory's mtime settled it in one command and avoided re-running a destructive step against unre-established state. **The general rule: when a command's blast radius exceeds its scope of intent, the command must assert its own scope.**

> **★ KDD-091 (adopted 2026-08-19) — NEVER TRUST A DERIVED ARTEFACT WHOSE PROVENANCE PREDATES THE FILTER THAT WOULD HAVE REJECTED ITS ROWS.** `mq_attach_evidence.jsonl` is append-only. Its earliest rows were written by a harvest that ran **before that harvest grew its own `quorum_matched` filter**. The current code would never write them; the file keeps them forever; and to every later reader they are **indistinguishable from valid rows**. They read as two rolls at `0/9 in-band, to_first 2.9s` — a coherent, plausible **"total attach blackout class"** that does not exist, and that was one step from being reported as a finding. ★ **THE FIX IS STRUCTURAL, NOT A BLOCKLIST:** `mq_analyse.py` **re-derives validity from `meshhop_results.jsonl` by txid** and never reads validity off the derived file at all. 32 rows in, 2 dropped, 30 used, 7 quorums. ★ **THE GENERAL RULE:** a guard added at time T protects only artefacts produced after T; every artefact produced before T is retroactively unlabelled and **silently retains the appearance of validity**. So when an authority is one join away, **join to it** — do not filter on the derived artefact's own fields, and do not assume a file is clean because the code that writes it is. ★ **This is KDD-089's family seen from the other end**: KDD-089 is two locations where the wrong one answers; this is one location where the *old rows* answer as though the guard had judged them. Prior instances: `mce_clean()` read as a health statement, the §11 stale baselines, the `quorum_matched` flag that set a field but left `ok=True`. **Corollary for retrospective filters: positive-control them.** The first §11 filter ("did the ACTIVE set change?") passed a run that `quorum_matched` had already proved 5-of-8 stale.

> **★ KDD-092 (adopted 2026-08-19) — V11 IS ACTIVATION-GATED ON THE CONNECT HEIGHT, AND THE GATE DEFAULTS TO ZERO.** V11 requires a PTXDKG's formation anchor to sit on the boundary schedule; the predicate reads `nBoundaryInterval`, so **changing the cadence on a chain with history retroactively invalidates every PTXDKG anchored under the old cadence** and a node syncing from genesis rejects blocks the network accepted — **split-on-resync, the h385 shape**. Un-gated was correct only while the fleet was disposable; a testnet is the moment that stops being true. ★ **New field `PTXFormationParams::nBoundaryEnforceHeight`, added LAST in the struct** — the aggregate initialiser is **positional**, so inserting anywhere else would silently reassign every existing chainparams value (the grep trap the header already warns about). ★ **GATED ON THE HEIGHT OF THE BLOCK BEING CONNECTED, NEVER THE ANCHOR'S HEIGHT.** Gating on the anchor would let a **NEW** off-boundary PTXDKG in simply by naming an **OLD** anchor — the gate would be bypassable by choice of input rather than partitioning time. ★ **0 = enforce from genesis, and 0 is the value on every network today**, so the change is behaviour-preserving everywhere and ptxbea's replay stays byte-identical (it has enforced V11 since its fresh genesis). The decision is extracted into a **named predicate**, `PTX_Formation_BoundaryRequiredAt`, rather than a bare `>=` inline in `CheckPTXDKGTx`, so the activation rule is greppable. **Proven by RED**: an off-by-one (`>` for `>=`) fails exactly the two rows that assert enforcement AT the activation height, and no others. Suite 483/483.

> **★ ODC-075 (opened 2026-08-19) — QUORUM SIZE 11 AND THRESHOLD t=6 ARE UN-GATED CONSENSUS CONSTANTS, AND THE FIVE-OPERATOR TESTNET CREATES PRESSURE TO CHANGE EXACTLY THEM.** The KDD-092 sweep asked what else would retroactively invalidate history. `PTX_Formation_IsBoundary` is the **only** consensus reader of `ptxFormation` (the other four call sites are startup validation, ceremony deadlines and the store driver) — but **11 and t=6 are hardcoded literals in the consensus path**: `specialtx_validation.cpp` rejects `member_node_ids.size() > 11` and `premit_commitments.size() < t` at DoS 100, and `ptx_formation.cpp:92` makes `pool < 11` a **deterministic skip**. Change either on a chain with history and every historical PTXDKG re-validates differently — **the identical split-on-resync class KDD-092 just closed for the cadence.** ★ **AND THE PRESSURE IS ALREADY THERE, AS ARITHMETIC:** a quorum needs **11** members; the planned testnet has **five operators**. Five operators running one node each is **five GMs — formation never fires, and the network produces zero quorums, zero rolls and zero settlements while looking entirely healthy.** Three resolutions: **(A)** ≥3 GMs per operator (5×3 = 15, four spare) — **recommended, touches no consensus constant**; **(B)** parameterise 11/t — **must ship with an activation height in the same commit**; **(C)** recruit operators. **On a fresh genesis the constants are correct as they stand; gating is required only once history exists.** Full analysis: `doc/ptx/PTX_TESTNET_GENESIS_CONFIG.md` §0.

> **★ KDD-093 (adopted 2026-08-19) — WHEN TESTING A FAILURE PATH, CHECK THE EXIT CODE OF THE THING UNDER TEST, NOT OF WHATEVER THE PIPELINE ENDS WITH.** `./self-check.sh | head -12` reported **exit 0** for a script that correctly exits 1: `$?` was **`head`'s** status. Re-run without the pipe, it is 1. ★ **THIRD INSTANCE OF ONE SHAPE — a tool reporting a different command's verdict than the one being tested:** (1) `grep -c` **prints 0 and exits 1** on no-match, so `grep -c … || echo 0` returns `"0\n0"` and fails every numeric test silently — it wrote ALL-DONE without measuring anything; (2) `findmnt` given **two** targets returns **nothing** (it takes one), which read as "both mounts failed after reboot" when `df` showed both mounted; (3) this one. ★ **The common root is not "shell is tricky": it is that the OBSERVING instrument sits between the SUBJECT and the VERDICT, and its own failure mode is indistinguishable from the subject's.** `head` succeeding looks like the script succeeding; `findmnt`'s misuse looks like an absent mount; `grep -c`'s no-match exit looks like a failed count. ★ **RULES:** capture the status of the subject **directly** (`cmd; rc=$?` before any pipe, or `set -o pipefail`, or redirect to a file and inspect separately); prefer `awk` over `grep -c` for counting; give single-target tools one target. **And the generalisation beyond shell — the same rule that produced the mq_analyse fix (KDD-091) and the mask-not-disable finding (KDD-090): when an instrument can fail in the same shape as the thing it measures, read the subject directly or make the instrument's failure loud.**

> **★ KDD-094 (adopted 2026-08-19) — A DIAGNOSTIC HAS THREE OUTCOMES, NOT TWO: PASSED, FAILED, AND **COULD NOT RUN** — AND THE THIRD NEEDS ITS OWN EXIT CODE.** `self-check.sh` reported **"2 passed, 0 failed … No failures"**, exit 0, on a px1 node with **nothing bound, no listener, and a stranger's PoSe score on screen**. After the fix, on the same unchanged node: **5 passed, 4 FAILED**. Three independent defects stacked — (1) it parsed `"addr"`, which **does not exist** on a deterministic GM's `getgamemasterstatus` (`CDeterministicGM::ToJson` nests under `dgmstate` and names it **`service`**), and which on the *legacy* branch is a **payment address**, so the bug's other failure mode is feeding a wallet address to a TCP probe; (2) `ptx_gm_pose` was called bare although `src/rpc/ptx.cpp` throws on `params.size() != 1`, so it always errored into a fallback; (3) that fallback read the **first** entry of `ptx_pose_status`, which lists **every GM on the network** — **someone else's score, presented as yours** — and surfaced only because the regex `pose_?score` used a **BRE**, where `?` is a literal and matched nothing. ★ **Fixing the regex alone would have made the script confidently report a stranger's health: a bug hidden by a second bug in the same block is not a mitigated bug.** ★ **BUT THE ROOT CAUSE IS THE OUTCOME MODEL, NOT ANY OF THE THREE.** Every failure above emitted a `[warn]`, and **warns do not move the exit code** — so the script's verdict line and its status agreed with each other and disagreed with reality. **A check that could not obtain its evidence has not passed.** Load-bearing sections now emit `[????]`, counted separately, named in the verdict (*"an unrun check is not a passed check"*), and **exit 2**: **0** = all ran and passed · **1** = something failed · **2** = nothing failed but something could not be checked. ★ **FOURTH INSTANCE OF FLAG-NOT-A-GUARD** — `mce_clean()` read as a health statement, the cap-6/6 display, `quorum_matched` setting a field while leaving `ok=True`, and now a warning that does not bind the exit code. **The general rule: an outcome that does not propagate to the caller's decision variable is a comment, not a result.**
>

> **★ KDD-095 (adopted 2026-08-19) — SIZE A WORKLOAD'S HEADROOM FROM ITS MEASURED ABSOLUTE DEMAND; THE HOST'S NEED DOES NOT SCALE WITH INSTALLED RAM.** The fleet bring-up guarded on `MemTotal/4`. On a 64 GB box that was a 16 GiB floor under a fleet needing ~30 GiB — 28 GiB of slack, so **the guard never once bound and nobody learned it was mis-derived**. When the box dropped to 40 GB the same absolute workload landed ~6 GiB clear of a now-10 GiB floor and the bring-up stalled at 140 of 153 GMs. Same fleet, same host need, opposite verdicts: **the ratio tracked the wrong quantity.** ★ **Corollary 1 — a floor derived from a PREFLIGHT SNAPSHOT mis-sizes a workload that GROWS after it starts.** The batch deltas implied 162 MiB/process; the *running* fleet read 171 MiB and had grown ~1.4 GiB in the 28 minutes after bring-up, which put the landing under a floor computed minutes earlier. **Measure the running thing, not the moment it was launched.** ★ **Corollary 2 — at tight headroom PSI is the real guard, not free-page count.** Free pages cannot distinguish "comfortable" from "one allocation short". Demonstrated the same hour: the host read 4473 MiB available — *below* the new floor — with 1140 MiB swapped, while `pressure/memory` reported `some avg10=0.00`, `full avg10=0.00`, zero OOM kills, 161/161 containers up. **The free-page count said distress; the stall counter said there was none, and the stall counter was right.** ★ Third instance of the same shape after KDD-087 (two ceilings on one clock) and the `-j$(nproc)` build limit: **a threshold derived from a quantity that has no causal relation to the constraint it guards.**

> **★★ KDD-096 (adopted 2026-08-19) — CONTAINERISING A WORKLOAD DOES NOT MOVE ITS HOST-SIDE SCAFFOLDING.** All 161 fleet containers were pinned off a suspect CPU core and **verified three independent ways** — `docker inspect` config 161/161, kernel `cpuset.cpus.effective` 161/161, and `sched_getaffinity` on every running main pid 161/161. Then the load was measured: the excluded core ran at **8.72%/10.60%** against neighbours at **8.47%/8.07%** and **6.18%/5.53%** — **at or ABOVE its neighbours.** A cgroup constrains the container and nothing else; the scaffolding — **161 `containerd-shim` + 161 `docker-proxy`, ~322 processes**, plus `dockerd`, `containerd`, the hypervisor daemons, the KVM guests and the monitoring writers — lives in `system.slice`/`user.slice`/`qemu.slice`, untouched. ★ **At this container count the scaffolding is comparable in size to the workload itself, so anyone reasoning about "the fleet's CPU footprint" from container stats alone is wrong by a large factor — `docker stats` cannot see half of it.** ★ **The narrow form is what caught it: VERIFYING THAT THE KNOB WAS SET IS NOT VERIFYING THAT IT DID ANYTHING.** Three perfect postcondition checks agreed with each other and none of them was the postcondition that mattered — flag-not-a-guard *inverted*: not a guard that fails to bind, but **a postcondition that binds perfectly and is the wrong one**. The check that mattered measured **the quantity the intervention exists to change**. ★ The diagnosis also named the remedy and is worth keeping even though the trial was retired unrun: `irq` at 0.00% and `softirq` at ~0.05% proved the residual was ordinary user+sys time and therefore cgroup-constrainable, so slice-level `AllowedCPUs` would have worked with no `isolcpus` and no reboot. **The method was sound; only the value of the answer was gone.**

> **★ KDD-097 (adopted 2026-08-19) — A `.gitignore` RULE AND THE INDEX DISAGREE SILENTLY, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** An ignore rule has **no effect on a file that is already tracked**. Discovered while fixing the `-dirty` defect: of the 29 autotools artefacts that every build rewrites, **28 were already listed in `.gitignore`** — `Makefile.in` (`:41`), `aclocal.m4` (`:42`), `autom4te.cache/*` (`:43`), `configure` (`:59`), `src/config/Hemis-config.h.in` (`:65`), `*.old` (`:86`), and the whole `build-aux/` tree via an over-broad `build*` (`:174`). The intent to untrack them predates us by the life of the repo; **the rules have been inert the entire time because the files were committed anyway**, and the cost was not cosmetic — a dirty tree makes `share/genbuild.sh:32-34` drop the tag name and emit `<sha>-dirty`, so **the release binary could never have named its own tag.** ★ **The mirror image is a live trap in the same file.** `build*` (`:174`) also matches **18 maintained macros** — `build-aux/m4/ax_*.m4`, `bitcoin_find_bdb48.m4`, `bitcoin_qt.m4`, `bitcoin_subdir_to_include.m4`, `gmp.m4`, `l_atomic.m4`, `l_socket.m4` — which are *authored*, not generated. They survive **only because they are in the index**: delete one and `git add` will not bring it back, silently, with no failing test, invisible until someone loses a macro and `./configure` fails on a machine that used to work. ★ **Generalisation: `.gitignore` states an intention; the index states the fact, and neither reports the disagreement.** Ask which files a rule is *supposed* to cover and which it *actually* covers, in both directions — the same family as flag-not-a-guard (KDD-096) and `mce_clean()`: **a rule that expresses intent without binding is a note, not a guard.** ★ Method note that caught the second half: the untracking set was scoped by **what the build actually rewrote** (`git status` against a built tree), not by directory. A directory sweep of `build-aux/` would have untracked all 18 maintained macros in the same commit that was meant to protect the tree.

> **★ KDD-098 (adopted 2026-08-20) — `fail-fast: false` DOES NOT MAKE MATRIX LEGS INDEPENDENT; `needs:` IS A WHOLE-MATRIX BARRIER.** Both matrices in `.github/workflows/build-and-release.yml` set `fail-fast: false` (`:65` on `build_depends`, `:162` on `build_depends_wallet`), which reads as a declaration that the legs are independent. It is not. `fail-fast` binds exactly one thing — **whether a failing leg cancels its siblings** — and says nothing about *waiting*. Ordering is bound by a different knob entirely: `:149`'s `needs: [lint, build_depends]` depends on the **job**, and a matrix job is not complete until **every** leg of it is. So the x86_64 Linux wallet leg cannot start until all five depends legs finish, including the four whose `depends/` output it will never read. ★ **The instance, measured.** In run #1 the Linux depends leg completed and its wallet leg then sat **5h38m** behind irrelevant siblings, until the whole run hit the 6h repository timeout and was cancelled — **taking the finished Linux result with it**. `fail-fast: false` was set the entire time and prevented nothing, because nothing had failed; the run died of waiting, which is the axis that was never configured. ★ **Generalisation — the KDD-097 family, one turn further: a setting states an intention the mechanism does not deliver, and nothing reports the disagreement.** `.gitignore` states an intention and the index states the fact; `fail-fast: false` states an intention and `needs:` states the fact. In both cases the declaration is *locally true* and *globally irrelevant, and reads to a maintainer as though the concern is handled.* Ask what a setting **binds**, not what its name implies — and when two knobs sit on the same apparent concern, find out which axis each one is actually on. ★ **Why the remedy is matrix restriction and not a finer dependency.** GitHub Actions has no per-matrix-leg `needs:` — a job cannot depend on one leg of an upstream matrix. The only ways to buy real per-host independence are to split the matrix into separate jobs per host, or to stop running the hosts you do not need. For v0.1.0-testnet we took the second (see the markers at `:68` and `:165`); the first remains the answer if all five platforms are wanted concurrently.

> **★ KDD-099 (adopted 2026-08-20) — DEFERRING A PLATFORM COSTS ONE CHARACTER; RESTORING IT DOES NOT. A REVERSIBLE-LOOKING CHANGE IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY A REVERSIBLE ONE.** When the release matrices were cut to linux-only for v0.1.0-testnet, the four other legs were **commented out rather than deleted**, each matrix carrying a marker whose restore instruction was literally *"delete the leading `# `"* — chosen precisely so the platforms would read as **deferred, not dropped**. ★ **That instruction was true for one of the four legs and false for two.** The blst dist sweep (BUG-043) established that the vendored tree carries x86_64 assembly for **ELF and COFF only**: `src/blst/build/` contains `elf/` (14 files) and `coff/` (14 files), **no `mach-o/` directory at all**, and **no `*-armv8.*` file under any ABI**. `assembly.S` dispatches on the platform macros and hard-fails off the end — `#error "unsupported platform"` (`:139`). So: **Win64 restores by deleting a `#`** (`coff/` is present, and BUG-043's fix now ships it); **ARM 32-bit restores by deleting a `#`** (32-bit takes the deliberately empty branch at `:136-138` and falls back to pure C — with the runtime stack caveat `src/Makefile.am:113` documents); but **macOS and AARCH64 cannot be restored by deleting a `#` at all**, because the files their branches `#include` **do not exist in this repository**. ★ **Generalisation: when you defer something, the cost of undoing it is a separate question from the cost of doing it, and it is not answered by the mechanism you used to defer.** A one-character deferral invites the assumption of a one-character restoration; nothing checks that assumption, and the marker I wrote asserted it. **Establish the restore path at the moment you defer, while the context is loaded — not in three weeks when someone uncomments a line and gets a compile error they have no context for.** ★ Same family as KDD-098 and KDD-097 in the specific sense that **a note asserted something the mechanism did not deliver** — but the failure mode is the mirror image: those were settings that promised more than they bound; this was **documentation that promised a reversal the repository could not perform.** ★ **Acted on, not just recorded:** the workflow's restore markers now name BUG-043 and say which legs are one-character restores and which are not.

> **★ KDD-100 (adopted 2026-08-21) — A TEST THAT STOPS WHERE THE SYSTEM STARTS IS NOT AN END-TO-END TEST, AND ALL FOUR DEFECTS LIVED IN THE FIRST INCH PAST ITS LAST ASSERTION.** `testnet/operator/install.sh`'s end-to-end test ran the installer to completion and asserted everything it produced: the binaries are present and run, the Sapling parameters match by sha256, the port reservation is active in the kernel, the config file exists with mode 600. It then stopped, at "installed and configured", **without ever starting a daemon.** Four defects were found on 2026-08-21 by a run that did start one, and every one of them is a startup effect: the config was written **`hemis.conf`** where the daemon reads **`Hemis.conf`** (`util/system.cpp:81`), so every installed node ran **mainnet** on default ports (`f37bf34`); `port`/`rpcport`/`rpcbind` sat **above** the `[ptxtestnet]` header, so the daemon dropped them and bound the ptxtestnet defaults (`e414e77`); `gamemaster=1` shipped enabled with no key, which is not a degraded mode but a refusal to start — and since `generateblskeypair` is an RPC call, a lockout from the daemon needed to produce the key (`e414e77`); and GMs 2 and 3 died on a port collision that GM 1's healthy `getblockcount` completely masked (`f37bf34`). ★ **The generalisation. Config, chain parameters, node role and port bindings are not exercised by writing files; they are exercised by BINDING SOCKETS.** A test that asserts the artefacts and stops has verified the half of the system that cannot fail interestingly. Ask where the assertions end, then ask what happens in the next inch — because that is where an untested seam is, by construction. ★ **Same family as KDD-097/098/099** — something declared an intention the mechanism did not deliver — but with the failure mode turned inside out: those were a *setting* or a *note* that over-promised; this was **a test whose coverage boundary nobody had stated out loud**, so its silence read as coverage. ★ **Acted on:** `testnet/operator/install-test.sh` starts three daemons and judges them on four outcomes — datadir layout for the chain, the kernel's socket table for the ports, credential authentication for the config read, and per-PID liveness with pairwise-disjoint ports for the fleet. ★ **Anti-vacuity is the load-bearing half, and it is a rule, not a flourish: a check that reads the daemon's own account of itself shares the defect being hunted.** `getblockcount` answered with a plausible height on a node silently synchronising mainnet; `getnetworkinfo` would have reported the ports it *meant* to bind; the log line `Using config file …/Hemis.conf` is printed from the path the daemon INTENDS to open and named a file that was not there. Every check observes what the node DID. Where RPC is unavoidable it is used only as a BARRIER ("are you finished initialising?") — a question whose answer is not among the things that can be wrong. ★ **Each of the four checks has been seen to FAIL against a reconstruction of the defect it names** (four RED mutations of the installer's own output), because a check that has never failed is not yet a check. ★ **Coda, and it is the part worth keeping: the new test's OWN first runs carried four defects of exactly this family**, and each reported a healthy system as broken or the reverse. (i) It settled on the FIRST listening socket and so measured a daemon mid-startup, reporting "configured P2P 29994 is NOT bound" about a node that bound it eight seconds later — startup has a PLATEAU (RPC early, then ~8s of "Creating HD Wallet", then the P2P bind), and a plateau is indistinguishable from an endpoint if all you watch is the set. (ii) It treated a ZOMBIE child as alive — `/proc/<pid>` survives and `kill -0` succeeds until `wait()` reaps — and so reported a daemon that died on startup as running, declaring its own RED leg vacuous when the leg was fine. (iii) It ran `getblockcount` through RPC warm-up and read `RPC_IN_WARMUP` as an authentication failure. (iv) ★ **And the sharpest one: `producer | grep -q needle` under `set -o pipefail` is a RACE that silently inverts a successful match.** `grep -q` exits on first match, closing the pipe; the producer dies of SIGPIPE (141); pipefail reports 141 as the pipeline's status. The host-state probe written that way returned "free" for **every** port, including 22 and including the 29902 a `docker-proxy` had held continuously for two days with **zero restarts** — so the RED leg printed *"default port 29902: free, checked just now — the exit is the defect, not the host"*, which was exactly backwards, and the check that was supposed to keep the test honest about its environment was the one lying about it. **The repository already knew about this trap** — `install.sh` section 3 uses `find -print -quit` rather than `find | head -1` and its comment names "the same SIGPIPE trap as the glibc check". Knowing a trap is not the same as not walking into it; the fix is a form that HAS no pipeline, not a more careful pipeline. ★ **Writing the test does not exempt the test.** Run it against a known-bad state before believing it about a good one — every one of these four was found that way, and none of them by reading the code.

> **★ KDD-101 (adopted 2026-08-21) — A DEFECT WHOSE VISIBILITY DEPENDS ON THE TEST ENVIRONMENT WILL COME BACK, BECAUSE NOTHING CHANGED IN THE CODE BETWEEN THE RUN THAT HID IT AND THE RUN THAT SHOWED IT.** The port-reservation section had two branches that disagreed about the same failure: with `procps` present it called `die()` with "the file is malformed"; without it, it returned 1 and let the caller print the correct diagnosis. **Which message an operator got depended on whether a package happened to be installed** (`d76224e`). It passed on 2026-08-19 in a container without `procps` and failed on 2026-08-21 in one with it — **the code was identical on both days.** ★ **The rule: any property of the test host that the result depends on must be MEASURED AND PRINTED by the test, every run.** An undeclared dependency on the environment turns a pass into a statement about the machine rather than the software, and nothing in the output distinguishes the two. ★ **Immediately confirmed, inside the fix for it.** `install-test.sh` was given a pre-flight that measures and prints exactly this. On its first run the pre-flight reported ptxtestnet's default RPC port 29902 **free** at 15:38:14; the RED leg that needs a mis-configured daemon to fall back onto that port found it **unbindable** at 15:39:16 — a container on the host had restarted and its `docker-proxy` had retaken the port **inside the test's own run**. The RED leg still failed, as required, but **for the wrong reason**, and a one-shot pre-flight would have reported the wrong one. ★ **So the rule has a second half: a pre-flight is a claim about the PAST.** Re-measure at the moment the answer is used, and print the finding on *both* branches — printing only when something is wrong leaves silence meaning two different things ("checked, all clear" and "never checked") that a later reader cannot tell apart. Same shape as the `[warn]`-that-was-not-a-`[FAIL]` which once let `self-check.sh` report a dead node as passing.

> **★ ODC-076 (raised AND CLOSED 2026-08-21) — THE SHIPPED CONFIG'S DUAL-STACK `rpcbind` PAIR FAILS ITS SECOND BIND ON EVERY START, AND IPv6 IS NOT BOUND.** ★★ **RESOLVED THE SAME DAY, AND NOT IN THE DIRECTION THE ORIGINAL ENTRY GUESSED.** The entry below declined to change a shipped `rpcbind` on container-only evidence, on the grounds that a container with no global IPv6 might be the whole explanation. **It was not.** Re-tested on `ptx01` — a fresh operator VM on VLAN 2 with a real global address (`2a07:244:46:6400:…/128`), running this tag's own `install.sh` output: `Binding RPC on address :: port 29995 failed.`, and the kernel's socket table joined to the PID by inode shows **29995 in `/proc/<pid>/net/tcp` and absent from `/proc/<pid>/net/tcp6`**, while P2P 29994 binds *both* families in the same process. So the defect is real, host-independent, and **launch-critical on an IPv6-reachable network**: VLAN 2's IPv4 side is RFC1918, so an IPv4-only RPC listener is one no peer can reach — quorums form and nobody signs. ★ **Fixed by binding explicitly rather than by choosing a wildcard.** `install.sh` section 5 now emits one `rpcbind=` line per global address on the host (falling back to the wildcard pair with a warning if none is found). Explicit per-address binds cannot collide with one another, which is precisely what the `0.0.0.0` + `::` pair did on a `bindv6only=0` kernel. That also removes the wildcard exposure the same section was criticised for. ★ **Lesson worth keeping: "it might be the test environment" was the right caution and the wrong conclusion.** The correct move was neither to fix on one container's evidence nor to leave it open indefinitely, but to *name the vantage point that would settle it* — and then test there the moment one existed. `install.sh` section 5 emits both `rpcbind=0.0.0.0` and `rpcbind=::`, commented as dual-stack, with the entirely correct observation that binding one family only is a silent failure mode — a node whose peers dial the family it is not listening on is selected, never contacted, and never signs. **Measured on all three GMs of the `install-test.sh` green run, 2026-08-21:** `Binding RPC on address :: port 29995 failed.`, and the RPC port appears in `/proc/net/tcp` but **not** `/proc/net/tcp6` — i.e. IPv4 only. The cause is that on Linux with the default `net.ipv6.bindv6only=0` a `::` wildcard **already covers IPv4**, so whichever of the pair binds second collides with the first. This is the second host on which it has been observed. ★ **DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED HERE.** Changing a shipped `rpcbind` is a change to the one setting whose failure mode costs the testnet its quorum, and the two obvious repairs are not equivalent: dropping `0.0.0.0` relies on `bindv6only=0`, which is a host setting an operator can change and some hardened images do; dropping `::` gives up IPv6 explicitly rather than by accident. The evidence for choosing between them is a real host with real peers, which is what `self-check.sh` section 4 is for. ★ **What HAS changed is that it is now measured rather than rediscovered:** `install-test.sh` reports the bound families of every green GM on every run, as an observation that does not gate the verdict.

> **★ ODC-077 (decided 2026-08-21) — `quorumdkgsimerror` STAYS OPEN ON THE PUBLIC TESTNET, AS A DECISION.** `rpc/rpcquorums.cpp:362` gates DKG error injection (`"justify-lie" 0.1` and friends) on `Params().IsTestChain()`, and `IsTestChain()` (`chainparams.h:81`) is `IsTestnet() || IsRegTestNet() || IsPTXTestNet() || IsPTXBeaTestNet()` — **four networks wide, and ptxtestnet is one of them**. So an authenticated operator on the public testnet can make their own node lie during a ceremony. Three siblings sit behind the same gate: `signsession` (`:21`), `hasrecoverysignature` (`:50`), `issessionconflicting` (`:79`). ★ **Left open on purpose.** Exercising failure modes with known, cooperating parties is a large part of what this testnet is for, and the blast radius is bounded — a node can only degrade its own participation and its own PoSe score, and the DIVERGENCE FLOOR (`ptx_ceremony_driver.h:44`) means under-width delivery aborts rather than finalising divergently. ★ **What is owed, and it is the whole reason this is recorded rather than left implicit: `self-check.sh` must report when error injection is active on that node.** A stuck DKG that is actually someone's simulated fault, misdiagnosed as a bug, costs a day — and it is indistinguishable from a real stall in every other output. Same family as the `[????]` outcome self-check already carries: a state that is neither pass nor fail needs its own line. ★ Note the gate's own error text says *"command available only for RegTest and TestNet network"*, which **describes a narrower gate than the code implements** — a small instance of the KDD-097 family (a message asserting something the mechanism does not deliver). Before mainnet the gate must become `IsRegTestNet() || IsPTXBeaTestNet()`, matching the three `rpc/ptx.cpp` debug RPCs.

> **★ KDD-102 (adopted 2026-08-24) — "THERE IS A PRECEDENT IN THE CODEBASE" ESTABLISHES THAT AN ORDER EXISTS, NOT THAT IT IS CORRECT.** Raised by BUG-048, but it generalises well past locks and is recorded here rather than inside that row. The settle block takes `cs_main -> mempool.cs -> cs_wallet` (`src/ptx/ptx_mempool.cpp:246-247`), and every lock in it is taken somewhere else in this tree in some order — so a precedent search returns hits and the sequence looks sanctioned. What a precedent search does **not** return is the *invariant*: 92 canonical `LOCK2(cs_main, cs_wallet)` sites establish `cs_main -> cs_wallet` before the mempool, and the settle block inverts the last two. ★ **A neighbour proves a pattern compiled and ran; only the invariant proves it is correct.** The failure mode is that the search feels like verification and terminates the enquiry — the same shape as BUG-022, where `AddDGMEntryToList` was the sole outlier against those same 92 sites. Cite what the code must be consistent WITH, not what it resembles. ★ **Corollary, and the reason BUG-048 and BUG-049 are filed as a pair: a build configuration that skips a check is indistinguishable from a check that passes.** The fleet binary carries no `DEBUG_LOCKORDER` and the release carries `--disable-tests`; in both cases the absence of a complaint was read as the absence of a problem. **Cross-ref:** BUG-048, BUG-049, BUG-022, ODC-078.

> **★ ODC-078 (raised 2026-08-24) — `-zapwallettxes` LEAVES BEHIND THE FILE THAT UNDOES IT, AND THE FLAG THAT MAKES ZAP SAFE IS WHY.** A zap is supposed to be the strong reset: delete the wallet's transactions, let them be re-derived from the chain. It did not stick on the first attempt across the 8 caller wallets (2026-08-24, fleet recovery), and the mechanism is a composition, not a bug in either half. `src/wallet/init.cpp:76-79` soft-sets `-persistmempool=false` whenever `-zapwallettxes` is given — *"parameter interaction: -zapwallettxes=1 -> setting -persistmempool=0"* — so the **zap container neither loads `mempool.dat` nor rewrites it**. But the container it replaced wrote that file on its own clean shutdown, and the next NORMAL start loads it: the poisoned transactions re-enter the mempool and `AddToWalletIfInvolvingMe` re-teaches the freshly-zapped wallet its own history. ★ **The very flag that makes zap safe — not persisting the mempool — is what leaves the stale file untouched for the next start to reload.** Working sequence, proven on the second attempt: **stop -> delete `mempool.dat` -> zap -> delete again -> start.** The second delete is not belt-and-braces: the zap container is running a daemon, and anything it or a peer relays in can re-create the file before it exits. **Cross-ref:** KDD-102 (the composition is invisible to a precedent search of either half), BUG-024 (the mempool.dat-persisted PTXSESS is the same file doing the same thing at a different arc).

> **★ ODC-079 (raised 2026-08-24) — THE FLEET'S SHARED RPC CREDENTIAL IS ALREADY IN THE PUBLIC REPOSITORY, IN TRACKED FILES, AND WAS BEFORE THIS WEEK.** Found by the pre-push secret scan, which is why it is recorded even though it blocks nothing. `ptxw2rpc` / `ptxw2pass2026` appears as a hard-coded default in at least eight tracked files under `testnet/w2fleet/ops/` — e.g. `bug034_detector.py:20` (`CLI = ["Hemis-cli", "-ptxbea", "-rpcuser=ptxw2rpc", "-rpcpassword=ptxw2pass2026"]`) — and `abort_track.py`, `latency_battery.py`, `verdict_compare.py`, `kill_replay_check.sh`, `netem_mesh.sh`, `latency_ladder.sh`, `ladder_ckpt.sh`, `persistence_survival_check.sh`. ★ **The exposure is bounded and was accepted implicitly, not decided:** these RPC ports are bound inside a private Docker network on a single host, the coins are testnet, and the credential guards nothing an attacker could reach without already being on the box. ★ **What matters is that it must never become the pattern for an OPERATOR-facing file.** The pre-push scan confirms it has not: `ptxw2rpc` appears in **none** of the five commits' files, `cold-sync-test.sh:55-56` requires `PTX_REF_USER`/`PTX_REF_PASS` via `${...:?}` with **no default**, and the only literals in operator docs are throwaway localhost-only pairs (`cs`/`cs` for an ephemeral probe datadir; `sporkgen`/`sporkgen` in `ONBOARDING.md:126-127`, bound `127.0.0.1`, disposable datadir). ★★ **DECIDED 2026-09-03 — ACCEPTED, NOT SCRUBBED, AND THE DECIDING EVIDENCE IS A MEASUREMENT.** The credential now appears in **37 tracked files**, 32 of them under `testnet/w2fleet/`, and in **zero** operator-facing files — the boundary this row said mattered has held. ★ **The question that actually decides severity is whether anything non-disposable inherited it, and nothing has:** all seven launch hosts (ptx001-005, ptx01, exp01) run `rpcuser=ptxop` with a **64-character generated** password; none carries `ptxw2pass2026`. Checked on each host against its own config, not inferred from the template. ★★ **Not scrubbed, deliberately, and the reasoning is about risk rather than tidiness:** it is permanently in git history, so removal buys nothing for what is already published — and **rewriting history on a repository five external operators are about to clone is a materially larger risk than the exposure it would remove.** A rewritten history breaks every clone, every pinned tag and every checksum an operator has been told to verify. ★ **What the acceptance is conditional on, stated so it can be checked later:** this credential is for the disposable fleet on a private Docker network and **must never be reused outside it** — not on a launch host, not in an operator document, not in any config that survives `docker compose down`.
