The RPC works fine for a chain that does not use PTX identities, which is why it was inherited without a mark against it. ★ **The doc-side origin, and it is worth keeping:** `doc/ptx/ptxbea-operator-guide.md` §2.1 listed the parameters of **`protx_register`** (`collateralHash`, `collateralIndex`) under the heading **`protx_register_fund`** — a signature that does not exist, since `_fund` takes a `collateralAddress` and no index at all. **Every downstream guide inherited the wrong RPC name from a correct parameter list.** The mislabelling was invisible to anyone who read the arguments rather than the title. ★★ **DOCS FIXED, CODE NOT — deliberately.** All four operator-facing sites now specify `protx_register` with a `sendtoaddress`-funded, confirmed collateral: `OPERATOR_GUIDE.md` §B2 (rewritten, eleven-argument table), `GENESIS_BOOTSTRAP.md:479`, `ptxbea-operator-guide.md` §2.1/§2.4/step 4, and the `/register` walkthrough. **The code fix is a consensus-adjacent change to `ProTxRegisterFund` and is not being made before launch** — the working path exists, is documented, and needs no rebuild. ★ **The real fix, when it is made, is to reject rather than to repair:** `_fund` should refuse a non-empty `ptxNodeId` up front with a message naming `protx_register`, because a two-pass hash that stabilises the suffix would be a consensus change for an RPC nobody needs on this chain. ★ **Same family as ODC-081 and KDD-104** — *a true reading of the wrong subject*: the parameter list was accurate, and it was accurate about a different function.

> **★ ODC-100 (raised 2026-09-03) — `nNewGMBlockReward` GOES LIVE THE MOMENT `SPORK_7` IS FLIPPED, AND IT IS A CHAINPARAMS VALUE, SO CONFIRMING IT AFTERWARDS IS TOO LATE.** The inherited-features work changed it from `6 * COIN` to ptxbea's `2.674999995 * COIN` (empirically confirmed at `2c9b26d`: 267,499,999 / +2.14). ★ **The gap is not the value, it is the verification order.** Nothing has confirmed that the binaries **currently running on the launch hosts** carry that value, and a chainparams constant cannot be corrected by a spork, a config edit or an RPC — only by a rebuild, a redeploy and a restart of every node. Flipping `SPORK_7` against a fleet running the wrong constant means gamemaster payments begin at the wrong rate on a live chain. ★ **The check is cheap and must happen BEFORE the flip:** confirm the deployed binaries' reward constant on each host, not the tag they were built from — **BUG-055 is the standing proof that the right tag does not imply the right running state.** ★ **Trigger: immediately before `SPORK_7` is enabled.** Not a launch blocker in itself; a blocker on the flip.

> **★ ODC-101 (raised 2026-09-03) — THE PAYMENTS-ON PATH HAS NEVER BEEN EXERCISED ON THE FLEET, AND THE LAUNCH SET PAYS EACH GAMEMASTER ~7.6× MORE OFTEN THAN THE FLEET WOULD HAVE.** All fleet work to date ran with gamemaster payments off. ★ **The arithmetic that makes it worth registering:** at ~20 registered gamemasters on a one-per-block rotation each is paid roughly every **20 blocks**; the 153-GM fleet rotation would have paid each every ~153. The launch configuration therefore produces payment events at **~7.6× the rate** any prior run has seen. ★ **What is actually unmeasured is wallet fragmentation**, not consensus: each payment is a fresh small UTXO in the payout wallet, and at that cadence a payout wallet accumulates outputs faster than anything has been observed doing. The known consequences are slow `listunspent`, slow coin selection, and fee inflation on spends — none catastrophic, all previously unobserved here. ★ **Explicitly NOT a claim that something is wrong** — it is a claim that nobody has looked, and that the first place it would show is an operator's wallet rather than the chain. ★ **Trigger: the first sustained period with `SPORK_7` on.** Watch payout-wallet UTXO counts on the coordinator's own gamemasters, which run the documented configuration and are the fleet's stand-in.

> **★ BUG-058 (raised 2026-09-03, CLOSED — NOT A DEFECT).** Raised as *"`OPERATOR_GUIDE.md` still tells operators to run `protx_update_service` for PoSe recovery, which rejects a banned GM."* **Investigated against the released `v0.3.1-testnet` binary and the source; the guide is correct and was left unchanged.** ★ `protx_update_service "proTxHash" "ipAndPort" ("operatorPayoutAddress" "operatorKey")` takes the BLS secret as **argument 4**, and `GetBLSSecretKey` (`rpcevo.cpp:250`) parses an explicitly-supplied key, falling back to the node's own active gamemaster key **only when that argument is empty**. It is that fallback which fails for a banned gamemaster — `GetValidGM` returns `nullptr` (`deterministicgms.cpp:114-121`) and the manager sits in `GAMEMASTER_POSE_BANNED` (`activegamemaster.cpp:146-147`). **With argument 4 supplied the command works; the guide already says so in bold, and already explains that the second argument is `""` because revival does not require an address change** (`deterministicgms.cpp:693-700`, help text at `rpcevo.cpp:921` being more restrictive than the code). ★★ **Recorded rather than discarded because the report was reasonable and the near-miss was expensive:** the reported line numbers (`1105-1108`) did not exist in a 761-line file, and acting on the summary instead of reading the section would have replaced a more accurate passage with a less accurate one. **The guide was the authority here, not the report.** ★ The general form is worth keeping: *a bug report about documentation must be checked against the source the documentation cites, not against the report's summary of it.*

> **★★ BUG-059 (found 2026-09-03, FIXED) — `protx_register` WAS MISSING FROM THE CLI ARGUMENT-CONVERSION TABLE, SO NO GAMEMASTER COULD BE REGISTERED FROM `Hemis-cli` AT ALL.** Found when the first real operator ran the command the `/register` walkthrough composed and got `error code: -1 / JSON value is not an integer as expected`. ★★ **The mechanism:** `Hemis-cli` sends **every** argument as a JSON string; `src/rpc/client.cpp` holds a table of `(method, 0-based position)` pairs to convert back to native types before dispatch. `protx_register` had **no entry**, so `collateralIndex` arrived as `"0"` and `rpcevo.cpp:547`'s `get_int()` threw. ★★ **The control that isolated it in one step:** `protx_register_prepare` **was** listed (`client.cpp:130`) and shares the same handler and the same eleven arguments — given byte-identical arguments on the same node it sailed past `collateralIndex` and failed later on an address. Two methods, one table entry apart, opposite outcomes. Proven again at the wire: the same `curl` call with `0` succeeds past that point and with `"0"` reproduces the error exactly. ★ **`operatorReward` at position 8 was NOT affected and needed no entry** — it is parsed via `ParseFixedPoint(params[..].getValStr(), ...)` (`rpcevo.cpp:440`), and `getValStr()` returns the raw string either way. The fix is **one** entry, not two; assuming both would have been a plausible over-correction. ★★ **WHY NOBODY HIT IT IN 153 REGISTRATIONS: the fleet registered over Python RPC, which sends native JSON integers. The entire `protx_*` CLI surface was untested** — the same shape as a release workflow that had never run on a commit, and as `install.sh`'s `rpcbind` template outliving the code that justified it (BUG-055). **A path used only by machines is not exercised by machine use.** ★ **Second, latent instance found by the same reading:** `protx_revoke`'s `reason` (`rpcevo.cpp:1113`, index 2) is also absent. **Not reproducible end-to-end** — the gamemaster-hash lookup fails first — so it is fixed from source, and that limit is recorded rather than papered over. ★ **A third, in an unrelated subsystem, found by the gate leg rather than by a report:** `gmfinalbudget` reads a bool at index 2 (`budget.cpp:657`). ★★ **THE GATE LEG — `test/lint/check-rpc-convert.py`, wired into `lint-all.sh`:** for every registered RPC method it resolves the handler, follows one level into shared implementations (`protx_register` → `ProTxRegister`), collects every `params[N].get_int/get_bool/get_real` with a literal `N`, and asserts `client.cpp` lists it. **135 methods checked.** ★ **Both limbs were PROVEN to fire, not assumed:** deleting the new entry makes it name `protx_register` and exit 1; injecting a computed index makes it report NOT ANALYSABLE. ★★ **Writing the checker cost three rounds of false positives, and each one is the lesson:** following a mention of *another registered handler* attributed `getnetworkhashps`'s arguments to `getmininginfo`; bounding a function body at "the next `UniValue` definition" swallowed helpers with other return types, so `validateaddress` inherited whatever followed it; and brace-matching without skipping string literals over-ran on RPC help text full of `{`, giving one function a 32 KB body. **A linter that cries wolf is worse than no linter**, so each was chased to root rather than suppressed. ★ **Runtime-computed indices FAIL the check rather than warn** — `lint-all.sh` reads exit codes only, so a warning that exits 0 is invisible in CI, which is the very false-green shape the script exists to catch (ODC-098). ★ **The walkthrough was NOT taken down:** the command it composes is *correct* — the CLI is not — so the page now states the defect and composes the working raw-JSON-RPC equivalent beside it, with `<rpcuser>` placeholders it never fills in. **Removing it would have left operators with nothing and fixed nothing.** ★ Two documentation defects surfaced alongside: `rpcevo.cpp:1074` help says `protx_update_revoke` but the dispatch name is `protx_revoke`, and `:1000` says `protx update_registrar` with a **space** — both unrunnable as printed, neither fixed here.

> **★★ ODC-102 (raised 2026-09-03) — THE CHEAPEST TEST ENVIRONMENT CANNOT EXERCISE THE `protx_*` SURFACE AT ALL, AND THAT IS WHY BUG-059 REACHED AN OPERATOR.** Found while trying to reproduce BUG-059 in regtest before touching a live host. ★ **The measurement:** `chainparams.cpp` sets regtest's `UPGRADE_V6_0` to `NO_ACTIVATION_HEIGHT`, and `CheckEvoUpgradeEnforcement` (`rpcevo.cpp:177-183`) throws *"Evo upgrade is not active yet"* for every deterministic-gamemaster RPC. **No amount of block mining opens that gate** — it is not a height that has not arrived, it is a height that does not exist. ★★ **So every `protx_*` call in regtest fails at the same early guard, and none of them ever reaches its own argument parsing.** A regtest smoke test over the whole family would pass through zero lines of the code it appears to cover, which is the ODC-098 shape at environment scale: the check runs, returns, and touches nothing. ★ **The consequence is structural, not incidental.** The only environments that can exercise these methods are ptxtestnet hosts — machines with real chain state, a real wallet and a real cost of being wrong. **A defect in this family therefore cannot be caught by the cheap loop and is only caught by an operator**, which is exactly how BUG-059 surfaced. ★ **What was done instead, and what it cost:** reproduction moved to a launch host (`qm guest exec 1001` on px1), made safe by an all-`a` collateral txid that fails long before broadcast. That works but is slow, is unavailable in CI, and requires a running fleet. ★★ **The mitigation actually built is static, not dynamic** — `test/lint/check-rpc-convert.py` reads the source rather than running the RPC, so it covers all 135 methods in an environment that cannot execute any of them. **When the dynamic environment cannot reach the code, a static check over the same property is not a lesser substitute; it is the only one that scales.** ★ **What stays open:** whether regtest *should* activate V6_0. Turning it on would make the family testable in CI, but it changes a consensus parameter for a network used by other tests, so it is a deliberate decision and not a drive-by. **Trigger: the next defect in the `protx_*` family that the static check cannot see** — anything about values rather than types. ★ Cross-ref BUG-059, ODC-098, and the release workflow that had never run on a commit (ODC-092) — three instances of the same family: *a path exercised only by one kind of use is untested for every other kind.*

> **★★ KDD-109 (adopted 2026-09-03) — A TRADE-OFF SURVIVES ONLY WHILE ITS PRECONDITION CAN HOLD, AND NOBODY RE-CHECKS THE PRECONDITION.** Found while deciding whether a gamemaster should ship with `disablewallet=1`. ★ **The instance:** `install.sh` kept the wallet ON, and defended it with a genuine benefit — un-banning a gamemaster needs `protx_update_service`, and running it *on the gamemaster* means the BLS secret never travels. **Every clause of that argument was true**, and I verified all of them against source: `GetGM` not `GetValidGM` so a banned gamemaster IS accepted; revival fires on any `ProUpServTx`; the key must be passed as argument 4 because a banned node cannot supply its own. ★★ **The argument was nevertheless worthless, because it required the gamemaster to be able to pay a fee.** `protx_update_service` funds through `FundSpecialTx` (`rpcevo.cpp:282`), and the operating rule is that **gamemasters never hold funds**. Measured on a live zero-balance host: the same funding path fails `Insufficient funds.` **So the benefit being defended had never once been available.** The wallet was retained to preserve a capability that the fleet's own funding policy had already removed. ★ **Why this is not simply "a stale comment":** the comment was *accurate*, re-read carefully, and cited correctly — I had defended it against a proposal to delete it, and I was right to. **The defect was one level up: a true argument about a situation that cannot occur.** Checking the reasoning finds nothing wrong; only checking whether the *premise can ever be satisfied* does. ★★ **THE RULE: when a decision rests on preserving a capability, verify the capability is REACHABLE in the configuration you actually ship — not merely that the code implementing it works.** Both halves must hold, and they fail independently: the code can be correct while policy has made it unreachable, and nothing in the source says so. ★ **The cheap discriminator, worth reusing:** ask *"what would have to be true for this to be used even once?"* and then check that thing. Here it was one RPC call against a real zero-balance host, and it inverted a decision that two rounds of careful source-reading had left standing. ★ **Same family as ODC-081/KDD-104** — *a true reading of the wrong subject* — but the subject here is a **counterfactual**: the reading was of a path no host could take. Nearest cousin is ODC-102 (an environment that cannot reach the code) and BUG-059 (a path exercised only by machines): three variants of **coverage that looks complete because nobody asked what it could not reach**. ★ **Applied:** the gamemaster role now ships `disablewallet=1`; the comment was **sharpened rather than replaced**, keeping its correct account of why the secret must be passed explicitly and adding what the measurement showed. The guide gains a **new operator obligation at A4** — keep an off-host copy of the BLS secret — because recovery now provably runs from the wallet machine. **That obligation is stated where the key is generated, not in the recovery section an operator reads for the first time while banned.**

> **★ ODC-103 (raised 2026-09-03) — AN UNROUTABLE ADDRESS FAMILY COSTS A MEMBER SLOT FOR 15 SECONDS ON A SYSCALL THAT FAILED IN 3 MILLISECONDS.** Found while establishing whether mixed IPv4/IPv6 hosts can sign for each other. ★ **The measurement:** from px1 (zero global IPv6 addresses, no default v6 route) a TCP connect to a live IPv6 gamemaster endpoint returns `Network is unreachable` in **3 ms**. ★★ **Nothing converts that into a terminal state.** `PTX_ResolveMemberP2PAddr` (`ptx_sign_client.cpp:262-269`) returns the DGM address whenever `addr.IsValid()` — a syntactic check with no notion of routability — and `CConnman::OpenNetworkConnection` (`net.cpp:1934`) has **no `IsReachable` gate** on this path; the screening at `net.cpp:1820` and `:2187` belongs to automatic peer selection and `AddLocal`. So the caller re-opens a connection every tick, the member never leaves `UNSENT`, and it retires to `UNREACHABLE` only when `PTX_SIGNREQ_MEMBER_MS` (15 000, `ptx_sign_client.h:113`) expires. **The failure is instant and the consequence takes 15 seconds.** ★ **Why the node cannot help itself:** `SetReachable(net,false)` is driven **only** by `-onlynet` (`init.cpp:1363`), so an IPv4-only host still believes `NET_IPV6` is reachable and will keep dialling it. The information needed to fail fast exists in `errno` and is discarded. ★ **Consequence, bounded honestly:** this is an efficiency defect, not a correctness one — the round still reaches the right verdict, and `PTX_SignRound_StillWinnable` still aborts an unwinnable round early. What it costs is that an unwinnable round takes **15 s to become legible instead of milliseconds**, and a *winnable* round with one cross-family member spends a member slot for the full budget. ★ **Not fixed, and the reason is proportion:** the address-family policy (one family, network-wide) removes the condition entirely, so this is a defence against a configuration the guide will forbid. It matters only if mixed families are ever admitted. ★ **The cheap fix if it is ever wanted:** map `ENETUNREACH`/`EAFNOSUPPORT` from the connect attempt onto an immediate absorbing state — a fourth terminal outcome beside `UNREACHABLE`/`TOO_OLD`, which the `_COUNT` sentinel and default-less switch (KDD-108) would force to be classified rather than defaulted. ★ **Trigger: any decision to admit mixed-family hosts.** Cross-ref KDD-108 (the member budget this leans on), ODC-102 (regtest cannot exercise this either — it is a routing property, not a chain one).

> **★★ KDD-110 (adopted 2026-09-03) — ADDRESS FAMILY IS A NETWORK-WIDE INVARIANT, NOT A HOST CHOICE, BECAUSE TIER TWO IS POINT-TO-POINT WHILE EVERYTHING VISIBLE IS GOSSIP.** Adopted deciding whether IPv4-only operators can run gamemasters. ★★ **The asymmetry that makes this a rule rather than a preference:** blocks and transactions **gossip**, so a node on any family stays in sync, reports `Ready`, and looks healthy. But **signing is direct** — the caller connects to the registered address (`ptx_sign_client.cpp:262-269`, and there is no port to graft: the DGM address IS the address, host and port) — and **the DKG relay does not bridge families either**, because its inv is *born-restricted* to GMAUTH-verified peers that are members of the resolved session (`ptx_dkg_net.cpp:33-45`). **A dual-stack bystander therefore has nothing to forward.** The one thing everybody assumes will save them — "put a dual-stack node in the middle" — helps only the traffic that never needed help. ★ **The operator-facing sentence, which is the whole finding in one line:** *a node on the wrong family is not a degraded participant — for those peers it does not exist.* ★★ **AND THE PENALTY LANDS ON THE INNOCENT PARTY.** `nPoSePenalty` is written in exactly one place (`deterministicgms.cpp:298`), reached only from `HandleQuorumCommitment` on `!qc.validMembers[i]` (`:812-830`). The **signing** path writes no penalties at all — so unreachability for rolls costs nothing. **DKG participation does**, and a minority-family member that cannot exchange with the majority can be flagged and banned **for a topology it did not choose and cannot diagnose from its own host**: `self-check.sh` sections 4 and 5 both probe *from the node itself* and neither can see "reachable, but only by half the network". ★★ **POLICY ADOPTED: IPv6 REQUIRED on gamemasters; IPv4 additionally is fine and excludes nobody.** Not "one family, either family" — a specific one, because the requirement has to be checkable by an installer and stated to an operator before they provision. ★★ **THE CODE ARGUED AGAINST THE INTUITIVE ANSWER, and this is the transferable part.** "Dual-stack is the safe default" was wrong **mechanically**: `install.sh` auto-selected `externalip` only when the host had **exactly one global address**, so a dual-stack host — the best-provisioned kind — fell to the warn branch and shipped with `externalip` commented out, a manual step whose failure mode is a node that registers and never reaches `Ready`. **Selecting by address COUNT punished the hosts with more addresses.** Selecting by FAMILY handles single-stack and dual-stack identically and deletes the manual step. ★ **`scope global` IS NOT A ROUTABILITY TEST, measured not assumed:** on a host whose only global-scope IPv6 were `fd00:31::1` and `fd00:32::1` — both ULA, both unroutable — `ip -o -6 addr show scope global` returned **both**. Link-local is excluded by scope (`fe80::` is `scope=link`) and loopback by `scope=host`, but **ULA is `scope=global`** and must be filtered by prefix. A naive check would have selected an unroutable address, or aborted for "too many addresses" on a host that has none. ★★ **NOTHING BELOW THE INSTALLER CATCHES THIS, WHICH IS WHY THE INSTALLER HAS TO.** Measured against the released daemon: `-externalip` **accepts** a ULA (`fd00:32::1`) and even a link-local (`fe80::1`) without complaint — it starts, syncs and advertises them. The **only** IPv6 address it refuses is the RFC 3849 documentation range `2001:db8::/32` (`CNetAddr::IsRFC3849()`, `netaddress.cpp:329`, consulted by `IsValid()` at `:438`). **So the daemon has no opinion about routability**, consensus checks only `IsRoutable()` on the *registered* address, and an operator who advertises a ULA gets a node that looks perfect from every angle available to them. **The check has to happen where the address is CHOSEN, and this is why it cannot be pushed down into the daemon later.** A daemon-side check would have to reject an address the operator has already registered on chain — by then the only remedy is a new collateral and a new registration. The installer is the last moment at which the answer is still free. ★ Both roles therefore abort, with DIFFERENT reasons: a gamemaster because signing is point-to-point and it would be invisible to callers; a wallet host because the seed peers are IPv6 and this network has no peer discovery at all, so it would have nothing to dial. Same outcome, different cause — and an operator handed the wrong explanation looks in the wrong place, so the test asserts the role-specific sentence rather than merely that it aborted. ★ That measurement cost seven green checks first: the harness was given `2001:db8::1` precisely because it is documentation-only, the daemon refused it, and every test daemon shut down cleanly — seven failures whose cause was the test's own address, not the code under test. **Brackets are irrelevant; both forms behave identically. The prefix is the whole story.** ★★ **AND THIS IS NOT HYPOTHETICAL — THE FLEET HAS ALREADY HIT IT TWICE.** `doc/ptx/ptxbea-known-limitations.md:326` records *"exactly the failure the fleet has hit twice (the IPv6 incident: nodes healthy on-chain, unreachable for signing)"* — written about the **pre-KDD-085 RPC fan-out**, a mechanism since deleted. **The transport changed and the failure shape did not**, which is the argument for making it a network invariant enforced by the installer rather than a caution in a document: the same class survived a complete rewrite of the code that caused it. **Cross-ref** ODC-103 (the 15 s cost of a cross-family dial), ODC-104 (multi-GM), KDD-085 (why signing is direct at all), KDD-108 (a guarantee lost in a rewrite with no textual footprint).

> **★ ODC-104 (raised 2026-09-03) — ONE GAMEMASTER PER HOST IS NOW A TOOLING LIMIT, NOT A PROTOCOL ONE, AND THE ORIGINAL REASON HAS BEEN GONE SINCE KDD-085.** ★ **The original constraint was mechanical:** the signing fan-out dialled every member on **one shared RPC port** (`PTX_FanoutRpcPort()` took no per-member argument), so two gamemasters on one host could not both be reached — one would register, be selected, show `ENABLED`, and silently never sign. **KDD-085 deleted the fan-out.** ★★ **Both halves of the replacement permit co-hosting.** Signing arrives over P2P at each gamemaster's own registered `ipAndPort` — the sign client is explicit that *"the DGM address IS the address — host AND port"* — and consensus accepts any port on a non-mainnet network: `specialtx_validation.cpp:44-52` forbids only the **mainnet default** port off-mainnet, nothing else. So several gamemasters behind one address on distinct ports is **consensus-legal and signing-workable today**. ★ **What actually blocks it is tooling, and it is unglamorous:** `install.sh` writes one config per host and reserves ports once per host; its RPC-bind rationale still reasons *"with one GM per host there is no reason to bind anything but this host's own address"*; and each daemon needs its own datadir and RPC port, none of which the script parameterises per-instance. **Nothing in that list is hard; none of it is written.** ★ **Recorded because the motivation evaporated rather than being answered:** the question arose from a single NATted IPv4 address, where inbound forwarding is one host per port — and the IPv6 policy (KDD-110) makes that constraint moot, since a `/64` gives every gamemaster its own address. **The pressure to co-host is gone; the capability quietly arrived anyway**, and an operator who discovers it themselves would be running an untested configuration. ★ **Stays one-per-host for launch** — a supported-configuration boundary, which is what `OPERATOR_GUIDE.md` already tells operators. **Trigger: an operator constrained to one address who cannot get a prefix**, or any decision to support co-hosting deliberately; the work is `install.sh` instance parameterisation plus a fleet run, not protocol change.

> **★★ KDD-111 (adopted 2026-09-03) — A FAILURE THAT OUTLIVES THE MECHANISM IT WAS ATTRIBUTED TO WAS NEVER ABOUT THAT MECHANISM.** Extracted from KDD-110's prior art, and kept separate because the generalisation is bigger than the address-family rule that produced it. ★ **The instance:** `doc/ptx/ptxbea-known-limitations.md:326` records *"exactly the failure the fleet has hit twice (the IPv6 incident: nodes healthy on-chain, unreachable for signing)"*. That was written about the **RPC fan-out** — a gamemaster whose RPC was firewalled, bound to localhost, or on a non-standard port looked registered, enabled, synced and `Ready` while silently failing every signing request. **KDD-085 deleted that transport entirely.** Signing now arrives over P2P at the registered `ipAndPort`; there is no fan-out, no shared RPC port, no `-ptxfanoutport`. **And the failure came back unchanged**, with a different cause and identical symptoms. ★★ **The diagnosis that was recorded was true and was not the explanation.** Every clause of the old write-up was accurate about the fan-out; the remedies it prescribed (`rpcbind`, open 29995, verify from outside) were correct. **But they were remedies for an instance, and the register filed them as the lesson.** When the transport was replaced, the accumulated knowledge went with it, and the next occurrence had to be re-derived from scratch — this time as an address-family problem. ★ **The invariant that actually holds, and would have held across both:** *reachability at the registered address is not verifiable from the registered host, and nothing on that host reports its absence.* That sentence is true of the RPC fan-out, true of P2P signing, and will be true of whatever replaces it — because it is a property of **self-attestation**, not of any transport. ★★ **THE RULE: when a failure recurs after the mechanism blamed for it has been deleted, do not re-diagnose the new mechanism. Ask what the two instances share, and file THAT.** A recurrence across a rewrite is evidence that the register is holding an instance where it should be holding an invariant — and the register is the artefact that is supposed to survive rewrites. ★ **Concretely applied here:** the fix went into the **installer** rather than the transport (KDD-110), the check that would catch it went into `self-check.sh` as a **property of the host's own visibility** rather than a probe of any particular port, and the guide's sentence — *"a node on the wrong family is not a degraded participant; for those peers it does not exist"* — is stated in terms of reachability rather than of signing. **None of those three would need rewriting if the transport changed again.** ★ Nearest cousins: **KDD-108** (a guarantee lost in a rewrite with no textual footprint — there the property vanished silently; here it survived and the *knowledge* was what got lost) and **KDD-109** (a true argument about a situation that cannot occur — the sibling failure of filing something accurate at the wrong level). **Cross-ref** KDD-110, KDD-085, ODC-102.

> **★★ KDD-112 (adopted 2026-09-03) — A CHECK'S CONTEXT FILTER IS PART OF THE CHECK, AND IT NEEDS ITS OWN FALSIFICATION.** Found while RED-proving the guard written to stop stale facts drifting — i.e. in a check whose entire purpose was to prevent this class. ★ **The instance:** the ports-and-collateral leg scans *instructional* mentions only, because a naive scan of every number flagged coordinator float figures, the harness's own port fixtures and the deliberate *"1000 HMS is the wrong number"* warning. That scoping was correct and necessary — **a linter that cries wolf gets ignored** — and it was implemented as two context filters: port-shaped tokens, and HMS figures written within 40 characters of the word *collateral*. ★★ **The funding-formula check was then placed inside the collateral filter, and a funding formula is not written beside the word "collateral".** The one-pager's sits beside *"for N gamemasters"*. So a deliberately corrupted `(N × 1000)` **passed**: the assertion was correct, the value was wrong, and the line was never handed to it. **The check did not fail — it was never reached.** ★ **What found it:** RED-proving each limb separately rather than the leg as a whole. A single green run, or a single RED that happened to corrupt a line the filter did admit, would have licensed the whole leg. **Three limbs, three separate falsifications** — wrong multiplicand, wrong collateral prose, wrong port — and only the first was silent. ★★ **THE RULE: when a check is scoped, the scope is a second assertion and must be falsified independently. Ask not only "does it fire on a bad value?" but "does the line carrying a bad value reach it at all?"** Those are different questions and only the second finds a filter that excludes its own subject. ★ **Same shape as ODC-098 one level up:** there the check was on the instrument instead of the thing; here the check is on the right thing and the *selector* never delivers it. **A check that is never reached is indistinguishable from a check that passes** — which is the sentence ODC-098 already earned, now true of the filter rather than the assertion. ★ **Corollary that cost something this session:** the duplicate-fact map I built to decide *what* to guard was itself wrong — it reported the funding figure at four sites when there were six, because it grepped collateral mentions rather than the formula. **The map used to aim a guard is a document, and documents drift**; it is the same class as the pin-site checklist that BUG-054 came from, and it should be regenerated rather than trusted. **Cross-ref** ODC-098, BUG-054, KDD-109.

> **★★ BUG-060 (found 2026-09-03, SHIPPED then FIXED) — `v0.3.3-testnet` TOLD OPERATORS TO CLONE `v0.3.2-testnet`, AND THE GATE REPORTED OK BECAUSE THE FILE DID NOT EXIST TO IT YET.** ★ **The defect:** `git show v0.3.3-testnet:testnet/operator/OPERATOR_ONEPAGER.md:38` reads `git clone -b v0.3.2-testnet`. The one-pager — the document brought into the repository **specifically so tooling would see it** — was the single file in that release carrying a stale pin, and it is the line that decides which tag an operator installs. ★★ **The mechanism, and it is not carelessness:** `git ls-files` lists **tracked** files only. Both the bump (`git ls-files … | xargs sed -i`) and `pin-check.sh` (the same glob) therefore skipped it, because it was **untracked at the moment each ran**. It was created, pinned and committed in one commit, so at bump time and at gate time it was invisible to both; `git add -A` then tracked it, and the tag went out. **pin-check reported "OK across 7 files" — true, and meaningless, because 7 was the count precisely BECAUSE the new file was not counted.** ★★ **This is KDD-112's shape, walked into four commits after registering it:** *a check that is never reached is indistinguishable from a check that passes.* Worse than being wrong — the gate was **correct about a smaller set than the reader believed it covered**, and it silently narrowed its own scope. The claim "pin-check clean at 7 sites" was carried into a commit message and a status report as evidence, where the number was itself the symptom. ★ **Recut as `v0.3.4-testnet` rather than moving the tag:** `v0.3.3-testnet` was pushed with a published prerelease and downloadable assets, so its `SHA256SUMS` are checksums someone may already hold — and moving it would break exactly the verification operators are told to perform. **A bad tag is cheaper than a tag that changed under someone.** ★★ **THE FIX IS THE SCOPE CHECK, NOT THE PIN:** `pin-check.sh` now runs `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` over its own globs and **exits 2** if anything untracked sits inside them, naming each file — *"reporting OK now would report OK for a smaller set than you think"*. RED-proven against the exact defect: an untracked operator document with a stale pin. The previous script does not mention it and passes; the new one refuses. ★ **The generalisation worth keeping: a gate that derives its own scope can be silently starved of the thing it exists to check, and a passing count is not evidence the scope was complete.** Any check that enumerates its inputs should assert the enumeration, not just the contents. **Cross-ref** KDD-112, BUG-054 (the pin-site checklist that was itself incomplete), ODC-098.

> **★★ ODC-105 (raised 2026-09-03) — THE DOCUMENT SWEEP CHECKS DOCUMENTS AGAINST EACH OTHER AND AGAINST PINS, AND NEVER AGAINST THE INSTALLER'S ACTUAL BEHAVIOUR.** Found reading the operator corpus as a whole, which surfaced eight defects at once. ★ **Every one was the same class:** `GM_QUICKSTART.md` and `OPERATOR_ONEPAGER.md` describing behaviour `install.sh` no longer has — a fan-out that was deleted, a `@reboot` cron line for a unit the installer now writes *and enables*, an `externalip` the operator is told to set that the installer selects, a `PTX_GM_COUNT` default that is not the default. **The guide was swept repeatedly; the quickstart never was**, because nothing in the sweep was comparing prose to the installer. ★ **The guards that exist are all content-shaped:** `pin-check` compares a tag literal, the config-key leg compares option names, the fact leg compares ports and collateral figures. **All three ask "is this string right?" and none asks "is this instruction still necessary?"** A procedure can be composed entirely of correct strings and still tell an operator to do something the installer already did. ★★ **Two of the eight reports were themselves inverted, and the source decided both** — the "broken one-command bootstrap" was a working default (`install.sh:94` defaults `PTX_ROLE` rather than aborting, proven by install-test's own green legs running without it), and `PTX_GM_COUNT` defaults to **1**, not 3, in `vps-install.sh:64`. **A finding about stale documentation was itself half stale**, which is the reason to check reports against source rather than act on them. ★ **On guardability, and the honest split:** a general "commands a document tells you to run that the installer has superseded" check needs *intent*, not string matching, and would be the fragile kind. A **narrow** version is not fragile and has the config-key leg's exact shape: assert that no operator document names a mechanism, variable or command that no longer exists in `install.sh` or in `src/` — `fan-out`, `@reboot`, a `PTX_*` variable with a value the script does not default to. **That catches nouns, which is most of what went stale here.** Not built pending a decision; registered so it is a choice rather than an omission. **Cross-ref** BUG-054, KDD-112, ODC-098.

> **★ ODC-106 (raised 2026-09-03) — `install.sh` STILL RESERVES 1000 KERNEL PORTS FOR A MECHANISM THAT WAS DELETED, ON EVERY OPERATOR'S MACHINE.** ★ **The measurement:** `install.sh` section 4 writes `net.ipv4.ip_local_reserved_ports=32000-33000` to `/etc/sysctl.d/99-ptx-fleet-ports.conf`, and its own comment states the entire reason — *"PTX uses ports in 32000-33000 for its fan-out."* **The fan-out is gone:** `ptx_fanout.*` is deleted, every remaining `FanOutSign`/`PTX_FanoutRpcPort` reference in `src/` is a **comment about the deletion**, one is a test asserting it stays absent, and **nothing in the PTX source references the 32000-33000 range at all — zero hits.** ★ **It is live on the fleet now**, confirmed on two launch hosts: `net.ipv4.ip_local_reserved_ports=32000-33000`. So every operator who runs the documented install has 1000 ports removed from their ephemeral range for nothing, and the installer prints a scary `COULD NOT RESERVE PORTS` warning on unprivileged containers about a reservation that buys them nothing. ★★ **NOT REMOVED, and the reason is proportion rather than doubt:** it changes a kernel setting on machines belonging to other people, and deleting section 4 renumbers sections that `install-test.sh` cites in five places (`install.sh section 3`, `section 5`) — so it wants its own commit, not a line inside a documentation pass. ★ **The shape when it is done:** keep the section number, replace the action with a cleanup that removes the stale sysctl file, so a re-run repairs hosts that already carry it. **The documentation references were corrected immediately** (three sites in `GM_QUICKSTART.md`) because a document describing a deleted mechanism as live is a different and cheaper problem. ★ Same family as KDD-108 — *a rewrite leaves behind the scaffolding of what it replaced* — except here the scaffolding is on someone else's server. **Cross-ref** KDD-085, ODC-105.

> **★★ ODC-107 (raised 2026-09-03) — CONTENT MOVED OUT OF THE GENERATED CONFIG, WHICH WAS 83% COMMENT AND ARGUING FOR A REVERSED DECISION.** Measured on a live host: `Hemis.conf` is **159 lines — 132 comment, 6 blank, 21 actual settings.** ★★ **The two worst blocks were not merely long, they were WRONG in a way only a config comment can be.** (1) The wallet block opened *"THE WALLET IS LEFT ON DELIBERATELY"*, walked through a trade, and told the reader *"uncommenting this shrinks that to availability only"* — while `disablewallet=1` is now the shipped gamemaster default (KDD-109). **The longest comment in the file defended a decision that had been reversed.** (2) A 25-line block sized RPC capacity for *"the signing fan-out's concurrent dials"* — a mechanism KDD-085 deleted, which the comment itself conceded in a parenthetical. ★ **The rule adopted, and it is why this cannot be fixed by rewriting more carefully: NO CONFIG COMMENT MAY DESCRIBE A STATE THE OPERATOR IS BEING INVITED TO CHANGE.** A comment sitting beside a setting the reader is told to edit is stale the moment they edit it, and it ships once while the file lives on. The test each surviving comment had to pass is *"does this change what the operator does in the next thirty seconds?"* — which keeps the section-header warning (a failure the operator is about to cause, at the moment of causing it) and the `gamemaster=1`-needs-its-key pairing, and removes everything that justifies a default or records how it was measured. ★★ **THE MEASUREMENTS BELOW ARE PRESERVED HERE BECAUSE THEY EXIST NOWHERE ELSE — the register had neither.** ★ **RPC capacity, as it was reasoned:** stock `DEFAULT_HTTP_WORKQUEUE=16` / `DEFAULT_HTTP_THREADS=4` (`httpserver.h:13-14`, read at `httpserver.cpp:404` and `:435`) are sized for an operator typing one command at a time, not for a signing member asked in parallel with its whole quorum. **Roll routing keys on the TIP HASH, so every roll in a block resolves to the SAME quorum — measured 2026-08-23 over 178 blocks: 161 used exactly one quorum**, the remainder being tip-change races mid-batch. So N concurrent rolls in a block arrive as N concurrent requests at each of the 11 members: **fleet size does not divide that load, it concentrates it.** Past the queue depth the daemon returns HTTP 500 *"Work queue depth exceeded"* (`httpserver.cpp:271`) and then stops accepting, so the overflow is visible at the caller and invisible at the gamemaster. ★ **This analysis is now vestigial for signing** — KDD-085 moved signing to P2P and nothing dials a member's RPC — but it is the only written account of the tip-hash concentration property, which is a fact about ROLL ROUTING rather than about the transport, and survives the transport's deletion (KDD-111's shape). ★ **The ACL ordering, with the citations the config carried and BUG-055 does not:** the allow-list is checked **before** authentication (`httpserver.cpp:236`, HTTP **403** on reject) and the credential **after** it (`httprpc.cpp:157`, HTTP **401**). That ordering is why a remote probe of an exposed `rpcbind` answers 403 and never 401 — which is what makes such exposure *an unnecessary listener rather than an authentication problem*, the distinction BUG-055 rests on. ★ **And the reason widening the ACL is never the answer:** this daemon has **no per-method restriction** — `jreq.authUser` (`httprpc.cpp:157`) is never read and there is no `-rpcwhitelist` — so anything that authenticates gets the entire RPC surface, `stop` and the wallet included. **Cross-ref** KDD-085, KDD-109, KDD-111, BUG-055, ODC-105.

> **★★ ODC-108 (raised 2026-09-03) — `install.sh` HAS HOST-GLOBAL SIDE EFFECTS OUTSIDE `PTX_PREFIX`, AND ITS PARAMETERISED VARIABLE NAMES IMPLY A CONTAINMENT THAT DOES NOT EXIST.** ★ **The instance that made it visible:** running the installer three times on the build machine purely to inspect the config it generates left a **systemd unit installed and ENABLED**, running as **root** from a scratch directory, with `Restart=on-failure`. Killing the daemon achieved nothing — systemd restarted it within seconds — so it presented as a *mystery process that reappeared after being killed*, and cost three aborted test runs to attribute. ★ **The second instance, found the same day:** section 4 wrote `/etc/sysctl.d/99-ptx-fleet-ports.conf`, so **`install-test.sh`'s own green legs had been mutating the test machine's kernel configuration on every run** — invisible for as long as what it wrote was harmless (see ODC-106). ★★ **The shape: `PTX_PREFIX`, `PTX_DATADIR` and `PTX_PARAMS_DIR` all say "tell me where to put things", which reads as an installer you can point somewhere and inspect.** Two of its effects are **singletons on the machine** — `/etc/systemd/system/hemis-ptx.service` and `/etc/sysctl.d/` — and nothing in the variable names, the usage line or the completion banner says so. **"Generate a config so I can look at it" is not a read-only operation on this script, and it looks like one.** ★ **Why it matters beyond tidiness:** it is the reason `install-test.sh` needs its own host rather than a directory, the reason its port-collision guard exists at all, and a live hazard for any operator who runs the installer twice on one machine to compare outputs. ★ **Not fixed here.** The honest options are to namespace the unit by datadir, to add a `PTX_NO_SERVICE=1` inspection mode, or simply to say it in the usage line — and that is a decision about the installer's contract rather than a defect to patch mid-pass. **Recorded so the next person who meets a reappearing daemon spends a minute rather than an hour.** ★ Same family as ODC-105 — *a surface asserting a property nothing checks* — except here the assertion is made by the variable names rather than by prose. **Cross-ref** ODC-106, ODC-105, KDD-112.

> **★★ ODC-108 AMENDMENT (2026-09-04) — IT RECURRED BY A NEW ROUTE, AND THE ROUTE IS THE TEST HARNESS.** The original concern was a *manual* `install.sh` invocation leaving an enabled root unit behind. Since **KDD-109** the **wallet** role does `systemctl enable --now hemis-ptx`, and `install-test.sh`'s ROLE leg exercises that role — so **running the suite now enables a root systemd unit on the test host on every run**, pointed at a scratch datadir `cleanup()` then deletes. ★★ Observed on node1: the unit had restarted **240 times** against `/tmp/ptx-install-test.OH2stt/role-collide/.Hemis`, a directory that no longer existed, while holding 29994/29995 — which aborted the **next** run of the same suite with exit 3, **and the abort blamed the ports rather than naming the cause.** ★★★ **The harness made itself unrunnable, and it took building an unrelated guard to notice**, because nobody runs it twice in quick succession by hand. ★ Fixed in `cleanup()`, scoped so it can only remove a unit whose `ExecStart` names that run's own `$BASE` — a blanket `disable hemis-ptx` would have been unsafe on any host with a real node. ★ **The transferable half: a side effect that is correct for the thing under test can be wrong for the harness that exercises it.** Enabling the unit is right for a wallet operator and wrong for a suite that deletes the datadir a minute later, and nothing in the role logic can tell those apart — the harness has to clean up after the behaviour it is verifying.

> **★ W2.5 STATUS 2026-09-03 — REFERENCE ONLY, FLEET REDUCED TO 15 GM.** `W25B_FLEET_CONFIG.md` is kept as a portable recipe rather than a plan of record, and its topology is now **15 GM / 1 quorum / 1 caller** (was 98 / 8 / 4). ★★ **The consequence is stated rather than discovered later: `floor(15 / 11) = 1`, so L = 1 and the multi-quorum behaviour the drill was built for is not reachable at this size.** L>1 routing, concurrent-quorum load and the reform-headroom arithmetic need **N ≥ 22, and really N ≥ 33** for redundancy (ODC-094). At 15 the fleet is a single-quorum functional environment, which is what it is now for. ★ The L=1 caveat travels with it — ODC-094 records that at L=1 a retirement window is **total outage**, since no second quorum exists to carry it. Acceptable on a reference fleet; not on anything load-bearing. ★ **It also fits without argument:** 15 GMs at the 400 MiB cap is **6 GB** against ~21 GB available RAM and 18 GB free disk, so none of the capacity question above applies to it.

> **★★ KDD-113 (adopted 2026-09-04) — AN INSTRUCTION THAT STATES AN ATTITUDE WITHOUT A PROCEDURE IS NOT AN INSTRUCTION; AND TESTING THE EXAMPLE YOU WERE GIVEN IS HOW THE DEFECT SHIPS.** Found while building the operator FAQ bot, but neither half is about it, and the artefact is not in this repository. **(1) The attitude/procedure half.** Its refusal spec said *"be generous at the boundary"* and gave the reason in full — a wrong refusal turns away a real operator, an attempt that misses costs almost nothing. Measured against fifteen operator-phrased questions, **four that the corpus plainly covered were refused anyway**, and the tier that existed solely to route uncovered operational questions to the coordinator **never fired once in fifteen** — silently deleting the only mechanism by which documentation gaps got reported. The words were present, correct, and changed nothing: an attitude gives the reader no test to apply, so it applies its own default, which was to refuse whenever the phrasing did not match. Replaced with a three-step procedure — restate the problem in the corpus's vocabulary, ask whether any entry is plausibly about it, refuse only if nothing is — plus one categorical discriminator for choosing between the two refusal tiers. Same fifteen questions: **four wrong refusals → zero; zero relays → three**, with no regression on the tiers that were already right. ★ The rule generalises to any prose instruction carrying a disposition rather than a test — *"be careful with"*, *"prefer"*, *"be conservative about"*, *"use judgement"* — and the repair is always the same shape: name the steps, or name the discriminator. A reason is not a procedure, and stating the reason more emphatically does not make one. **(2) ★★ The named-example half, which is the more general finding.** The single question supplied as the acceptance case — *"why won't my node sync"* — was answered **correctly by the defective version**, because it happened to be phrased in the artefact's own vocabulary. Had the fifteen not included phrasings nobody suggested (*"it just sits there doing nothing"*, *"sent the coins but it wouldn't register"*), the defect would have shipped with its acceptance test green. **A supplied example is a floor, not a sample.** The cases that discriminate are the ones written in the words the *user* would reach for, not the words the artefact is written in — and for anything with a classifier or a matcher in it, those must be generated deliberately, because the obvious test set inherits the artefact's vocabulary by construction.

> **★★ KDD-114 (adopted 2026-09-04) — INHERITED UPSTREAM TOOLING THAT SUCCEEDS AT INSTALLING A DIFFERENT BLOCKCHAIN. TWO INSTANCES, SO IT IS A CLASS.** A fork inherits every file in the tree, and the dangerous ones are not the stale ones — they are the ones that still **work**, against the wrong chain, and report success while doing it. **Instance 1 (found 2026-09-02):** `main` carried the upstream `vps-install.sh`, 18 lines fetching `Hemis-Blockchain/Hemis/releases/latest`, unzipping into `/usr/local/bin` and writing a `daemon=1` config — it does not fail, it prints *"Hemis successfully configured."* while installing another chain's mainnet node. Fixed by pinning the quickstart's `raw.githubusercontent.com` URL to a **tag** rather than `main`, since a branch in that path is not a pin. **Instance 2 (found 2026-09-04, this entry):** `autoupdate.sh`, tracked at the repo root since the fork commit `d0af3da` and still present at `v0.3.4-testnet`. It polls the same upstream `releases/latest`, and on any change runs `Hemis-cli stop`, unzips **mainnet** `Hemis-Linux.zip` into `/usr/local/bin`, and starts a bare `Hemisd`. ★★ **On a PTX host `/usr/local/bin/Hemisd` is a SYMLINK into `/opt/hemis-ptx/bin`**, so the unzip lands on the live install. Found on ptx001/003/004 — not because anything installed it, but because `/opt/hemis-ptx` on those hosts is an unpacked copy of the repository tree, so shipping the file *is* deploying it. Never scheduled (no crontab, `cron.d` or timer on any host checked), so dormant rather than live; removed from the repo here and renamed to `.disabled-mainnet-updater` on the fleet. ★ **Swept for a third: there is none.** Of the four root-level scripts at the tag (`autogen.sh`, `autoupdate.sh`, `rename.sh`, `vps-install.sh`), `autoupdate.sh` is the only one anywhere in the tree that fetches and installs a binary from a non-`vileda-hemis` repository, and nothing references it but itself. ★★ **The rule: at a fork, audit for files that ACT rather than files that are stale.** A stale doc misleads a reader who can check it; a working installer pointed at the parent project's release feed is indistinguishable from correct until it has replaced your binaries. The discriminating question is not "is this out of date" but **"what does this do if someone runs it, and against whose chain."**

> **★★★ KDD-115 (adopted 2026-09-04) — A PREDICATE WHOSE DOMAIN EXCLUDES THE CASE IT WAS WRITTEN FOR. IT EXISTS, IT PASSES, AND IT CANNOT FAIL.** The sharpest instance yet, from the `/register` walkthrough: `if (v.ipport.indexOf(":") < 0) msgs.push("Include the port…")`. It asks whether the string contains **any** colon — and the page accepts **only** bracketed global IPv6, which is made of colons. So on every input the check was written to police, it was **vacuous**; it could fire only on something that was not an IPv6 address at all. A reviewer reads it and sees a port check. The suite is green because nothing exercises the case. ★ **The consequence was disproportionate to the typo:** `protx_register` accepts a bracketed address with no port and does not complain, the chain stores the default `:29993` which no host listens on, the gamemaster installs cleanly and syncs and reports `Ready`, and the failure surfaces only at arming — with a remedy (`protx_update_service` from the wallet host) that requires the **BLS secret to travel**, the one movement this architecture otherwise avoids. ★★ **A second instance landed the same day, in `self-check.sh`:** the gamemaster branch tested `[ "$_extip" -ge 1 ]` for `externalip=`, so **one line and two lines both passed** — and v0.3.4's one-pager was telling operators to *add* a second one. Same shape: the predicate's domain ("at least one") excludes the defect ("exactly one"). ★★ **The rule: for every check, name the input that must make it FAIL, and confirm that input is inside its domain.** "Has a colon" over IPv6 and "at least one" over "exactly one" are not weak checks, they are **non-checks wearing the costume of one** — and they are worse than no check, because their presence is read as coverage. ★ This is the falsification discipline applied one level down: KDD-112 requires a check's *context filter* to be falsified; this requires the *predicate itself* to have a reachable failing input. A RED leg that cannot be constructed is the symptom — if you cannot write the input that fails, the check does not test what its name says.

> **★★ ODC-109 (raised 2026-09-04) — `self-check.sh`'s OUTPUT IS LONGEST EXACTLY WHEN IT MATTERS MOST, AND DISCORD TRUNCATES IT.** Measured, not estimated: `[ok]` messages average **76 characters**, `[FAIL]` **175**, `[????]` **140** — because the failure messages were deliberately written to explain themselves and carry their own remedy. The arithmetic that follows is uncomfortable. A clean run (9 section headers + 15 `[ok]`) is **~1,545 chars** and fits Discord's 2,000-character message limit. A run with five failures is **~2,192**, and a badly broken node (8 `[FAIL]` + 3 `[????]`) is **~2,833**. ★★ **So the worse the node, the longer the output, the more certain it is cut off — the paste you most need complete is the one that never arrives complete.** An operator pasting a failing self-check into Discord is systematically pasting a partial one, and any reader (human or machine) that summarises it reports fewer failures than exist. **Wrong by omission, and silent.** ★ **The mechanism if this is ever acted on is already in the script:** the terminal `=== RESULT: N passed, M failed, K could-not-run, W warnings ===` line is a **completeness token** — its presence proves the paste is whole, its absence proves it is not. That is a deterministic check rather than a guess about truncation, and it is the right gate for any tool that parses this output. ★ Recorded now because it is a property of `self-check.sh` itself, not of any consumer: it is equally true of a person reading a pasted run. **Not fixed here** — shortening the failure messages would trade a real explanation for a length budget, which is the wrong trade; the honest options are attachment/file paste, a `--brief` mode, or accepting truncation and always checking for the RESULT line.

> **★★ ODC-105 BUILT 2026-09-04 — `doc-check.sh`, FOUR INVARIANTS, AND THE CROSS-DOCUMENT DIFF WAS REJECTED ON MEASUREMENT.** The guard raised as "the sweep never checks prose against the installer" is built, with a presence limb added. ★ **What it checks:** (1) `generateblskeypair` must be preceded by a daemon start — it is an RPC call; (2) any document describing a gamemaster setup must name `systemctl enable --now hemis-ptx`; (3) `externalip=` must never be presented as a line to *add*; (4) retired nouns (`@reboot`, `fan-?out`, `32000-33000`, `ptxfanoutport`, `ip_local_reserved_ports`) must not appear without a retirement marker. Five documents, one exemption list sharing `pin-check.sh`'s model — **exemptions are themselves checked, so an allowlist entry that stops matching is reported rather than silently becoming a hole.** ★★ **A CROSS-DOCUMENT DIFF WAS SCOPED AND REJECTED, and the measurement is the reason:** comparing the one-pager's gamemaster sequence against the guide's Part A produced **eight differences, six of them legitimate** — the one-pager covers registration end-to-end where the guide splits it onto the wallet host, and `Hemis-cli stop` + `Hemisd -daemon` versus `systemctl restart hemis-ptx` are the same operation by different mechanisms. `GM_QUICKSTART.md` is worse: **three commands, because it is a bootstrap path rather than a terser version of the same sequence**, so three-way agreement would demand documents match when they should not. Both real defects came from named invariants; the diff contributed nothing and cost six false positives. ★ Ordering is checked **whole-document** because delimiting sections needs a map the documents do not carry — a first attempt ran past Part A into Part B and picked up `sendtoaddress`, and a hand-maintained section map is a fourth thing to drift on a guard whose purpose is catching drift. **Weaker and maintainable beats precise and rotting.** ★★★ **FOUND FIVE REAL DEFECTS ON THE DAY IT WAS BUILT, all in the bootstrap path nobody had swept:** `GM_QUICKSTART.md` told operators *"`install.sh` has already started it under systemd, so you do not start it yourself"* — **false**, `install.sh:1373` leaves a gamemaster stopped and `vps-install.sh` says *"NOTHING HAS BEEN STARTED"* in its own banner, so the very next command could not connect; neither the quickstart nor `vps-install.sh` said to **enable** the unit, which is the failure already measured on four coordinator hosts; `vps-install.sh:26` still claimed to *"reserve the fan-out ports"* after ODC-106 deleted that; and `:150`/`:163` gave the **deleted fan-out** as the live reason co-hosting fails, in text an operator actually sees. ★ **What it does not catch, stated in the script's own header rather than only here:** a semantic reversal. The `disablewallet` case had the setting correct in two places and prose arguing the opposite in a third — every token present, every document internally consistent, the meaning contradicted. No structural check finds that; it took PeteX answering with four mutually contradictory sentences. ★ RED-proved per limb (`doc-check-red.sh`): five constructible failing inputs, five falsified, zero vacuous — the ordering limb against the **real** pre-`48cb8d4` one-pager rather than a synthetic fixture.

**Next-free: KDD-116 / ODC-110 / BUG-061.** *(KDD-089/090 are operating rules adopted during the px1 separation, 2026-08-19. KDD-086 and ODC-069/070 were assigned in the standup during the 2026-08-11..16 arc, and ODC-073/074 in doc/ptx/ptxbea-known-limitations.md §12; counters here jump past them so no ID is ever reused. BUG-057 was assigned in `install-test.sh` and BUG-058 in session, both outside this register; the counter skips them.)*
